# Green Party of Aotearoa New Zealand — every 2026 election policy

*Compiled 2026-10-23 for the 7 November 2026 general election. Part of a non-partisan archive; every item links to its source and, wherever it could be retrieved, to the full verbatim text in [`documents/`](documents/).*

## About this page

- **226 policy statements from [policy.nz](https://policy.nz)** (the independent, non-partisan guide), each with its *Why* and *How* summary exactly as policy.nz wrote it, its topics and its citations. These are the core of this page.
- **245 verbatim "Our plan" commitments** from the 38 chapters of the Green Party Manifesto 2026 (launched 26 July 2026), so that every manifesto commitment is listed even where policy.nz did not write it up. These are quoted exactly; they have no separate summary.
- **93 further entries** for party policy documents and 2026 campaign announcements, with a short neutral summary written for this archive (marked `summary_source: "archive"` in [`policies.json`](policies.json)).
- **185 full-text documents** in [`documents/`](documents/): 54 PDFs (manifesto, Māori manifesto, Easy Read manifesto, 40 standing policy papers, 2026 campaign policy papers, Green Budget 2025), 123 greens.org.nz pages and 8 press releases mirrored on Scoop.

**How the text was obtained.** greens.org.nz is behind Cloudflare and refuses automated access (HTTP 403), and the Internet Archive (web.archive.org) could not be reached from the research environment. So:
1. **PDFs** were downloaded directly from the party's file host `assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/…` (and two from `img.scoop.co.nz`) and converted with `pdftotext`. Reading order was checked; the default (non-layout) mode was cleaner for the multi-column brochures, and pages that contain tables are additionally reproduced in `-layout` form so that table rows stay intact. One image-only PDF (the KiwiPower explainer) was transcribed by hand and is marked as such. Local copies are in `raw/party-docs/green/pdf/`.
2. **greens.org.nz web pages** (manifesto chapters, policy summary pages, press releases) were recovered from **Common Crawl** WARC captures (crawls CC-MAIN-2026-30, -34, -39 and -43, i.e. July to 18 October 2026); each document records its capture date.
3. **Press releases not captured by Common Crawl** were taken from the copies the party posted on **scoop.co.nz**.
4. Nothing was paraphrased inside a full-text document. Where a source could not be retrieved the document says so.

**Not retrievable:**
- `greens.org.nz/disability_policy_full`. The Disability Policy PDF it points to now returns HTTP 403, so it appears to have been withdrawn. The disability policy summary page and the manifesto disability chapter are included. See [note](documents/unretrievable-disability-policy-full.md).
- `greens.org.nz/greens_announce_jobs_guarantee_for_young_people` (about 11 Oct 2026). The policy paper it announced is included in full. See [note](documents/unretrievable-greens-announce-jobs-guarantee-for-young-people.md).
- The **fiscal plan released on 23 October 2026** (the day this page was compiled). It is not yet in any crawl or mirror reachable from here. The section below summarises press coverage of it.
- policy.nz also cites two pages from other parties for Green policies (POL54980, POL55038). They are flagged on those entries.

## Headline commitments

policy.nz marks these as the Greens' **key policies**:

- **Create a 2.5 percent tax on assets worth over $10m** (Economy). [POL54655](#pol54655)
- **Implement a tax on wealth received over $1m** (Economy). [POL54729](#pol54729)
- **Increase the corporate tax rate to 33 percent for big corporations only** (Economy). [POL54730](#pol54730)
- **Make the first $10,000 that people earn tax-free** (Incomes and employment). [POL54735](#pol54735)
- **Create a new tax bracket for income earned over $160,000** (Incomes and employment). [POL54737](#pol54737)
- **Establish a government backed clean energy loan scheme** (Environment). [POL54744](#pol54744)
- **Mandate the installation of solar on new or renovated public homes, where appropriate** (Housing). [POL54764](#pol54764)
- **Create a job guarantee programme for jobs focused on ecosystem regeneration** (Incomes and employment). [POL54787](#pol54787)
- **Provide stable funding for creative organisations and events** (Media, culture and recreation). [POL54954](#pol54954)
- **Support the repatriation of taonga overseas back to the Māori community** (Media, culture and recreation). [POL54956](#pol54956)
- **Establish infrastructure across ministries to strengthen our creative and cultural economy** (Media, culture and recreation). [POL54957](#pol54957)
- **Provide $200m towards te reo Māori initiatives** (Te ao Māori). [POL54979](#pol54979)
- **Expand voting rights in New Zealand** (Law, justice and government). [POL54981](#pol54981)
- **Entrench the Māori electoral seats** (Law, justice and government). [POL54982](#pol54982)
- **Strengthen political lobbying rules** (Law, justice and government). [POL54984](#pol54984)
- **Remove barriers to accessing essential services for disabled people** (Health). [POL55004](#pol55004)
- **Ensure rainbow communities can access gender-affirming healthcare** (Health). [POL55010](#pol55010)
- **Restore the monitoring role of the Children's Commissioner** (Community and inclusion). [POL55019](#pol55019)
- **Allow people with Māori whakapapa to become New Zealand citizens no matter where they are born** (Te ao Māori). [POL55024](#pol55024)
- **Make it easier for people based in other countries to repay student loans** (Education). [POL55027](#pol55027)
- **Pursue reciprocal healthcare, welfare and visa-waiver arrangements with other countries** (Migration). [POL55028](#pol55028)
- **Introduce free public transport fares for young people, the disabled and CSC card holders** (Transport and infrastructure). [POL55029](#pol55029)
- **Build mass rapid transit in three major cities and invest in better rail and bus links** (Transport and infrastructure). [POL55030](#pol55030)
- **Break up the supermarket duopoly and use part of it to create a government-owned supermarket** (Economy). [POL55032](#pol55032)
- **Increase climate aid funding to the Pacific** (Defence and foreign affairs). [POL55034](#pol55034)
- **Introduce fees-free tertiary education, universal student allowances and paid placements** (Education). [POL55035](#pol55035)
- **Increase visa-free travel and work visa set-ups for Pasifika visitors and workers in NZ** (Defence and foreign affairs). [POL55036](#pol55036)
- **Create a state-owned retirement village system and more support for seniors** (Community and inclusion). [POL55042](#pol55042)
- **Create an independently enforced Rental Warrant of Fitness** (Housing). [POL55079](#pol55079)
- **Limit annual rent increases to 2 percent** (Housing). [POL55082](#pol55082)
- **Increase funding for food grants, food banks and other food security programmes** (Health). [POL55091](#pol55091)
- **Make early childhood education free** (Education). [POL55093](#pol55093)
- **Repeal the Fast-track Approvals Act** (Environment). [POL55095](#pol55095)
- **Amend the definition of consent to be free and voluntary** (Community and inclusion). [POL55108](#pol55108)
- **Support sovereignty and independence in the Asia-Pacific** (Defence and foreign affairs). [POL55115](#pol55115)
- **Increase the refugee quota** (Migration). [POL55127](#pol55127)

The party's campaign is built around three priorities: "affordable living, healthy nature and real democracy". It is to be paid for by taxing "the super-rich and mega-corporations" while cutting income tax "for 96 percent of people" ([manifesto introduction](documents/web-manifesto-2026.md)). The main policy packages released during 2026 were:

- **Tax policy 'A tax system for all of us': wealth tax, capital acquisitions tax, higher tax on big corporates, bank levy, and income tax cuts for 96% of earners**: The 12-page tax plan (announced 21 June 2026). Revenue measures: a 2.5% tax on the net assets of the super-rich above $10 million (family homes exempt); a capital acquisitions tax on gifts and inheritances over $1 million (family farms and homes exempt); a 33% company tax rate for the largest 0.7% of companies while small and medium firms stay at 28%; a 0.06% levy on the liabilities of the four big banks; enforcing a 5% withholding tax on big tech profits sent offshore; and reversing the coalition's landlord interest-deductibility and bright-line changes. Income tax: a $10,000 tax-free threshold, new stepped brackets and a 45% rate above $160,000. The party's own Parliamentary-Library-based modelling puts the net gain at about $5.1-5.7 billion a year (2027/28-2030/31). [full text](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md)
- **'Drink Swim Fish' freshwater and oceans policy**: A 4-page water policy (announced 6 July 2026) aimed at safe drinking water, swimmable rivers and healthier oceans: science-based national freshwater limits including lower nitrate limits, stronger wastewater-discharge rules, phasing out synthetic nitrogen fertiliser, phasing out bottom trawling and other destructive fishing, protecting at least 30% of oceans, and reviewing the fisheries quota system. [full text](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md)
- **'Power for all of us' energy policy: Kiwipower, Warmer Kiwi Homes expansion, community energy and zero-interest solar loans**: The 12-page energy policy (announced 20 July 2026). It proposes 'Kiwipower', a publicly owned company to contract and build electricity firming; an expanded Warmer Kiwi Homes insulation/heating programme; $200m for community-owned energy; a government-backed Clean Energy Loans Scheme (modelled as zero-interest, tied to the property) for rooftop solar and other home energy upgrades; and other electricity-market changes. It includes four-year cost tables and says spending would be funded by the proposed super-rich tax. [full text](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md)
- **Te Waonui a Tāne: an ongoing, protected funding source for marae**: An 11-page policy (announced 9 August 2026) to recognise and resource marae as social and cultural infrastructure. It proposes a Rangatiratanga Trust with a $2.44 billion Crown funding input, including a $2 billion intergenerational fund (Te Tōtara Haemata) and annual restoration funding for marae, governed with regional marae representation. [full text](documents/te-waonui-a-tane-policy-2026.md)
- **'Affordable food for all of us': KiwiMart public supermarket chain, stronger grocery regulation and a Fair Food Fund**: A 15-page food-system policy (announced 6 September 2026). It proposes a publicly owned supermarket chain 'KiwiMart' (estimated acquisition and capitalisation cost about $2.8bn, citing an estimate of $1.3bn to buy 120 stores and two distribution centres), more Commerce Commission funding and higher penalties for grocery-sector breaches, a $150m Fair Food Fund for community food security, and more funding for regional food providers. [full text](documents/affordable-food-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md)
- **'Safe, sovereign AI': regulate artificial intelligence for the public good**: An 8-page AI plan (announced 12 October 2026). It proposes rules so that AI does not replace human or democratic decision-making (no fully automated decisions with substantive effects in public services), protects data sovereignty, privacy and creators' work, operates within environmental limits (including controls on data centres), shares benefits rather than concentrating them, and is transparent and accountable. [full text](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md)
- **Youth Jobs Guarantee: a job or training pathway for every 16-24-year-old out of work for six months**: A 7-page jobs policy (published October 2026) guaranteeing every 16-to-24-year-old who has been out of work for six months a pathway into a locally based job or training, delivered with councils and building on the Mayors Taskforce for Jobs. The document's indicative costings rise from $604m in the first year to $1,631m in the fourth. [full text](documents/youth-jobs-guarantee-policy-2026.md)
- **Paid placements for trainee nurses, teachers and social workers; abolish the training wage for apprentices**: A 4-page workforce policy (16-17 October 2026): a universal weekly payment for students during compulsory practical placements (nursing, teaching, social work and similar), relocation support, abolition of the training-wage exemption so apprentices get at least the minimum wage, a grant for apprentices' tools, and more pastoral support. Indicative costs are about $75-80m and $117m a year for the two parts. [full text](documents/paid-placements-apprenticeships-policy-2026.md)
- **Connect: Aotearoa transport plan**: 4 Oct 2026: transport plan with free public transport for children, students, Community Service Card holders and Total Mobility users, a $2 fare cap for everyone else, bus lanes in the three main cities and mass rapid transit. [full text](documents/web-green-party-launches-the-transport-plan-to-combat-fossil-fuel-crisis.md), [policy page](documents/web-connect-aotearoa.md)

## Costed plan and fiscal position

**Tax plan revenue.** The table below gives the party's own estimates from *A tax system for all of us* (page 10). Figures are $ million per year; negative figures are revenue forgone. The document says the estimates come from Parliamentary Library models, with an allowance for avoidance and evasion of the super-rich tax.

| Policy | 2027/28 | 2028/29 | 2029/30 | 2030/31 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Super-rich tax | 3,762 | 3,883 | 4,008 | 4,137 |
| Capital Acquisitions Tax | 953 | 1,001 | 1,051 | 1,103 |
| Big corporations tax | 1,370 | 1,436 | 1,502 | 1,570 |
| Major banks levy | 373 | 388 | 404 | 420 |
| Big tech tax | 204 | 214 | 223 | 233 |
| Reversing bright-line test changes | 45 | 45 | 45 | 45 |
| Reversing landlord tax cuts | 876 | 922 | 970 | 1,003 |
| Tax setting changes (income tax cuts) | -2,335 | -2,439 | -2,557 | -2,680 |
| Funding for Inland Revenue | -100 | -102 | -104 | -106 |
| **Total** | **5,147** | **5,348** | **5,541** | **5,725** |

Source: [tax policy, full text](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) (the table is reproduced in layout form on page 10).

**Fiscal plan (23 October 2026).** The party released its overall fiscal plan at Parliament on 23 October 2026. The document itself could not be retrieved here. According to [RNZ's report](https://www.rnz.co.nz/news/politics_election-2026/2097318/green-party-releases-fiscal-plan-promises-to-increase-health-spending-by-3-billion-each-year) (Russell Palmer, 23 Oct 2026), the plan:
- adds $3 billion to health funding in each of the next four Budgets, about $30 billion over the period;
- relies on "nearly $29 billion in extra revenue over four years" from the wealth tax, the tax on large gifts and inheritances, higher company tax for big businesses, a bank levy and landlord tax changes, alongside income tax cuts for most workers;
- raises future operating allowances to $4.5b–$4.7b a year, compared with about $2.4b under the coalition's plans;
- returns to surplus in 2029/30, a year later than Treasury forecasts, and leaves debt $26.6 billion higher than Treasury expects by 2030/31;
- commits future Budget funding to buying overseas carbon credits. Treasury estimated that bill could reach $5 billion; the Greens say more domestic cuts would reduce it.
Infometrics reviewed the plan's direct fiscal effects. Swarbrick said every election policy had been costed, and that the party still wants a Parliamentary Budget Office. National (Simeon Brown) and ACT (David Seymour) criticised the plan; their reactions are in the same RNZ report. Other outlets' coverage gave a debt figure of 44% of GDP by 2030/31 against Treasury's 39.5%. That figure has not been verified against the party document.

**Costings in individual policy papers.** Most 2026 policy papers end with four-year cost tables (2027/28–2030/31). These include [Power for all of us](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md), [KiwiPower](documents/kiwipower-explainer-2026.md), [Te Waonui a Tāne](documents/te-waonui-a-tane-policy-2026.md), [Affordable food](documents/affordable-food-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md), [Youth Jobs Guarantee](documents/youth-jobs-guarantee-policy-2026.md) and [Paid placements & apprenticeships](documents/paid-placements-apprenticeships-policy-2026.md). Release-level costings include pay equity for care workers (about $1.1bn a year), cancelling public-service cuts ($3.17bn over four years) and te reo Māori ($200m). The party's 2025 alternative budget, [Green Budget 2025](documents/green-budget-2025.md), includes a full fiscal summary.

## Economy

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54655"></a>
**Create a 2.5 percent tax on assets worth over $10m** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54655` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, tax-and-income-support, key-policies-economy, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says that the richest 311 households in NZ pay an effective tax rate of 9 percent, half that of most working people. This is because much of this wealth sits in asset portfolios, rather than being taxed as income. The wealthy need to contribute to solving shared problems with the rest of New Zealand. With the 150 richest people in NZ holding as much as 917,000 households, it is important to ensure the wealthy contribute through taxes.
- **How:** This party would implement a 2.5 percent annual tax on the net assets of people holding over $10m in wealth. This would affect 0.3 percent of New Zealanders.
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525)); ['Income', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-income.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_income))

<a id="pol54729"></a>
**Implement a tax on wealth received over $1m** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54729` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, tax-and-income-support, key-policies-economy, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says that more than $1.6 trillion in wealth is passed on intergenerationally. The party believes that being rich shouldn't be determined by who your parents are.
- **How:** This party would introduce a Capital Acquisitions Tax on assets and gifts received worth more than $1m. Family farms and family homes would be exempt.
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525)); ['Policy Announcement - A tax system for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-tax-system-for-all.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/tax_system_for_all))

<a id="pol54730"></a>
**Increase the corporate tax rate to 33 percent for big corporations only** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54730` · topics: economy, incomes-and-employment, business-support, government-revenue-and-finance, key-policies-economy, tax-and-income-support

- **Why:** This party says that large corporations in NZ are profiting from the public without contributing back towards society. It says that cuts to the corporate tax rate have not increased wellbeing, growth or investment for most New Zealanders; rather, corporations see more profits.
- **How:** This party would return the corporate tax rate to 33 percent for the 0.7 percent biggest corporations, such as the supermarket duopoly, energy companies, and banks. It would keep the corporate tax rate at  28 percent for small and medium enterprises. It would also introduce a Bank Levy of 0.06 percent on the liabilities of the four main banks. It would also enforce the 5 percent tax rate on service and license fees for large tech companies.
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525)); ['Policy Announcement - A tax system for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-tax-system-for-all.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/tax_system_for_all))

<a id="pol54742"></a>
**Phase out certain fishing practices**  
`POL54742` · topics: economy, environment, fisheries, oceans, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that bottom trawling, set netting, dredging and seamounts are destructive fishing practices, destroying native ecosystems and catching endangered species such as the Māui and Hector's dolphins. Banning these fishing practices would protect NZ's ocean ecosystems for generations to come.
- **How:** This party would phase out bottom trawling, set netting and dredging, starting with an immediate ban on seamounts. The focus on banning these fishing practices would begin in ecologically sensitive areas such as Tīkapa Moana/Hauraki Gulf and the habitats of Māui and Hector's dolphins.
- **Full text:** ['Oceans and Fisheries', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-oceans-and-fisheries.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_oceans_and_fisheries)); ['Drink Swim Fish Fixing the rules for fresh water and oceans' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74309/attachments/original/1783476578/DSF_Policy_2026.pdf?1783476578)); ['Policy Announcement - Drink, Swim, Fish — Fixing the rules for water', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-drink-swim-fish.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/drink_swim_fish))

<a id="pol54743"></a>
**Review the quota system for fisheries**  
`POL54743` · topics: economy, environment, fisheries, oceans

- **Why:** This party says that the current fisheries rules allow for overfishing, depleting NZ's oceans. It wants to replenish our fish stocks with an improved management system which is founded on ecosystem protection.
- **How:** This party would review the Quota Management System and move towards ecosystem-based fisheries management that prioritises abundance and long-term sustainability.
- **Full text:** ['Oceans and Fisheries', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-oceans-and-fisheries.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_oceans_and_fisheries)); ['Drink Swim Fish Fixing the rules for fresh water and oceans' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74309/attachments/original/1783476578/DSF_Policy_2026.pdf?1783476578)); ['Policy Announcement - Drink, Swim, Fish — Fixing the rules for water', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-drink-swim-fish.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/drink_swim_fish))

<a id="pol54902"></a>
**Implement Waitangi Tribunal recommendations to ensure governance of the agricultural sector upholds Te Tiriti o Waitangi.**  
`POL54902` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, agriculture, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that governance of the agricultural sector should recognise the expertise and rights of Māori and have self-governance over mahinga kai (food production). The agriculture sector and regional councils should work in partnership with Māori, in line with Te Tiriti o Waitangi.
- **How:** This party would implement the recommendations of the Waitangi Tribunal to ensure that governance of the agricultural sector upholds Te Tiriti o Waitangi.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54903"></a>
**Support Māori to address barriers to traditional food production**  
`POL54903` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, law-justice-and-government, agriculture, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, land-and-resources, local-government

- **Why:** This party says governance of the agricultural sector should recognise the expertise and rights of Māori and have self-governance over mahinga kai (traditional food gathering practices). The agriculture sector and regional councils should work in partnership with Māori, in line with Te Tiriti o Waitangi.
- **How:** This party would support Māori to address barriers found in banking, tax, company and co-operative laws that prevent te ao Māori principles from being applied to the production of food and fibre and/or Māori land use and development.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Food Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/food-policy-2024-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64668/attachments/original/1779663153/Policy-Greens_Food_Policy_2024-2026.pdf?1779663153)); ['Food, Fibre and Forestry', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-food-fibre-and-forestry.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_food_fibre_and_forestry))

<a id="pol54904"></a>
**Establish an Agricultural Standards Framework**  
`POL54904` · topics: economy, agriculture

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand needs strong agricultural standards to ensure it remains known for its premium-quality and environmentally sustainable products.
- **How:** This party would establish an Agricultural Standards Framework that includes requirements to measure all agricultural inputs and outputs, and robust and consistent measures of environmental impacts caused by agriculture. The framework would also include sustainability standards for all food and fibre sectors and incentives and other support to farmers to continuously improve ecosystems on productive land.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54905"></a>
**Prohibit palm kernel expeller (PKE) imports**  
`POL54905` · topics: economy, agriculture

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand needs strong agricultural standards to ensure it remains known for its environmentally sustainable products. NZ should not import products known for their link to tropical deforestation and habitat loss.
- **How:** This party would prohibit palm kernel expeller (PKE) imports. This is a solid by-product left over from extracting oil from the seeds of oil palm fruit.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54906"></a>
**Impose nitrogen caps on all agricultural practices**  
`POL54906` · topics: economy, environment, agriculture, fresh-water-and-pollution

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealanders should be able to swim in unpolluted rivers and lakes. However, nitrogen fertilisers used on farms are polluting our freshwater. Fossil fuels are used in the production of nitrogen fertiliser, contributing to the climate emergency.
- **How:** This party would impose nitrogen caps on all agricultural practices, and it would also prohibit the production of nitrogenous fertilisers from fossil fuels.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54907"></a>
**Phase out most grazing concessions on public conservation land**  
`POL54907` · topics: economy, environment, agriculture, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that that New Zealand should be working to look after its environment so that its communities and biodiversity are sustained. Allowing animals to graze on conservation land is prioritising economic opportunities over the core purpose of conservation land: preserving and protecting our native ecosystems.
- **How:** This party would phase out most grazing concessions on public conservation land and require sustainable farm plans for active concessions.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54908"></a>
**Support farmers in sensitive ecosystems to work collaboratively to protect indigenous biodiversity**  
`POL54908` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, environment, agriculture, te-reo-maori-and-culture, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that NZ should be working to look after its environment so that its communities and indigenous biodiversity are sustained.
- **How:** This party would support farmers in sensitive ecosystems to work collaboratively to protect indigenous biodiversity.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54910"></a>
**Create a resilient agricultural sector**  
`POL54910` · topics: economy, environment, agriculture, conservation, business-support

- **Why:** This party says that to protect New Zealand's food and agricultural products for generations to come, the agricultural sector needs to become resilient, diversified and agile. It will continue to face changing weather patterns, markets, and consumer demands.
- **How:** This party would require imported foods to meet our own national growing and production requirements and support a transition to agroecological practices (such as regenerative and organic approaches). It would also encourage preserving and growing heritage seeds for personal use and commercial production and strengthen the collective resilience of grassroots community collaborations like local catchment groups.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54911"></a>
**Provide resources and funding for innovative agricultural projects**  
`POL54911` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, agriculture, economic-development-and-industry, business-support, te-reo-maori-and-culture

- **Why:** This party says that communities benefit from local and seasonal produce that is nutritious, safe, fresh, and affordable. We should be supporting innovative agricultural practices and systems that encourage a thriving sector.
- **How:** This party would provide startup funding for innovative systems and/or new food and fibre trials, and fund ongoing research to explore sustainable agricultural livelihoods that require less energy, time and costs. It would also provide resources for projects addressing the food and fibre needs of local communities.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54912"></a>
**Honour traditional Māori agricultural practices**  
`POL54912` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, agriculture, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that in meeting New Zealand's commitment to Te Tiriti o Waitangi, mātauranga Māori must be recognised, and Māori communities should be supported to grow, forage and hunt for food on their land.
- **How:** This party would prioritise iwi-based initiatives in the management and restoration of retired and/or degraded agricultural land. It would fund research and development that supports hapū and iwi, Māori agribusiness and resources. A Māori-led authority would be set up to oversee the distribution of start-up funding for locally-led and sustainable Māori agricultural initiatives.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Food Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/food-policy-2024-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64668/attachments/original/1779663153/Policy-Greens_Food_Policy_2024-2026.pdf?1779663153)); ['2026 Māori Manifesto' (PDF), undated (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/maori-manifesto-2026-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1785798775/Ma%CC%84ori_Manifesto_Web_Version.pdf?1785798775))

<a id="pol54935"></a>
**Support Māori led education for growing and preparing food**  
`POL54935` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, agriculture, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that food is deeply connected to wellbeing, culture, identity, and the health of the planet. By restoring Māori-led kaitiakitanga of mahinga kai and prioritising nutritious, sustainable food systems, we can strengthen relationships with the environment and support healthier communities.
- **How:** This party would support learning opportunities for children and communities to grow, harvest, prepare, and share nutritious wholefoods through school gardens and food education programmes. It would strengthen Māori-led approaches to caring for mahinga kai and ensure traditional knowledge and practices are valued and supported. A national whole food system strategy will guide decision-making by honouring Te Tiriti o Waitangi and prioritising the health of people and the planet alongside sustainable practices.
- **Full text:** ['Food, Fibre and Forestry', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-food-fibre-and-forestry.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_food_fibre_and_forestry)); ['Food Policy', undated (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/web-food-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/food_policy)); ['Food Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/food-policy-2024-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64668/attachments/original/1779663153/Policy-Greens_Food_Policy_2024-2026.pdf?1779663153))

<a id="pol54986"></a>
**Regulate social media platforms to address harmful content**  
`POL54986` · topics: economy, community-and-inclusion, education, health, internet-and-digital-technologies, young-people, schools, mental-health

- **Why:** This party says that digital environments need to support our wellbeing, especially for young social media users who are often more vulnerable in the digital space.
- **How:** This party would introduce a Digital Duty of Care to address harmful content and behaviour on social media platforms. This would require algorithmic transparency and accessible and responsive complaints systems, as well as strengthening enforcement with an independent national regulator.
- **Full text:** ['Digital Technology', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_digital_technology))

<a id="pol54987"></a>
**Regulate artificial intelligence to limit the harm from scams and deepfakes**  
`POL54987` · topics: economy, internet-and-digital-technologies, artificial-intelligence-ai

- **Why:** This party says that digitial platforms should be held accountable for protecting the public and its information. This party believes a harm-reduction approach should be taken to digital technology.
- **How:** This party would regulate artificial intelligence to ensure its use is safe, transparent and accurate. It would also create stricter rules around "deepfakes" and scam material.
- **Full text:** ['Digital Technology', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_digital_technology))

<a id="pol54989"></a>
**Protect Māori data sovereignty**  
`POL54989` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, internet-and-digital-technologies, artificial-intelligence-ai, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that without the regulation of AI models, Māori culture is being exploited and stolen by overseas platforms.
- **How:** This party would adopt and implement the Māori Data Governance Model and Māori AI Governance Framework from Te Kāhui Raraunga across the public sector to protect Māori data sovereignty.
- **Full text:** ['Digital Technology', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_digital_technology))

<a id="pol54991"></a>
**Strengthen obligations for data-holders to protect user data**  
`POL54991` · topics: economy, health, internet-and-digital-technologies, health-services

- **Why:** This party says that that the companies that are holding public data, including health biometrics and personal information, cannot always be trusted to protect it.
- **How:** This party would strengthen obligations on data-holders to protect user and biometric data by increasing penalties for privacy breaches, and strengthen the Privacy Commissioner's enforcement powers to protect personal information better.
- **Full text:** ['Digital Technology', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_digital_technology))

<a id="pol54992"></a>
**Expand media and digital literacy education**  
`POL54992` · topics: economy, community-and-inclusion, education, media-culture-and-recreation, internet-and-digital-technologies, young-people, artificial-intelligence-ai, schools, broadcasting-and-media, trades-and-vocational-education, seniors

- **Why:** This party says that digital environments should support our wellbeing, yet many young people have been exposed to harmful and toxic content. We should equip our young people with adequate tools so they can use digital technologies without compromising their privacy and online safety and keep up with the rapid development of technology.
- **How:** This party would expand media and digital literacy education to equip New Zealanders to navigate AI-generated media and other emerging digital technologies.
- **Full text:** ['Digital Technology', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_digital_technology))

<a id="pol54994"></a>
**Reduce barriers to digital inclusion**  
`POL54994` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, internet-and-digital-technologies, wellbeing-and-services

- **Why:** This party says that in NZ, there is a great digital divide, which only increases inequalities. Every person should be able to access the digital infrastructure necessary to actively participate and thrive in society.
- **How:** This party would reduce barriers to digital inclusion by ensuring all communities have access to the internet, have suitable devices and have offline alternatives for essential services.
- **Full text:** ['Digital Technology', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_digital_technology))

<a id="pol55032"></a>
**Break up the supermarket duopoly and use part of it to create a government-owned supermarket** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55032` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, prices, economic-development-and-industry, business-support, key-policies-economy

- **Why:** This party says that two supermarket companies control what almost everyone in New Zealand eats and are taking about $1m a day in profit. This party says that in a cost of living crisis, there should be greater competition in the grocery sector so everyone can afford healthy and nutritious food.
- **How:** This party would break up the supermarket duopoly by requiring at least 120 stores and distribution centre capacity to be sold into public ownership. These would be folded into KiwiMart, a publicly owned supermarket chain with a mandate to prioritise affordability. It would also ban price gouging through legislation and introduce a pricing accuracy system which would compensate shoppers if overcharged. 
This is part of a wider policy which would establish a National Food Strategy, a beefed up Commerce Commission, more money for community-led food security and food grants, and a restored and expanded school lunch programme.  The cost of setting up KiwiMart would be $2.8bn.
- **Full text:** ['Greens launch plan to break up the supermarket duopoly and bring down the price of food', 6 September 2026 (accessed 18 September 2026)](documents/web-greens-launch-plan-to-break-up-the-supermarket-duopoly-and-bring-down-the-price-of-food.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_launch_plan_to_break_up_the_supermarket_duopoly_and_bring_down_the_price_of_food)); ['Affordable food for all of us' (PDF), undated (accessed 22 September 2026)](documents/web-affordable-food-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/affordable_food_for_all_of_us))

<a id="pol55040"></a>
**Support NZ made products, small business and sustainable industries**  
`POL55040` · topics: economy, business-support, economic-development-and-industry

- **Why:** This party says that small businesses are facing an economy that is stacked against them. Liquidations are the highest in almost 15 years and a lack of supermarket competition is squeezing shoppers and local suppliers. International corporates are flourishing while factories close and local businesses struggle. This party will work for an even playing field for local businesses and support sustainable low-carbon industries.
- **How:** This party would encourage consumer to support local by revitalising ‘Buy Kiwi Made’ and expanding country of origin labelling; commit government departments to buying more NZ goods and services; reform competition laws to create a fairer playing field for small businesses and consumers. It would foster sustainable, low carbon jobs in the ICT sector, particularly smaller firms. And it would invest in community-led regional development with a focus on sustainable building, regenerative agriculture, low-carbon infrastructure and iwi/hapū led economic activity.
- **Full text:** ['Small Business', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-small-business.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_small_business))

<a id="pol55041"></a>
**Better resource the agencies fighting anti-competitive practices**  
`POL55041` · topics: economy, business-support, economic-development-and-industry

- **Why:** This party says that small businesses are faced with a challenging landscape including a 15-year high in liquidations and a lack of supermarket competition which is squeezing local suppliers. Small businesses need an even playing field against international corporates where the profits go offshore.
- **How:** This party would reform competition laws to create a fairer playing field for small businesses and consumers. It would also increase the resources and powers of regulatory agencies investigating anti-competitive behaviour.
- **Full text:** ['Small Business', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-small-business.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_small_business))

<a id="pol55083"></a>
**Implement a one-year pause on AI data centers**  
`POL55083` · topics: economy, transport-and-infrastructure, artificial-intelligence-ai, energy, economic-development-and-industry, regional-development, internet-and-digital-technologies

- **Why:** This party says that AI data centres can currently be consented by councils with no community input. Overseas, data centres have had adverse effects on local water supplies and are high energy users, driving up power prices. The Datagrid data centre in Southland is expected to use 6 per cent of the country's electricity supply. This party says there needs to be more transparency and a chance for regulation to be developed.
- **How:** This party would call for a one-year moratorium on new AI data centres while proper rules are put in place to ensure there are benefits and don't unduly strain our resources. Solutions could include forcing centres to build their own energy or use closed loop water cooling systems, but currently there are no requirements for these under NZ rules.
- **Full text:** ['Call for a One Year Pause on Consenting New AI Data Centres', 26 July 2026 (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/web-call-for-one-year-pause-on-consenting-new-ai-data-centres.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/call_for_one_year_pause_on_consenting_new_ai_data_centres))

<a id="pol55111"></a>
**Fill empty stores by changing retail lease rules and addressing vacant buildings**  
`POL55111` · topics: economy, transport-and-infrastructure, business-support, regional-development, urban-planning-and-resource-management

- **Why:** This party says that towns and cities need help to keep their centres vibrant. Empty shops are apparent on most main streets because of high rents set when the market was stronger. Many commercial leases don't allow for a rent review below the level set.
- **How:** This party would ensure market rent reviews can lower rents as well as raise them. This will apply to both new and existing leases for businesses with a turnover of under $30m. 
It would also give councils the power to auction leases on shops that have been empty for more than a year, and a low-cost tribunal will be established for commercial lease disputes.
- **Full text:** ['Greens announce plan for city centre vibrancy ', 19 September 2026 (accessed 22 September 2026)](documents/web-green-party-announce-plan-for-city-centre-vibrancy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_announce_plan_for_city_centre_vibrancy))

<a id="pol55116"></a>
**Increase democratic scrutiny of trade agreements**  
`POL55116` · topics: economy, defence-and-foreign-affairs, te-ao-maori, trade-and-exports, international-relations, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that that NZ should have an independent approach to foreign affairs and trade that focuses on climate action, nature protection, indigenous and human rights.
- **How:** This party would implement a suite of measures including increased democratic scrutiny of trade agreements, and enforceable recognition of Te Tiriti o Waitangi obligations, workers’ rights, human rights and climate commitments in trade agreements.
- **Full text:** ['Global Affairs and Trade', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-global-affairs-and-trade.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_global_affairs_and_trade))

<a id="pol55279"></a>
**Ensure AI does not replace human judgement or democratic decision-making**  
`POL55279` · topics: economy, artificial-intelligence-ai

- **Why:** This party says that artificial intelligence should support human judgement, not replace it. Decisions that affect people's rights, welfare, health, education, employment, or access to public services must remain subject to meaningful human oversight and appeal.
- **How:** This party would require public registers of AI systems used by government agencies with full transparency around AI use, including where in policy processes it has been used, and where or how it has been used to inform decisions. The party would ensure people are held accountable for AI decisions and that everyone has a right to challenge AI-assisted decisions, and to opt out of being subject to AI-assisted decision- making.
- **Full text:** ['Safe, sovereign AI. An AI Plan for Aotearoa' (PDF), 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041)); ['Greens will regulate AI for the public good', 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/scoop-greens-will-regulate-ai-for-the-public-good.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_will_regulate_ai_for_the_public_good))

<a id="pol55280"></a>
**Refrain from purchasing or deploying autonomous weapons**  
`POL55280` · topics: economy, defence-and-foreign-affairs, artificial-intelligence-ai, defence-and-veterans

- **Why:** This party says that Artificial intelligence should support human judgement, not replace it, especially when it concerns warfare and the implications of that for the life and wellbeing of humans.
- **How:** This party would refuse to purchase or deploy autonomous weapons systems and support an international ban on these, in the same way the international community has banned chemical and biological weapons.
- **Full text:** ['Safe, sovereign AI. An AI Plan for Aotearoa' (PDF), 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041)); ['Greens will regulate AI for the public good', 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/scoop-greens-will-regulate-ai-for-the-public-good.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_will_regulate_ai_for_the_public_good))

<a id="pol55281"></a>
**Protect data sovereignty**  
`POL55281` · topics: economy, te-ao-maori, artificial-intelligence-ai, te-reo-maori-and-culture

- **Why:** This party says that data is a taonga (treasure). The party believes that New Zealanders, iwi, hapū, communities, and creators should retain meaningful control over how data about them is used. Language in particular is crucial to identity and culture, and must be protected from AI.
- **How:** This party would enforce explicit consent standards for AI training data, where appropriate remuneration must be provided for any Intellectual property used for training AI models. The party would ensure there are fines for any breaches, with the Privacy Commissioner given more power to enforce NZ's privacy laws. Government AI systems would be transparent, and there would be specific regulations requiring co-governance and co-development of Māori data and IP to protect Māori data sovereignty. Lastly, private information provided to the government and classified government will not be sent to third parties for model training.
- **Full text:** ['Safe, sovereign AI. An AI Plan for Aotearoa' (PDF), 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041)); ['Greens will regulate AI for the public good', 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/scoop-greens-will-regulate-ai-for-the-public-good.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_will_regulate_ai_for_the_public_good))

<a id="pol55293"></a>
**Place limits on AI data centres' use of water and electricity**  
`POL55293` · topics: economy, artificial-intelligence-ai

- **Why:** This party says that while AI seems like a virtual technology, it actually consumes large amounts of resources, taking up land, using water to cool servers and requiring electricity to power data centres. This party says that any data centres based in New Zealand would need to follow strict regulations to protect the environment.
- **How:** This party would require AI data centres to report on their environmental impact before being built. New facilities would be required to provide new renewable energy to cover their electricity use and their would be community consultation during the consenting process. To increase transparency, this party would require data centres to report their water use publicly.
- **Full text:** ['Safe, sovereign AI. An AI Plan for Aotearoa' (PDF), 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041))

<a id="pol55295"></a>
**Encourage the adoption of multiple types of AI**  
`POL55295` · topics: economy, law-justice-and-government, artificial-intelligence-ai, public-sector

- **Why:** This party says that AI companies can concentrate economic power, leading to bad outcomes for workers and individuals. These risks should be regulated by government.
- **How:** This party would encourage the use of public-interest and open-source AI models wherever possible, and mandate the use of multiple and interchangeable AI models to prevent vendor lock-in. It would encourage the use of NZ-based and NZ-focussed large language models, rather than relying on AI tools from overseas companies.
- **Full text:** ['Safe, sovereign AI. An AI Plan for Aotearoa' (PDF), 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041))

<a id="pol55297"></a>
**Create an AI office and require transparent AI use**  
`POL55297` · topics: economy, law-justice-and-government, defence-and-foreign-affairs, artificial-intelligence-ai, public-sector, internet-and-digital-technologies, international-relations

- **Why:** This party says that people have a right to know when they are interacting with AI and how it is being used. AI content should always be clearly labelled so that people understand when they are using an AI model.
- **How:** This party would create labelling requirements for artificial intelligence, and audit algorithms for high-risk AI systems, so there is monitoring of AI use in the public sector. It would create an AI office within the department of prime minister and cabinet (DPMC) and aim to eventually establish an AI commission with independent powers to conduct audits assurance and education regarding AI use. This party would also collaborate with international best practice like the EU AI Act and build on NZ's work in the Christchurch Call to cooperate with like-minded countries regarding AI regulation and governance.
- **Full text:** ['Safe, sovereign AI. An AI Plan for Aotearoa' (PDF), 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Digital Technology**: *Technology helping, not harming.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md))

1. Regulate social media platforms to address harmful content and behaviour by introducing a Digital Duty of Care. This will require algorithmic transparency and accessible and responsive complaints systems, as well as strengthening enforcement with an independent national regulator.
2. Regulate artificial intelligence to ensure its use is safe, transparent and accurate,
3. Crack down on tax avoidance and the offshoring of profits by multinational tech giants such as Meta, Amazon and Microsoft.
4. Adopt and implement the Māori Data Governance Model and Māori AI Governance Framework from Te Kāhui Raraunga across the public sector to protect Māori data sovereignty.
5. Strengthen obligations on data-holders to protect user and biometric data, increase penalties for privacy breaches, and strengthen enforcement powers for the Privacy Commissioner to better protect personal information.
6. Expand media and digital literacy education, and equip New Zealanders to navigate AI-generated media and other emerging digital technologies.
7. Reduce barriers to digital inclusion and ensure all communities have access to the internet, suitable devices and offline alternatives for essential services.

**Small Business**: *Championing small, local enterprise.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-small-business.md))

1. Revitalise ‘Buy Kiwi Made’ and expand country of origin labelling so it’s easier to support local businesses.
2. Commit government departments to buying more goods and services from Aotearoa businesses.
3. Foster sustainable, low-carbon jobs in the information and communication technology sector by expanding access to grants and rebates for smaller sized firms.
4. Continue to invest in community-led regional development with a focus on sustainable building, regenerative agriculture and horticulture, improving low-carbon infrastructure, and iwi- and hapū-led economic activity.
5. Reform competition laws to create a fairer playing field for small businesses and to benefit consumers. Increase the resources and powers of regulatory agencies to investigate anti-competitive practice.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **2026 election manifesto: 'A Green government. For all of us.' (38 policy chapters)**: The party's 43-page (23 PDF spreads) 2026 election manifesto, launched at its AGM on 26 July 2026. It frames the campaign around three priorities (affordable living, healthy nature and 'real democracy'), funded by taxes on the very wealthy and large corporations alongside an income tax cut for most earners, and sets out a short numbered 'Our plan' list in each of 38 policy chapters (Children to Zero waste). Every 'Our plan' commitment is listed individually below under its topic. Full text: [Green Party Manifesto 2026 — A Green government. For all of us. (full PDF)](documents/manifesto-2026-full-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1786330483/2026_GREEN_PARTY_MANIFESTO.pdf?1786330483))
- **Tax policy 'A tax system for all of us': wealth tax, capital acquisitions tax, higher tax on big corporates, bank levy, and income tax cuts for 96% of earners**: The 12-page tax plan (announced 21 June 2026). Revenue measures: a 2.5% tax on the net assets of the super-rich above $10 million (family homes exempt); a capital acquisitions tax on gifts and inheritances over $1 million (family farms and homes exempt); a 33% company tax rate for the largest 0.7% of companies while small and medium firms stay at 28%; a 0.06% levy on the liabilities of the four big banks; enforcing a 5% withholding tax on big tech profits sent offshore; and reversing the coalition's landlord interest-deductibility and bright-line changes. Income tax: a $10,000 tax-free threshold, new stepped brackets and a 45% rate above $160,000. The party's own Parliamentary-Library-based modelling puts the net gain at about $5.1-5.7 billion a year (2027/28-2030/31). Full text: [A tax system for all of us — Contributing fairly to society (Tax policy 2026)](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525))
- **'Safe, sovereign AI': regulate artificial intelligence for the public good**: An 8-page AI plan (announced 12 October 2026). It proposes rules so that AI does not replace human or democratic decision-making (no fully automated decisions with substantive effects in public services), protects data sovereignty, privacy and creators' work, operates within environmental limits (including controls on data centres), shares benefits rather than concentrating them, and is transparent and accountable. Full text: [Safe, sovereign AI — An AI Plan for Aotearoa](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041))
- **'Affordable food for all of us': KiwiMart public supermarket chain, stronger grocery regulation and a Fair Food Fund**: A 15-page food-system policy (announced 6 September 2026). It proposes a publicly owned supermarket chain 'KiwiMart' (estimated acquisition and capitalisation cost about $2.8bn, citing an estimate of $1.3bn to buy 120 stores and two distribution centres), more Commerce Commission funding and higher penalties for grocery-sector breaches, a $150m Fair Food Fund for community food security, and more funding for regional food providers. Full text: [Affordable food for all of us — Reforming the food system](documents/affordable-food-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/89620/attachments/original/1788650611/AFFORDABLE_FOOD_FOR_ALL_OF_US_POLICY_FINAL.pdf?1788650611))
- **Green Budget 2025: the Greens' costed alternative Budget (2025)**: The party's 44-page alternative Budget published in 2025 (not a 2026 election document, but the basis of several 2026 policies). It sets out spending on health (free GP and dental care), families, ending poverty, climate, oceans, nature, a green industrial strategy, transport, housing and energy, funded by wealth, corporate and other tax changes, with a fiscal summary. Cited by policy.nz for the free-ECE policy. Full text: [Green Budget 2025 (the Greens’ costed alternative Budget, 2025)](documents/green-budget-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/39988/attachments/original/1756800427/Budget_Print_MW_version_5-08-25_web.pdf?1756800427))
- **Standing policy: Business Policy**: Standing party policy on business: reform regulators, require accessibility, support the Māori economy and new business models, promote local business and co-invest in skills and research. Full text: [Business Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/business-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64528/attachments/original/1781650904/Policy-Greens_Business_Policy_2025-2026.pdf?1781650904))
- **Standing policy: Community in the Economy Policy**: Standing party policy on regional development, local economies and procurement, and on government relationships with, and funding of, the community and voluntary sector. Full text: [Community in the Economy Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/community-economy-policy-2011-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64570/attachments/original/1778624597/Policy-Greens_Community-Economy_Policy_2011-2025.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Digital Policy**: Standing party policy on equitable access to digital technology and data, with a harm-based approach to social media, AI and quantum computing, Te Tiriti and privacy/online-safety protections. Full text: [Digital Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/digital-policy-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64584/attachments/original/1778624597/Policy-Greens_Digital_Policy_2025.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Government in the Economy Policy**: Standing party economic policy: a fairer tax system, universal public services and infrastructure, living within planetary limits, Māori economic justice and more public ownership and direct service provision. Full text: [Government in the Economy Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/government-economy-policy-2024-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64724/attachments/original/1778624605/Policy-Greens_Government_Economy_Policy_2024-2026.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Tourism Policy**: Standing party tourism policy: sustainable, low-emission, community-benefiting tourism and Māori cultural tourism under tikanga and tino rangatiratanga. Full text: [Tourism Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/tourism-policy-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65004/attachments/original/1778624618/Policy-Greens_Tourism_Policy_2025.pdf))

Full text: [Greens commit to tax the super-rich and large corporates fairly, cut income tax for 96%](documents/web-greens-commit-to-tax-the-super-rich-and-large-corporates-fairly-cut-income-tax-for-96.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_commit_to_tax_the_super_rich_and_large_corporates_fairly_cut_income_tax_for_96))

## Incomes and employment

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54653"></a>
**Return the tax rate to 33 percent for New Zealand's biggest corporations**  
`POL54653` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, tax-and-income-support, business-support

- **Why:** This party says that multi-national businesses send profits offshore and don't pay the same taxes as local businesses. This increases prices and decreases wages. Lower corporate taxes have not led to improvements for working New Zealanders. The corporate tax rate increase would only be on the 0.7 percent biggest businesses in NZ, including banks, supermarkets and energy companies. This would level the playing field for small and medium businesses.
- **How:** This party would increase the tax rate to 33% for large corporations while keeping it at 33% for small and medium businesses. A bank levy of 0.06 percent would be added to the liabilities of the four big banks (ANZ, BNZ, Westpac and ASB).
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525))
- **Note:** Discrepancy: policy.nz's "How" says the rate would be kept "at 33% for small and medium businesses"; the cited tax policy PDF says the corporate rate would be kept at 28 percent for small and medium enterprises and returned to 33 percent only for the biggest 0.7 percent of corporations (see document, page 3).

<a id="pol54654"></a>
**Enforce the 5 percent withholding tax on major international tech and finance companies**  
`POL54654` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, tax-and-income-support, internet-and-digital-technologies, foreign-investment

- **Why:** This party says that big corporations like Google, Meta, Microsoft, Visa, Uber and Mastercard use loopholes to avoid taxes in NZ. Withholding tax requirements must be enforced so that these companies contribute their fair share.
- **How:** This party would enforce a 5 percent withholding tax on the services and licences of big tech and finance companies.
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525))

<a id="pol54731"></a>
**Reverse interest deductibility for residential property investment**  
`POL54731` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, housing, tax-and-income-support, economic-development-and-industry, housing-affordability, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says that says that a landlords' tax cut means housing speculators and landlords can spend more on house-buying than first home buyers. This makes it harder for New Zealanders to get onto the property ladder.
- **How:** This party would  remove interest deductibility for residential property investment and restore the bright-line test period to 10 years. The bright-line test will require income tax to be paid on gains from selling residential investment property if sold within 10 years of being purchased. The profit from the sale is applied to the seller’s annual personal income and taxed at the marginal income tax rate.  The family home would be exempt.
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525)); ['Policy Announcement - A tax system for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-tax-system-for-all.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/tax_system_for_all))

<a id="pol54735"></a>
**Make the first $10,000 that people earn tax-free** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54735` · topics: incomes-and-employment, tax-and-income-support, key-policies-incomes-and-employment

- **Why:** This party says that people working for a salary or wage pay double the effective tax rate of the very richest people in NZ. It believes this tax cut will help support New Zealanders and ease the high cost of living. This party says 96 percent of New Zealanders would get a tax cut with this policy.
- **How:** This party would create a new tax-free threshold of $10,000.
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525)); ['Policy Announcement - A tax system for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-tax-system-for-all.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/tax_system_for_all)); ['Income', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-income.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_income))

<a id="pol54737"></a>
**Create a new tax bracket for income earned over $160,000** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54737` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, tax-and-income-support, economic-development-and-industry, key-policies-incomes-and-employment

- **Why:** This party says that says that NZ has low income taxes for people who earn high salaries compared to most other countries. It believes the tax system should be updated so that those who earn the most should contribute more and those who earn less should contribute less.
- **How:** This party would reset NZ's income tax settings, including a 45 percent threshold for income above $160,000. Everyone earning under $160,000 per year will receive a tax cut, with those earning the least benefiting most. It would reduce taxes for lower and middle-income earners by implemening progressive tax brackets between $10,000 and $160,000. For example, people would pay just 10 percent of the income they earn between $10,000 and $19,999, and so on.
- **Full text:** ['A tax system for all of us' (PDF), undated](documents/tax-policy-2026-a-tax-system-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/71965/attachments/original/1782084525/GreensTaxPolicy2026.pdf?1782084525)); ['Policy Announcement - A tax system for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-tax-system-for-all.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/tax_system_for_all)); ['Income', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-income.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_income))

<a id="pol54787"></a>
**Create a job guarantee programme for jobs focused on ecosystem regeneration** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54787` · topics: environment, incomes-and-employment, te-ao-maori, economy, conservation, jobs-and-employment, key-policies-incomes-and-employment, land-and-resources, key-policies-environment, regional-development

- **Why:** This party says that the NZ economy currently incentivises the exploitation of the environment for short-term gain. This has resulted in ecosystem loss and degradation. This policy would incentivise environmental protection by providing jobs that focus on that goal.
- **How:** This party would establish a job guarantee programme focused on understanding, protecting, and regenerating ecosystems.  It would expand funding for Jobs for Nature – Mahi mō te Taiao.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54988"></a>
**Crack down on tax avoidance by offshore corporations**  
`POL54988` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, tax-and-income-support, internet-and-digital-technologies

- **Why:** This party says that multinational tech giants are part of New Zealand's digital infrastructure. However, they are not adequately regulated, meaning profits are going overseas rather than back into the New Zealand economy.
- **How:** This party would crack down on tax avoidance and the offshoring of profits by multinational tech giants such as Meta, Amazon and Microsoft.
- **Full text:** ['Digital Technology', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-digital-technology.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_digital_technology))

<a id="pol55015"></a>
**Support better working conditions including pay equity rules**  
`POL55015` · topics: incomes-and-employment, jobs-and-employment, wages-and-working-conditions

- **Why:** This party says that everyone is entitled to secure and meaningful work in innovative, environmentally sustainable, and socially responsible workplaces. That includes fair pay, freedom from discrimination and an equitable relationship between employers, union and workers.
- **How:** This party would ensure pay equity, such as requiring pay transparency and pay-parity management, and ensure everyone including marginalised groups get the training and information they need to have equitable employment opportunities. It would support people whose work has been affected by climate change, and progress towards a shorter standard working week It would ensure time in lieu or overtime is provided for work out of contracted hours.

**Support default union membership for new workers**  
`POL55016` · topics: incomes-and-employment, jobs-and-employment, wages-and-working-conditions

- **Why:** This party says that everyone is entitled to secure and meaningful work in innovative, environmentally sustainable, and socially responsible workplaces. That includes fair pay, freedom from discrimination and an equitable relationship between employers, unions, and workers.
- **How:** This party would make union membership an opt-out option when people start a new job. Newly hired employees would be default, be members unless they wish not to be.
