<a id="pol55026"></a>
**Return to a 10-year period based in New Zealand for superannuation eligibility**  
`POL55026` · topics: incomes-and-employment, community-and-inclusion, superannuation-and-savings, seniors

- **Why:** This party says that overseas New Zealanders deserve to remain connected to NZ, and have a interest in the future of the country. Nearly 15 percent of New Zealanders live in other countries. The government should take a compassionate approach to New Zealanders who have lived or are living overseas.
- **How:** This party would restore the 10-year period of being based in NZ for superannuation eligibility.
- **Full text:** ['Overseas New Zealanders', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-overseas-new-zealanders.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_overseas_new_zealanders))

<a id="pol55112"></a>
**Refuse to use AI for decisions on welfare payments**  
`POL55112` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, benefits, artificial-intelligence-ai

- **Why:** This party says that the government's push for automated decision erroneously left tens of thousands of financially vulnerable people without funds.  Similar issues overseas have proven why decisions on who receives financial support should not be left to algorithms.
- **How:** This party would not use AI and automated decision making for welfare payment decisions such as cancelling, suspending, or reducing welfare payments, or imposing benefit sanctions.
- **Full text:** ['Green Party pledge to remove AI from welfare payment decisions', 11 September 2026 (accessed 16 September 2026)](documents/web-green-party-pledge-to-remove-ai-from-welfare-payment-decisions.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_pledge_to_remove_ai_from_welfare_payment_decisions))

<a id="pol55132"></a>
**Increase benefit payments and improve standards for beneficaries**  
`POL55132` · topics: incomes-and-employment, tax-and-income-support, benefits

- **Why:** This party says that everyone has a right to a liveable income that guarantees enough for food, safe housing and good quality of life. This party says the cost of living has got worse, and thousands of children go to bed hungry and families are struggling to pay rent or mortgages.
- **How:** This party would increase benefits to liveable rates and individualise benefit entitlements. This party would also abolish benefit sanctions. The party would reform the Social Security Act to uphold dignity of people seeking support and ensure everyone has a secure income, and implement a high-trust working model at work and income. This party would also overhaul recoverable assistance grants, write off existing debt to MSD and ban attachment orders against beneficiaries.

<a id="pol55135"></a>
**Introduce a tax for net wealth over $10m**  
`POL55135` · topics: incomes-and-employment, economy, tax-and-income-support, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says a wealth tax is part of a fairer tax system, that can reduce income inequality and ensure everyone has enough to live. This party says multi-millionaires pay $9 in tax for every $100 they make, while teachers pay $22 in tax for every $100 they make. This party says a wealth tax makes super-rich New Zealanders pay a fair share.
- **How:** This party would introduce a 2.5 percent tax on net wealth worth over $10m, with an exemption for the family home.
- **Full text:** ['Income', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-income.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_income)); ['Greens commit to tax the super-rich and large corporates fairly, cut income tax for 96% ', 21 June 2026 (accessed 23 September 2026)](documents/web-greens-commit-to-tax-the-super-rich-and-large-corporates-fairly-cut-income-tax-for-96.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_commit_to_tax_the_super_rich_and_large_corporates_fairly_cut_income_tax_for_96))

<a id="pol55136"></a>
**Expand ACC to cover income support and treatment**  
`POL55136` · topics: incomes-and-employment, community-and-inclusion, tax-and-income-support, disability, benefits, wages-and-working-conditions

- **Why:** This party says that that everyone deserves a livable income if they are unemployed, disabled, sick or a caregiver.
- **How:** This party would extend ACC to cover income support and treatment for injuries, disability and illness, regardless of origin.
- **Full text:** ['Income', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-income.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_income))

<a id="pol55137"></a>
**Strengthen working for families**  
`POL55137` · topics: incomes-and-employment, tax-and-income-support, benefits

- **Why:** This party says that that families are struggling to pay rent or mortgages, to feed their children, or to warm their homes, as the cost of living has worsened. This party says that families should be looked after at every stage of life.
- **How:** This party would increase the working for families rate, reduce abatements and extend tax credits to families who are unemployed.
- **Full text:** ['Income', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-income.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_income))

<a id="pol55296"></a>
**Protect children and worker's rights in the use of AI**  
`POL55296` · topics: incomes-and-employment, community-and-inclusion, education, economy, wages-and-working-conditions, young-people, schools, artificial-intelligence-ai

- **Why:** This party says that AI can concentrate economic power and impact individuals and communities. Power should not be concentrated, and the use of AI should not be at the expense of children and worker's rights.
- **How:** This party would ensure children are not forced to use AI, especially at school, and broadly make sure that AI use around kids is in line with the UNCRC’s Joint Statement on Artificial Intelligence and the Rights of the Child. It would also require that workers are consulted about AI use in their workplace.
- **Full text:** ['Safe, sovereign AI. An AI Plan for Aotearoa' (PDF), 12 October 2026 (accessed 13 October 2026)](documents/ai-policy-2026-safe-sovereign-ai.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/97177/attachments/original/1791760041/AI_policy_2026_WEBSITE.pdf?1791760041))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Income**: *Liveable incomes made possible through a fair tax system.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-income.md))

1. Reform the Social Security Act to uphold the dignity of those who need support and ensure everyone has a secure income and a decent standard of living. Implement a high-trust operating model at Work and Income.
2. Increase benefits to liveable rates, individualise benefit entitlements and abolish benefit sanctions.
3. Extend ACC to cover income support and treatment for injuries, disability and illnesses regardless of origin.
4. Strengthen Working for Families by boosting the rate, reducing abatements and extending tax credits to families who are out of work.
5. Make income tax settings fair and progressive, by introducing a tax-free threshold, reducing tax rates for low- and middle-income earners, and increasing the top rate of income tax.
6. Introduce a super-rich tax of 2.5% on net wealth over $10 million so that multimillionaires pay their fair share.
7. Overhaul recoverable assistance so that grants are available for urgent needs instead of loans. Work towards writing off existing debt to MSD and ban attachment orders against beneficiaries.

**Workforce rights**: *Employment protections and decent pay for all workers.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-workforce-rights.md))

1. Strengthen worker bargaining power by changing employment law to default union membership. This means people automatically join a union when they start a new job, but can opt out later.
2. Expand the right to strike, including for breaches of collective agreements and political and solidarity reasons.
3. Strengthen employment law to protect all workers’ free time with a right to disconnect. Ensure protection from unwanted contact or surveillance out of working hours.
4. Explore legislating to put workers’ representatives on boards of publicly listed companies.
5. Guarantee annual minimum wage increases that at least keep up with inflation. Pay all publicly funded roles a living wage. Work towards matching the minimum wage to the living wage.
6. Increase paid parental leave to 15 months for the primary caregiver, with additional ring-fenced leave for partners, while also increasing payments.
7. Protect platform and gig-economy workers by strengthening the test for whether a worker is a contractor.
8. Close the gender and ethnic pay gaps by undoing the Government’s changes to the pay equity regime and resuming claims, pay transparency measures, and comprehensive pay gap reporting.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **Youth Jobs Guarantee: a job or training pathway for every 16-24-year-old out of work for six months**: A 7-page jobs policy (published October 2026) guaranteeing every 16-to-24-year-old who has been out of work for six months a pathway into a locally based job or training, delivered with councils and building on the Mayors Taskforce for Jobs. The document's indicative costings rise from $604m in the first year to $1,631m in the fourth. Full text: [Youth Jobs Guarantee — A guaranteed job for every young person in Aotearoa](documents/youth-jobs-guarantee-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/96649/attachments/original/1791665775/a_job_for_every_young_person-policy.pdf?1791665775))
- **Standing policy: Livelihoods Policy**: Standing party policy on income support: an adequate, accessible and dignified welfare system recognising unpaid work, regulation of lenders and debt help for low-income households. Full text: [Livelihoods Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/livelihoods-policy-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64836/attachments/original/1778624609/Policy-Greens_Livelihoods_Policy_2025.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Workforce Policy**: Standing party workforce policy: a just transition to a low-emissions economy, training and upskilling, workers' rights to secure income, safety and collective organising, and better work-life balance. Full text: [Workforce Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/workforce-policy-2020-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65074/attachments/original/1781651291/Policy-Greens_Workforce_Policy_2020-2026.pdf?1781651291))
- **Protect Uber drivers' employee status** (November 17, 2025): 17 Nov 2025: the party said a Green government would honour the Supreme Court ruling that Uber drivers are employees and oppose legislation removing that status. Full text: [Greens to protect employee status of Uber Drivers](documents/web-greens-to-protect-employee-status-of-uber-drivers.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_to_protect_employee_status_of_uber_drivers))
- **Make union membership the default for new employees** (July 16, 2026): 16 Jul 2026: the party backed an Automatic Union Membership member's bill amending the Employment Relations Act so new employees are union members by default, and said it would take the policy into the election. Full text: [Green Party calls for union membership as default setting for new employees](documents/web-green-party-calls-for-union-membership-as-default-setting-for-new-employees.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_calls_for_union_membership_as_default_setting_for_new_employees))
- **No AI or automated decisions on welfare payments or sanctions** (September 11, 2026): 11 Sep 2026: pledge that in government AI and automated decision-making would not be used to cancel, suspend or reduce benefits or impose sanctions. Full text: [Green Party pledge to remove AI from welfare payment decisions](documents/web-green-party-pledge-to-remove-ai-from-welfare-payment-decisions.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_pledge_to_remove_ai_from_welfare_payment_decisions))
- **Restore pre-2025 pay equity law and fund a care-worker settlement (~$1.1bn a year)** (October 01, 2026): 1 Oct 2026: commitment to restore pay-equity law to its pre-2025 form and set aside about $1.1 billion a year to settle the claim of around 65,000 care and support workers. Full text: [Greens commit to pay equity for care workers](documents/web-greens-commit-to-pay-equity-for-care-workers.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_commit_to_pay_equity_for_care_workers))
- **Abolish the training wage; more support for apprentices** (17 October 2026): 17 Oct 2026: commitment to remove the training-wage exemption so apprentices are paid at least the minimum wage, with more support for apprentices. Full text: [Green Party To Abolish Training Wage And Provide More Support For Apprentices](documents/scoop-green-party-to-abolish-training-wage-and-provide-more-support-for-apprentices.md) ([original](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2610/S00150/green-party-to-abolish-training-wage-and-provide-more-support-for-apprentices.htm))

## Health

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54937"></a>
**Ensure everyone has reliable access to affordable, fresh, and nutritious food**  
`POL54937` · topics: health, public-health

- **Why:** This party says that access to nourishing food is essential for people’s health, well-being, and ability to participate fully in society. A food system therefore should support communities by reducing food insecurity, strengthenen local connections, and ensuring that healthy choices are available to everyone.
- **How:** This party would support community food hubs, distribution programmes, and local food initiatives that improve access to fresh and nutritious foods. It would address unfair practices within the grocery sector through stronger legislation that promotes competition and fairness. Actions would also focus on making nourishing foods and drinks more affordable and accessible.
- **Full text:** ['Food Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/food-policy-2024-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64668/attachments/original/1779663153/Policy-Greens_Food_Policy_2024-2026.pdf?1779663153))

<a id="pol55004"></a>
**Remove barriers to accessing essential services for disabled people** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55004` · topics: community-and-inclusion, health, housing, disability, health-services, social-and-public-housing, key-policies-health

- **Why:** This party says that our current laws, policies and social norms mean disabled people are often denied the same rights as others. They are often excluded from places and activities non-disabled people take for granted, such as accessing public transport, basic health care and inclusive education. Interactions with public services should effectively support disabled people's wellbeing.
- **How:** This party would remove barriers to accessing the essentials like health care, transport, inclusive education and disability support for all who need it. The party would do this by drawing on the Enabling Good Lives approach as a guide to how government agencies work with disabled people. It would provide public service staff with resourcing and training to meet access needs and counter ableism and operate using a high-trust model that recognises disabled people as experts in their own lives, while making support and services available when needed.
- **Full text:** ['Disability', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_disability)); ['Disability Policy', 2 March 2026 (accessed 16 September 2026)](documents/unretrievable-disability-policy-full.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/disability_policy_full))

<a id="pol55007"></a>
**Repeal and replace the Misuse of Drugs Act**  
`POL55007` · topics: health, law-justice-and-government, alcohol-and-drugs, health-services, medicines, criminal-justice

- **Why:** This party says that drugs exist and people use them. This party wants to reduce harm that can arise from drug use by taking a health-based approach rather than a punitive approach to drug use. Drug criminalisation leads to unsafe drug use, fosters organised crime, and traps those convicted in a cycle of poverty that, in turn, contributes to harmful drug use.
- **How:** This party would repeal and replace the Misuse of Drugs Act with an evidence-based approach that reduces harm, treats drug use as a health issue and provides for regulated access to cannabis for personal use. As a short-term measure, it would implement the recommendations of the Law Commission’s 2011 review of the Misuse of Drugs Act 1975. The new Act would be co-designed with Māori under a Te Tiriti o Waitangi framework and would promote the rights and interests of whānau, hapū, and iwi.
- **Full text:** ['Drug Law Reform', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-drug-law-reform.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_drug_law_reform)); ['Drug Policy' (PDF), 2 March 2026 (accessed 17 September 2026)](documents/drug-policy-2016-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64612/attachments/original/1778624599/Policy-Greens_Drug_Policy_2016-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol55008"></a>
**Increase funding for harm reduction from drugs and addiction support services**  
`POL55008` · topics: health, alcohol-and-drugs, health-funding-and-workforce

- **Why:** This party says that drug-related harm is significant and increasing in Aotearoa New Zealand, driven mainly by alcohol, methamphetamine, and emerging cocaine use. Dependence on alcohol or other drugs affects a person’s physical and mental health, relationships, employment, and personal finances. It can also affect the family and friends of people who use drugs and the victims of drug-related or drug-funded crime.
- **How:** This party would ramp up funding for harm reduction and addiction support for people who reduce or stop their drug intake, including through kaupapa Māori, rainbow, youth and harm reduction programmes. This would include funding innovative approaches to working with groups where smoking rates are disproportionately high, such as Tagata Moana and Māori. It would provide age-appropriate education about substance use disorders in schools and ensure harm reduction information is easily accessible for everyone.
- **Full text:** ['Drug Law Reform', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-drug-law-reform.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_drug_law_reform)); ['Drug Policy' (PDF), 2 March 2026 (accessed 17 September 2026)](documents/drug-policy-2016-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64612/attachments/original/1778624599/Policy-Greens_Drug_Policy_2016-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol55010"></a>
**Ensure rainbow communities can access gender-affirming healthcare** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55010` · topics: community-and-inclusion, health, rainbow-community, health-services, key-policies-health, key-policies-community-and-inclusion

- **Why:** This party says that all New Zealanders need to be able to live with dignity and safety. Rainbow New Zealanders in particular need access to public services and healthcare that serve their needs. This includes healthcare that supports their gender identity and removing barriers, such as surgery for intersex people and the ban on puberty blockers.
- **How:** This party would provide timely, non-discriminatory access to gender-affirming healthcare, such as hormones and surgery. These treatments would be given more resources in the health system, and the focus would be on informed consent and self-determination. The ban on puberty blockers would be revoked.
- **Full text:** ['Rainbow Communities', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-rainbow-communities.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_rainbow_communities))

<a id="pol55021"></a>
**Support measures to reduce violence**  
`POL55021` · topics: health, law-justice-and-government, community-and-inclusion, public-health, family-justice-and-safety, mental-health, women, rainbow-community

- **Why:** This party says that everyone must be able to live free from violence and abuse. Aotearoa has among the highest rates of intimate partner violence, sexual violence and child abuse, and this is disproportionately harmful to people in vulnerable professions and families.
- **How:** This party would invest in services to help victims and increase awareness of the drivers of violence and of restorative practices. It would fund a national strategy to eliminate family and sexual violence (Te Aorerekura) and amend the Crimes Act to criminalise coercive control. It would also amend the definition of consent. and restore relationship and sexuality teaching guidelines in all schools. Funding would be lifted for mental health support for victims and others in the justice system.
- **Full text:** ['Violence Prevention', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-violence-prevention.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_violence_prevention))

<a id="pol55069"></a>
**Expand free mental health services to all communities**  
`POL55069` · topics: health, te-ao-maori, community-and-inclusion, mental-health, wellbeing-and-services, young-people

- **Why:** This party says that people experiencing mental distress deserve access to care, no matter where they are. Social factors like the climate crisis, poverty and unemployment exacerbate the mental health crisis in NZ. Mental health services should be well funded and integrated into the public health system, not fragmented and requiring people to turn to private services which also have long waiting lists. Harmful practices, like seclusion, should be phased out so that care does not cause further harm
- **How:** This party would extend free mental health care to all communities, with more funding for community-specific mental health care. This includes youth services like Piki, Mana Ake and Youth One Stop Shops throughout Aotearoa. It also includes Asian Family Services and dedicated support for postnatal mental health. Mental health facilities would be further resourced so that the practice of seclusion could be phased out, replaced by community care models. Specific kaupapa Māori mental health and addiction programmes, including programmes focused on youth, would be funded as well.
- **Full text:** ['Mental Health', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-mental-health.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_mental_health))

<a id="pol55074"></a>
**Implement suicide prevention recommendations, especially focused on youth**  
`POL55074` · topics: health, community-and-inclusion, mental-health, young-people

- **Why:** This party says that all people in distress should be able to access support, especially as social factors like poverty, unemployment and climate change worsen mental health outcomes. Caring for those in distress and ensuring mental health workers are funded and resourced to avoid burnout will lead to better outcomes.
- **How:** This party would implement all the recommendations in Every Life Matters – He Tapu te Oranga o ia Tangata Suicide Prevention Strategy. It would also commit to further actions identified as part of a kaupapa Māori suicide prevention strategy, especially those aimed at reducing suicide among people younger than 24.
- **Full text:** ['Mental Health', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-mental-health.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_mental_health))

<a id="pol55075"></a>
**Connect primary and secondary mental health care with clear referral pathways**  
`POL55075` · topics: health, mental-health, public-health

- **Why:** This party says that everyone deserves to access quality mental health care in the community where they live. Services should be culturally relevant and focused on the area of greatest need. With demand high and mental health workers burned out, making referral to secondary services clear, fast and consistent will lead to good outcomes.
- **How:** This party would link primary health and secondary mental health services with clear referral pathways in every health region. The focus would be on areas that have disproportionate need.
- **Full text:** ['Mental Health', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-mental-health.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_mental_health))

<a id="pol55091"></a>
**Increase funding for food grants, food banks and other food security programmes** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55091` · topics: incomes-and-employment, health, prices, public-health, key-policies-health

- **Why:** This party says that NZ produces enough food to feed 40 million people, but somehow food prices are too high for many. Dismantling the supermarket duopoly alone is not enough to deliver food security, and demand for community solutions like food banks is immense. This party would offer more support to those building alternative models for food security, processing and distribution.
- **How:** This party would double the cap on food grants at the cost of $94-$100m annually. It would lift funding for regional food distribution hubs and community food providers from up to $8m to $25m a year and set up a $150m a year Fair Food Fund for community-led food security, such as food rescue programmes, marae and social supermarkets.
- **Full text:** ['Greens launch plan to break up the supermarket duopoly and bring down the price of food', 6 September 2026 (accessed 22 September 2026)](documents/web-greens-launch-plan-to-break-up-the-supermarket-duopoly-and-bring-down-the-price-of-food.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_launch_plan_to_break_up_the_supermarket_duopoly_and_bring_down_the_price_of_food)); ['Affordable food for all of us' (PDF), undated (accessed 22 September 2026)](documents/web-affordable-food-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/affordable_food_for_all_of_us))

<a id="pol55117"></a>
**Decriminalise cannabis for personal use**  
`POL55117` · topics: health, law-justice-and-government, alcohol-and-drugs, public-health, criminal-justice, medicines

- **Why:** This party says that harmful drug use must be treated first as a health issue, rather than a criminal justice issue. While there can be adverse consequences from drug use, not all drug use is abusive or problematic, and evidence indicates making substance use illegal makes it harder for people to seek help. In terms of drugs, cannabis causes far less harm than alcohol, methamphetamine, cocaine, or tobacco.
- **How:** This party would regulate cannabis for legal personal use, with a legal age limit. Previous criminal convictions for these offences are removed from all records. Cultivation for personal use would generally be no longer illegal, and an evidence-based approach would be taken on which regulatory model best minimises harm. This includes improving drug-driving laws and providing education on the harmful effects of heavy or prolonged cannabis use.
Medicinal cannabis would be accessible and affordable for patients.
- **Full text:** ['Drug Law Reform', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-drug-law-reform.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_drug_law_reform)); ['Drug Policy' (PDF), 2 March 2026 (accessed 17 September 2026)](documents/drug-policy-2016-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64612/attachments/original/1778624599/Policy-Greens_Drug_Policy_2016-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol55118"></a>
**Increase free public health services**  
`POL55118` · topics: health, te-ao-maori, incomes-and-employment, health-services, mental-health, public-health, wellbeing-and-services, prices

- **Why:** This party says that everyone should have reliable access to public healthcare, including during and after civil emergencies. This party says hospitals and GP clinics are stretched and over-capacity, and suffering from under-investment from successive Governments. It says private provider costs in healthcare are preventing people from seeking help early, causing health issues to escalate.
- **How:** This party would work towards free public primary healthcare and dental care, that can be accessed by everyone. They would increase focus on prevention and early screening, early diagnosis and treatment of non-communicable diseases, such as kidney disease, cancers, diabetes, cardiovascular and respiratory diseases. This party would fund the creation of mobile clinics for health care, mental health, diagnostic services and dental vans, for rural communities and during and after civil emergencies, including by-Māori, for-Māori community clinics. This party would also increase funding for data and digital infrastructure and capability in the health sector. This party would also regulate providers of private infrastructure and hold them to high standards of privacy.

<a id="pol55119"></a>
**Simplify funding for Hauora Māori providers**  
`POL55119` · topics: health, te-ao-maori, health-funding-and-workforce, wellbeing-and-services, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

This party says Māori are underserved by the health system, and that progress to provide equitable healthcare has been undone. This party says kaupapa Māori health services should be supported to end health inequalities.
- **How:** This party would stop overly prescriptive Hauora service contracts. This party would simplify and improve funding arrangements for providers of health services for whānau, hapū iwi and hapori. They would commit to working with Iwi Māori partnership boards and Hauora Māori to do this.

`POL55120` · topics: health, incomes-and-employment, health-funding-and-workforce, wages-and-working-conditions

- **Why:** This party says that that under-investment in health services has led to an understaffed hospital workforce and GP clinics that can't accept new enrollments. They say there are unsafe conditions for health workers and lengthy wait times. This party says everyone should be able to access timely healthcare from a supported workforce
- **How:** This party would ensure all health workers have fair wages. They would fund pay parity for primary care and hauora providers. This party would set safe staffing levels, to improve worker wellbeing and quality of care.

`POL55122` · topics: health, health-services, medicines

`POL55148` · topics: health, media-culture-and-recreation, alcohol-and-drugs, sports-and-recreation, broadcasting-and-media

<a id="pol55149"></a>
**Enable licensing for psychedelic treatment**  
`POL55149` · topics: health, alcohol-and-drugs, health-services, medicines

- **Why:** This party says that the 'war on drugs' approach from 50 years ago has increased harm and addictions, and diverts money away from public health services. This party supports an evidence-based approach that prioritises people's health.
- **How:** This party would enable a licensing system for psychedelic treatment, and recognise taonga status of our native psylocibin (magic mushroom) species.
- **Full text:** ['Drug Law Reform', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-drug-law-reform.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_drug_law_reform))

<a id="pol55150"></a>
**Reduce nicotine availability and fund culturally responsive services to quit smoking and vaping**  
`POL55150` · topics: health, health-services, alcohol-and-drugs

- **Why:** This party says that that funding for addiction services should be increased, and NZ should take a harm-reduction approach to alcohol and drugs. This party believes young people should be protected from nicotine by stronger regulations for retailers.
- **How:** This party would phase out nicotine availability and fund culturally responsive vaping and smoking cessation services. This party would also implement stronger regulation and enforcement for nicotine retailers.
- **Full text:** ['Drug Law Reform', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-drug-law-reform.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_drug_law_reform))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Drug Law Reform**: *Putting health first to keep people safe.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-drug-law-reform.md))

1. Repeal and replace the Misuse of Drugs Act with an evidence-based approach that reduces harm, treats drug use as a health issue and provides for regulated access to cannabis for personal use.
2. Ramp up funding of harm reduction and addiction support for people who want to reduce or stop their drug intake, including through kaupapa Māori, rainbow, youth and harm reduction programmes.
3. Work with local government and service providers — focused on safe drug use, addictions and social housing — to support town centres negatively impacted by drug use.
4. Enable a licensing system for psychedelic treatment and recognise taonga status of our native psylocibin (magic mushroom) species.
5. Pass Good Samaritan laws that allow people to call for help without fear of punishment. Legalise and fund other overdose measures including increasing the availability of naloxone and medically supervised consumption spaces to reduce harm and save lives.
6. Fund culturally responsive vaping and smoking cessation services, phase down nicotine availability, and implement stronger regulation and enforcement for nicotine retailers to protect young people and children.
7. Replace the Land Transport (Drug Driving) Amendment Act 2025 with an impairment approach to reduce harm from all causes of driving impairment.
8. Phase out all alcohol advertising and sponsorship, including at sporting and cultural events, and use funding from the alcohol levy to establish a well-resourced national harm-reduction campaign.

**Health**: *Accessible care to support everyone’s good health.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-health.md))

1. Ensure public health care services are accessible for all with a focus on prevention and early screening, diagnosis and treatment for non-communicable diseases, such as kidney disease, cancers, diabetes and cardiovascular and respiratory diseases.
2. Fund mobile health care and mental health care clinics, diagnostics services and dental vans to ensure rural communities are able to access health services regularly, including during and after civil emergencies.
3. Work towards a publicly delivered, fully free provision of dental and primary care services, including by-Māori for-Māori community clinics.
4. Fund one-stop-shops that provides pā harakeke wrap-around services for whānau throughout hapūtanga, after birth, first stages of life, and navigating parenthood.
5. Stop overly prescriptive Hauora service contracts and commit to working with Iwi Māori Partnership Boards and Hauora Māori to simplify and improve funding arrangements for providers of health services for whānau, hapū iwi and hapori.
6. Increase sustained funding for Pharmac so that it can invest in new medicines and health care devices to enhance treatment outcomes and quality of life.
7. Expand the Minor Health Conditions Service to help pharmacies treat common health issues and update the Integrated community pharmacy services agreement to encourage pharmacists to give appropriate medical advice by compensating them fairly.
8. Ensure all health care workers have fair wages, fund pay parity for primary care nurses and hauora providers, and set safe staffing levels to support worker wellbeing and quality of care for patients.
9. Work towards diagnosing and treating endometriosis faster, including by developing formal clinical guidelines for diagnosing and treating endometriosis and other pelvic pain, and by funding specialised clinics across Aotearoa.
10. Increase funding for data and digital infrastructure and capability in the health sector, and regulate and hold private providers of infrastructure to high standards of privacy.

**Mental Health**: *A safe, supportive and accessible care system.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-mental-health.md))

1. Expand free mental health services to all communities so everyone can access support when they need it. This includes expanding youth services like Piki, Mana Ake and Youth One Stop Shops throughout Aotearoa, Asian Family Services and dedicated support for postnatal mental health.
2. Implement the recommendations of the Every Life Matters – He Tapu te Oranga o ia Tangata Suicide Prevention Strategy, together with further actions identified as part of the kaupapa Māori response to suicide prevention with particular focus on people under 24.
3. Implement community care models and ensure mental health facilities are resourced adequately to enable phasing out the use of seclusion (solitary confinement) entirely, and increase the availability of early intervention to reduce the use of compulsory care.
4. Fund kaupapa Māori mental health and addiction programmes and ensure rangatahi Māori voice is represented in policy.
5. Link primary health and secondary mental health services with clear referral pathways in every health region and focus on areas which have disproportionate need.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **Standing policy: Drug Policy**: Standing party policy taking a harm-minimisation, health-based approach to alcohol and other drugs instead of criminal prohibition: accessible addiction treatment, a legal regulated cannabis market and drug-checking/education. Full text: [Drug Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/drug-policy-2016-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64612/attachments/original/1778624599/Policy-Greens_Drug_Policy_2016-2026.pdf))
- **Presumptive ACC cover for firefighters with occupational cancers** (July 27, 2026): 27 Jul 2026: a member's bill to give eligible career and volunteer FENZ firefighters automatic ACC cover for cancers likely caused by their work. Full text: [New Bill would give firefighters with cancer automatic ACC cover](documents/web-new-bill-would-give-firefighters-with-cancer-automatic-acc-cover.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/new_bill_would_give_firefighters_with_cancer_automatic_acc_cover))

## Housing

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54764"></a>
**Mandate the installation of solar on new or renovated public homes, where appropriate** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54764` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, housing, energy, housing-quality, social-and-public-housing, key-policies-housing

- **Why:** This party says that installing solar panels on public housing would reduce the power bills of those dwelling there.
- **How:** This party would install rooftop solar panels on more than half of all public homes within four years. It would also mandate the installation of solar on new or renovated public homes, alongside energy  efficient appliances that cut bills.  This party estimates that this policy would cost $460 million over four years..
- **Full text:** ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy)); ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies))

<a id="pol55079"></a>
**Create an independently enforced Rental Warrant of Fitness** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55079` · topics: housing, renting, housing-quality, key-policies-housing

- **Why:** This party says that a warm safe home should be a basic right, but much of NZ's housing is a source of misery and expense, while people who can afford to own their homes get richer. Housing should be affordable and healthy.
- **How:** This party would increase the stringency of the current Healthy Homes Standards for rental housing. It would also introduce a "Rental Warrant of Fitness" to certify that a house was appropriate to rent out, which would be independently certified.
- **Full text:** ['Housing', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-housing.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_housing))

<a id="pol55081"></a>
**Increase public housing supply and housing support**  
`POL55081` · topics: housing, transport-and-infrastructure, housing-affordability, social-and-public-housing, construction

- **Why:** This party says that housing should be a basic right, not a "get rich quick" scheme only for those who can afford it. Building affordable, sustainable homes in the places where people want to live will focus on housing for homes, rather than housing as an investment.
- **How:** This party would increase public housing stock by building tens of thousands of new homes. It would fund papakāinga to be built on Māori land. People in need of emergency housing would be guaranteed "same-day" placement, and concepts like "housing first" and supported housing would be used for those in most need to support them to build a life in a stable home.
- **Full text:** ['Housing', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-housing.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_housing))

<a id="pol55082"></a>
**Limit annual rent increases to 2 percent** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55082` · topics: housing, renting, housing-affordability, key-policies-housing

- **Why:** This party says that housing should be a basic right, not an investment that a lucky few can get rich from. Renting should be fixed so that healthy homes in places where people want to live are affordable.
- **How:** This party would limit annual rent increases to no more than 2 percent per year. The amount charged in rent for a new tenancy would be linked to what previous tenancies paid, so that there is not a jump in price between tenants.
- **Full text:** ['Housing', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-housing.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_housing))

<a id="pol55084"></a>
**Encourage mixed-use areas and walkability in new housing developments**  
`POL55084` · topics: housing, transport-and-infrastructure, law-justice-and-government, housing-affordability, housing-quality, urban-planning-and-resource-management, local-government, public-transport-cycling-and-walking

- **Why:** This party says that a home is a place to build a life with loved ones. Building houses in existing urban areas will prevent cities from encroaching into rural areas, and mean homes are more sustainable for the next generation.
- **How:** This party would require councils to collaborate with communities, including mana whenua on decisions about new housing. Housing should be in mixed-use areas with shops and housing, within existing urban areas, and prioritise being able to walk around easily.
- **Full text:** ['Housing', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-housing.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_housing))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Housing**: *Safe, warm, affordable homes for everyone.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-housing.md))

1. Build tens of thousands of new public homes, significantly increase accessible public housing stock and fund more papakāinga to be built on whenua Māori.
2. Make rents more affordable by limiting annual rent increases to no more than 2%, and link rents in a new tenancy to what the previous tenants paid.
3. Strengthen the Healthy Homes Standards and enforce them with an independently certified Rental Warrant of Fitness.
4. Expand immediate wraparound support such as housing first, transitional and supported housing services, and guarantee same-day accommodation placement for people experiencing homelessness.
5. Require councils to collaborate with communities, including mana whenua, to provide sustainable, walkable, mixed-use urban areas and enable enough new affordable homes within existing urban areas to meet the needs of the next generation.
6. Reverse generous tax benefits for landlords and property speculators that encourage housing to be treated as an investment, rather than a basic right.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **Standing policy: Housing and Sustainable Communities Policy**: Standing party housing policy: a large government-run public housing programme, more Māori housing, accessible housing for disabled people, lower building emissions and regenerative urban development; housing treated as a right. Full text: [Greens announce plan to end homelessness & fix housing crisis](documents/web-greens-announce-plan-to-end-homelessness-fix-housing-crisis.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_announce_plan_to_end_homelessness_fix_housing_crisis?ref=webworm.co))

- **How:** This party would keep and expand the Ka Ora, Ka Ako programme in schools. It would extend the programme to approximately 400 more schools and fund the programme permanently so schools and families have long-term certainty.

<a id="pol55027"></a>
**Make it easier for people based in other countries to repay student loans** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55027` · topics: education, incomes-and-employment, tertiary-fees-and-student-support, key-policies-education, superannuation-and-savings

- **Why:** This party says that overseas New Zealanders deserve to remain connected to NZ, and have an interest in the future of the country. Nearly 15% of New Zealanders live in other countries and many have moved overseas recently. The government should take a compassionate approach to New Zealanders who have lived or are living overseas. People with student loans should not face harsh consequences or border arrests.
- **How:** This party would reduce student loan interest rates for New Zealanders in other countries. It would cease the practice or arresting people at the NZ border for not repaying their student loans and extend the repayment holiday for people living overseas.
- **Full text:** ['Overseas New Zealanders', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-overseas-new-zealanders.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_overseas_new_zealanders))

<a id="pol55035"></a>
**Introduce fees-free tertiary education, universal student allowances and paid placements** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55035` · topics: education, community-and-inclusion, tertiary-education-and-research, trades-and-vocational-education, key-policies-education, young-people

- **Why:** This party says that our education system should be lifelong and accessible to everyone. But currently students struggle to pay for and stay in tertiary education - including vocational and community adult education - and providers are struggling to provide courses and attract and retain talent..
- **How:** This party would provide fees-free tertiary education, reduce student loan repayments, and work towards a write-off of student loan debt. It would also introduce a universal student allowance for tertiary students to cover their needs, and training allowances for those doing compulsory work placements.
- **Full text:** ['Tertiary Education', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-tertiary-education.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_tertiary_education))

<a id="pol55037"></a>
**Ensure proper resourcing for tertiary education providers**  
`POL55037` · topics: education, tertiary-education-and-research, tertiary-fees-and-student-support, trades-and-vocational-education

- **Why:** This party says that education should be lifelong and accessible to all. In reality, tertiary education is cost-prohibitive to many, and the institutions themselves struggle with funding to keep courses open and attract and retain good teachers and researchers. Polytechnics are underfunded and don't always meet community needs and  more apprenticeship programmes are needed.
- **How:** This party would ensure polytechnics have adequate funding and strong local control to meet workforce and community needs, and protect staff and courses. 
Tertiary organisations would receive a funding formula that prioritises teaching, pastoral care, and research, with a focus on collaboration rather than competition between providers. 
Apprenticeship programmes would be expanded and all tertiary education would be fees-free.

**Expand access to learning support**  
`POL55100` · topics: education, community-and-inclusion, schools, disability

- **Why:** This party says that people with disabilities and diverse learners should be fully supported to learn, belong and thrive.
- **How:** This party would expand the criteria for access to learning support and establish a single agency for specialist supports for learners with health and disability needs. It would implement all actions in the Ministry for Educations 'Framework for Action: A Quality Education for All' strategy.
- **Full text:** ['Education', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-education.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_education))

<a id="pol55101"></a>
**Work towards creating a Māori Education Authority**  
`POL55101` · topics: education, te-ao-maori, schools, te-reo-maori-and-culture

- **Why:** This party says that that education should be grounded in Te Tiriti o Waitangi, and Māori voices and communities should be central to decision making. It believes education should honour tino rangatiratanga and embrace te reo Māori and mātauranga Māori.
- **How:** This party would work towards a Māori Education Authority which governs and funds all Māori medium and Kaupapa Māori education. This party would support Māori communities to provide an education system that is driven by whānau needs and makes whakapapa-based decisions.
- **Full text:** ['Education', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-education.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_education))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Education**: *An Aotearoa where everyone can reach their full potential.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-education.md))

1. Make early childhood education free and provide it as a public good like schooling, removing the market and stopping corporate interests from running for-profit centres.
2. Reset the Government’s rushed and ideological curriculum and assessment reforms, reverting to the original direction of Te Mātaiaho and ensuring qualification reform follows curriculum reform.
3. Expand the criteria for access to learning support. Establish a single agency to coordinate and resource specialist supports for learners with health and disability support needs. Implement all actions in the ‘Framework for Action: A quality education for all’.
4. Expand Ka Ora, Ka Ako as a first step towards providing a warm and nutritious locally made lunch to all schoolchildren.
5. Support rangatiratanga to provide an education system driven by whānau needs, allowing space for whakapapa-based decisions, and working towards a Māori Education Authority governing and funding all Māori medium and Kaupapa Māori education.
6. Support the workforce so all teachers and educators have fair wages and staffing levels that support quality teaching. Co-develop a long-term workforce plan with the sector.

**Tertiary Education**: *Opportunities for everyone to learn and grow.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-tertiary-education.md))

1. Support students with a universal student income, including at postgraduate level, and ensure courses that require work placements pay students at least the living wage.
2. Overhaul funding for the tertiary sector to ensure it delivers rigorous research, a wide range of course options, quality teaching, comprehensive student services, decent pay and conditions for staff, and full participation of disabled students.
3. Empower polytechnics with adequate funding and strong local control to meet workforce and community needs, and protect courses, campuses and staff from cuts.
4. Expand free apprenticeship programmes, reduce student loan repayments and work towards fees-free tertiary education and a student debt write-off.
5. Ensure all tertiary institutes uphold Te Tiriti o Waitangi and deliver equitable outcomes for ākonga Māori (Māori students). Expand the Māori and Pasifika Trades Training programme, and fully resource Whare Wānanga to increase opportunities for kaupapa Māori education.
6. Return to default student union membership and develop a governance model for tertiary organisations in which staff, students, communities and tangata whenua govern together.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **Paid placements for trainee nurses, teachers and social workers; abolish the training wage for apprentices**: A 4-page workforce policy (16-17 October 2026): a universal weekly payment for students during compulsory practical placements (nursing, teaching, social work and similar), relocation support, abolition of the training-wage exemption so apprentices get at least the minimum wage, a grant for apprentices' tools, and more pastoral support. Indicative costs are about $75-80m and $117m a year for the two parts. Full text: [Paid Placements & Apprenticeships — For our future workforce](documents/paid-placements-apprenticeships-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://img.scoop.co.nz/media/pdfs/2610/Green_Party__Paid_Placements__Apprenticeships_for_our_future_workforce_2026.pdf))
- **Restore, extend and permanently fund Ka Ora, Ka Ako free school lunches** (August 20, 2026): 20 Aug 2026: commitment to restore the original Ka Ora, Ka Ako school lunch model, extend it and fund it permanently, with locally prepared food. Full text: [Green Party announces plan for free and nutritious school lunches](documents/web-green-party-announces-plan-for-free-and-nutritious-school-lunches.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_announces_plan_for_free_and_nutritious_school_lunches))
- **Free early childhood education (Green Budget 2025)** (May 14, 2025): 14 May 2025 (Green Budget 2025): plan to make early childhood education free and shift the sector away from for-profit provision. Full text: [Green Budget: Early Childhood Education for Everyone](documents/web-green-budget-early-childhood-education-for-everyone.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_budget_early_childhood_education_for_everyone))
- **Universal weekly payment for students on practical placements** (16 October 2026): 16 Oct 2026: a universal weekly payment for students during practical training for nursing, teaching, social work and similar roles, plus relocation support. Full text: [Greens Announce Paid Placements For Nurses, Teachers, And Social Workers](documents/scoop-greens-announce-paid-placements-for-nurses-teachers-and-social-workers.md) ([original](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2610/S00143/greens-announce-paid-placements-for-nurses-teachers-and-social-workers.htm))

## Environment

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54738"></a>
**Set science-based national standards for freshwater**  
`POL54738` · topics: environment, fresh-water-and-pollution

- **Why:** This party says that it supports taking a precautionary approach to protect people's health. It says there is emerging evidence of a link between nitrate contamination in drinking water and bowel cancer and preterm births. The Danish government has committed to lowering nitrate levels in drinking water by almost 90%.
- **How:** This party would lower the nitrate limit in drinking water based on scientific evidence. It would also create a drinking water regulator to monitor, report on and enforce protections for drinking water sources.
- **Full text:** ['Drink Swim Fish Fixing the rules for fresh water and oceans' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74309/attachments/original/1783476578/DSF_Policy_2026.pdf?1783476578)); ['Policy Announcement - Drink, Swim, Fish — Fixing the rules for water', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-drink-swim-fish.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/drink_swim_fish))

<a id="pol54739"></a>
**Strengthen rules for wastewater discharges**  
`POL54739` · topics: environment, media-culture-and-recreation, fresh-water-and-pollution, sports-and-recreation

- **Why:** This party says that NZ's favourite swimming spots are often too polluted to safely swim in due to weak rules for discharging wastewater into our rivers and coastlines.
- **How:** This party would strengthen rules for wastewater discharges to ensure rivers, lakes and seas are swimmable.
- **Full text:** ['Drink Swim Fish Fixing the rules for fresh water and oceans' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74309/attachments/original/1783476578/DSF_Policy_2026.pdf?1783476578)); ['Policy Announcement - Drink, Swim, Fish — Fixing the rules for water', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-drink-swim-fish.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/drink_swim_fish))

<a id="pol54740"></a>
**Phase out synthetic nitrogen fertiliser**  
`POL54740` · topics: environment, economy, health, fresh-water-and-pollution, conservation, agriculture, public-health

- **Why:** This party says that synthetic nitrogen fertiliser is harming our environment by polluting our rivers, lakes and groundwater. Between 1991 and 2019, the use of synthetic nitrogen increased by more than 600%. At the same time, more than a third of monitored groundwater sources saw an increase in nitrate levels. It says that contamination of community water supplies poses a risk to human health.
- **How:** This party would lower the current cap on synthetic nitrogen fertiliser for the dairy industry. This is because the dairy industry uses more than 60% of synthetic nitrogen. It would work with farmers across other sectors to cut fertiliser. There is no timeframe set for the phase-out. It would work alongside the Ministry for Primary Industries to develop a clear implementation plan with milestones and would support regenerative farming initiatives such as the Sustainable Food and Fibre Futures fund.
- **Full text:** ['Drink Swim Fish Fixing the rules for fresh water and oceans' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74309/attachments/original/1783476578/DSF_Policy_2026.pdf?1783476578)); ['Policy Announcement - Drink, Swim, Fish — Fixing the rules for water', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-drink-swim-fish.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/drink_swim_fish))

<a id="pol54741"></a>
**Protect at least 30% of  New Zealand’s oceans and seas**  
`POL54741` · topics: environment, economy, te-ao-maori, oceans, fisheries, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that current rules allow overfishing and enable the pollution of NZ's oceans. It believes marine ecosystems should be protected and restored so New Zealanders can enjoy health oceans to swim in and abundant fish stock to feed their families with.
- **How:** This party would reform marine-protected area legislation to make it fit-for-purpose, consistent with the Tiriti o Waitangi, and guided by tikanga values. This would include supporting kaitiakitanga Māori in marine management and iwi- and hapū-led conservation and restoration initiatives.
- **Full text:** ['Drink Swim Fish Fixing the rules for fresh water and oceans' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74309/attachments/original/1783476578/DSF_Policy_2026.pdf?1783476578)); ['Oceans and Fisheries', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-oceans-and-fisheries.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_oceans_and_fisheries)); ['Policy Announcement - Drink, Swim, Fish — Fixing the rules for water', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-drink-swim-fish.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/drink_swim_fish))

<a id="pol54744"></a>
**Establish a government backed clean energy loan scheme** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54744` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, environment, incomes-and-employment, energy, emissions-reduction, prices, key-policies-environment

- **Why:** This party says that this policy would help make household power bills more affordable so people would not have to choose between heating or eating. This party says that since 2021, 100,000 more households cannot afford to heat their home and 76,000 could not pay their bills on time. The easiest way to lower household power is to help people generate and store solar power but most people are barred from this due to the expensive upfront cost of equipment purchase.
- **How:** This party would establish a government-backed loan scheme to enable zero-interest clean energy loans for up to 90% of homeowners. Homeowners can use these loans for rooftop solar, batteries and making energy efficiency upgrades. Some borrowers will have the option to defer repayments until property sale, while loans will be paid off over time via a separate levy. Central government would be a 20 percent shareholder, with participating councils and the Local Government Funding Agency (LGFA) splitting the remaining 80 percent. The Energy Efficiency and Conservation Authority (EECA) would implement the scheme.
- **Full text:** ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies)); ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy))

<a id="pol54771"></a>
**Advocate for universal membership to international agreements that support biodiversity**  
`POL54771` · topics: environment, defence-and-foreign-affairs, conservation, international-relations

- **Why:** This party says that there is a global biodiversity crisis, and the rates of species extinction have increased due to pressures from human activity. As this is a global problem, it requires a global commitment to respect biodiversity.
- **How:** This party would ensure NZ becomes party to and advocates for universal membership of international agreements that support biodiversity, such as the Convention on Biological Diversity.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54772"></a>
**Prevent ongoing biodiversity loss**  
`POL54772` · topics: environment, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that there is an urgent need for action on the global biodiversity crisis caused by human activities.
- **How:** This party would expand the National Policy Statement for Indigenous Biodiversity, setting clear implementation plan targets with a pathway on how to achieve those. It would also support community education on biodiversity loss through incorporating environmental education into early-to-adult curricula, funding nature-based school activities and providing more resources to DOC to expand its advocacy role.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54773"></a>
**Minimise climate change's impact on biodiversity**  
`POL54773` · topics: environment, climate-adaptation, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that human activities have resulted in a warming climate. This climate change is harming the Earth's biodiversity, with species unable to quickly adapt to their changing habitats.
- **How:** This party would consider climate change impacts and adaptation strategies when designing conservation programmes.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54774"></a>
**Strengthen New Zealanders' relationship with nature and conservation efforts**  
`POL54774` · topics: environment, economy, media-culture-and-recreation, conservation, climate-adaptation, tourism, sports-and-recreation

- **Why:** This party says that NZ's priorities need to urgently change to prevent rapid biodiversity loss and the increased rates of species extinction. Biodiversity is declining at such a rate it is threatening ecosystems and the human systems that rely on them.
- **How:** This party would explore redirecting DOC's purpose to focus solely on conservation. It would enable this to happen by reallocating its tourism, recreation and heritage priorities to other agencies. It would also support practices that recognise the rights and wellbeing of the natural world.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54788"></a>
**Create a fund to resource ecosystem protection projects**  
`POL54788` · topics: environment, conservation, climate-adaptation

- **Why:** This party says that loss and degradation of ecosystems are occurring due to human activity encouraged by an economy focused on short-term gain. This would provide reliable funding that instead encourages environmentally protective activities.
- **How:** This party would fund Action for Nature, implement the biodiversity strategy, and also national guidance to protect indigenous biodiversity, including the protection of taonga species.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54789"></a>
**Encourage landowners to regenerate and maintain native biodiversity**  
`POL54789` · topics: environment, economy, te-ao-maori, conservation, agriculture, te-reo-maori-and-culture, land-and-resources

- **Why:** This party says that prioritising the health of indigenous species and ecosystems will allow NZ's environment to thrive.
- **How:** This party would introduce a biodiversity credits system that incentivises private and Māori landowners to establish areas of indigenous biodiversity and regenerate indigenous ecosystems. A strong emphasis would be placed on protecting areas such as wetlands, as they are particularly affected by biodiversity loss. Resource management legislation and plans would take an ecosystem-based approach, encouraging this indigenous regeneration. It would also require new developments to undergo in-depth analysis of environmental impacts.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54790"></a>
**Ensure New Zealand's activity in Antarctica protects its biodiversity**  
`POL54790` · topics: environment, conservation, oceans

- **Why:** This party says that says humans are interdependent with other species and environments. Our survival requires a functional biosphere, including protecting Antarctica's biodiversity and environment.
- **How:** This party would develop and fund an environmental regeneration plan for the Ross Dependency and establish an environmental impact assessment system for human activities in Antarctica, such as research and tourism. It would also collaborate with other Antarctic Treaty signatories to uphold responsible management of the region.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54792"></a>
**Monitor and control air pollutants**  
`POL54792` · topics: environment, health, conservation, fresh-water-and-pollution, emissions-reduction, public-health

- **Why:** This party says that efforts such as this would ensure future generations inherit a healthy environment where they can breathe easily. Air pollution has a negative impact on human health.
- **How:** This party would introduce mandatory monitoring and controls for air pollutants. It would also update the National Environmental Standards for Air Quality to reflect international standards.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54793"></a>
**Minimise light pollution**  
`POL54793` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, conservation, te-reo-maori-and-culture

- **Why:** This party says that this would help protect ecosystems that rely on natural dark and light cycles. Aoraki Mackenzie is the largest Dark Sky Reserve in the Southern Hemisphere. Protecting more areas from light pollution protects both natural and cultural heritage, with te ao Māori seeing the night sky as an important connection to ancestral knowledge and navigation.
- **How:** This party would support research and development on efficient light technologies that minimise light pollution. It would also increase the number of protected dark sky reserves.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54794"></a>
**Restore native species populations so they do not become extinct**  
`POL54794` · topics: environment, community-and-inclusion, conservation, animal-welfare, oceans

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand is known as a hotspot for its native biodiversity that has evolved with unique characteristics due to the island environment. For example, there are many native birds that can't fly, as they evolved in a land without predators. In the last 150 years, any native species have been placed at risk of extinction due to habitat loss, predators and pollution.
- **How:** This party would broaden and update recovery plans to cover more threatened species. It would broaden legal protections and conservation strategies to cover indigenous fish, plants, invertebrates, and fungi to recognise the importance of diversity. It would also investigate appropriate regulation that limits or suspends the harvesting of vulnerable aquatic species, such as whitebait, to allow populations to regenerate.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54796"></a>
**Exterminate, manage and monitor invasive species**  
`POL54796` · topics: environment, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that many of New Zealand's unique flora and fauna are at risk of extinction due to invasive species. There are many efforts in place to control and eliminate these invasive species and they need continued support.
- **How:** This party would expand the "mainland islands" where intensive invasive species management occurs, as well as significantly increasing funding for organisations that eradicate and control invasive species and prioritising the elimination of high-risk species.
