It would also strengthen regulation and enforcement around introducing, releasing, and commercial use of invasive species and develop human and effective chemical, biological and physical invasive species control options, while still using effective options currently available like 1080, cyanide and trapping.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54798"></a>
**Ensure that diseases that negatively affect indigenous species are controlled**  
`POL54798` · topics: environment, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand's unique biodiversity is particularly vulnerable to the negative impact of invasive species, pollution and disease such as Kauri dieback and myrtle rust.
- **How:** This party would fund research to understand diseases that affect indigenous species and establish strategies to combat the spread of these diseases.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54799"></a>
**Ensure genetic diversity and variation of indigenous species are protected**  
`POL54799` · topics: environment, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand's biodiversity has evolved uniquely, which means our indigenous species are particularly vulnerable to extinction and human pressures, including genetic bottlenecks.
- **How:** This party would research and monitor genetic bottlenecks and inbreeding depression in indigenous species. It would fund genetic rescue programmes to increase genetic variation, establish genome and seed banks for indigenous species and explore ways to use eco-sourcing in nurseries and re-wilding programmes.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54800"></a>
**Tighten biosecurity measures to prevent the introduction of new species**  
`POL54800` · topics: environment, economy, conservation, agriculture

- **Why:** This party says that the introduction of new species and biological hazards threatens New Zealand's unique biodiversity and agricultural sector.
- **How:** This party would ensure biosecurity measures that prevent the introduction of new species and biological hazards are vigorous, well-resourced, and effective.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54801"></a>
**Regenerate indigenous forests in New Zealand**  
`POL54801` · topics: environment, economy, media-culture-and-recreation, conservation, forestry, sports-and-recreation

- **Why:** This party says that indigenous forests provide a home and habitat to many of New Zealand's native fauna. Protecting these forests will ensure the whole ecosystem can thrive.
- **How:** This party would strengthen the existing restrictions and penalties on the harvesting and export of indigenous logs and chips, including swamp kauri. It would ensure that any removal of windthrown trees on public conservation land is ecologically and culturally sound. It would also prioritise the control of introduced pests that contribute to forest collapse (i.e. possums, pigs, and deer) over recreational hunting.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54804"></a>
**Take a collaborative, Te Tiriti o Waitangi and te ao Māori approach to conservation**  
`POL54804` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, conservation, climate-adaptation, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says conservation has become disconnected from, and is failing to uphold, Te Tiriti o Waitangi. This is further distancing Māori from their ancestral lands and undermining their kaitiaki guardian role of looking after the environment. Te ao Māori principles can help all New Zealanders better care for their environment.
- **How:** This party would ensure all environmental protection and resource management legislation reflects Te Tiriti o Waitangi and Māori cultural values, and upholds the tino rangatiratanga of iwi and hapū to protect their environment. In cooperation with local government, this policy would also ensure conservation efforts uphold kaitiaki through enabling customary use of indigenous resources and implementing the recommendations of the Ko Aotearoa Tēnei Waitangi Tribunal (WAI262) report. It would recognise native species as tāonga, Māori treasures, and increase public awareness of the colonial history of public conservation land, and how Te Tiriti o Waitangi and conservation intersect.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Te Tiriti o Waitangi', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-te-tiriti-o-waitangi.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_te_tiriti_o_waitangi)); ['2026 Māori Manifesto' (PDF), undated (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/maori-manifesto-2026-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1785798775/Ma%CC%84ori_Manifesto_Web_Version.pdf?1785798775))

<a id="pol54805"></a>
**Ensure conservation projects are provided with sufficient funding and resources to achieve their goals**  
`POL54805` · topics: environment, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that despite decades of conservation efforts across New Zealand, biodiversity is continuing to decline.
- **How:** This party would expand DOC's core capacity to work on conservation efforts by increasing resource funding and employing more field and research personnel. It would also enable the purchase, protection and regeneration of special places that are threatened in the public conservation estate and strengthen the role of conservation boards as individual advocates representing the community. Community efforts to regenerate urban biodiversity would also be supported, with funding increased for evidence-based landscape-scale conservation projects.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54830"></a>
**Support Māori autonomy in their responses to climate change**  
`POL54830` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, economy, climate-adaptation, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, government-revenue-and-finance, emissions-reduction

- **Why:** This party says that climate change is damaging Māori customary practices, cultural identity, well-being and connection to the land. Historical and current injustices have also weakened the resources of Māori communities and businesses to mitigate and adapt to climate change.
- **How:** This party would ensure Māori communities and leadership networks are resourced to research, monitor and provide advice on climate change on policies and legislation that might negatively affect them. It would also fund Māori-led initiatives for transitioning away from fossil fuel and agribusiness ventures into sustainable industries. 
This policy would ensure overarching support for the autonomy and self-determination of hapū, iwi and Māori businesses in leading their own climate change responses, with funding provided to redress inequities.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf)); ['Māori Centred', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-maori-centred.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_maori_centred)); ['2026 Māori Manifesto' (PDF), undated (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/maori-manifesto-2026-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1785798775/Ma%CC%84ori_Manifesto_Web_Version.pdf?1785798775)); ['Te Tiriti o Waitangi', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-te-tiriti-o-waitangi.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_te_tiriti_o_waitangi))

<a id="pol54831"></a>
**Include agriculture, shipping and aviation in emissions calculations and reduction efforts**  
`POL54831` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, economy, climate-adaptation, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, emissions-reduction, forestry, economic-development-and-industry, agriculture

- **Why:** This party says that the overarching legislation for addressing climate change is too weak to actually prevent the negative impacts we are seeing and will see more in the future. Large, profit-driven corporations are influencing decision-makers reducing the voice of individuals and communities. NZ's dependence on fossil fuel is also deeply embedded in all parts of our economy, which is a barrier to economic resilience and limits the effectiveness of climate policies.
- **How:** This party would require the implementation of Te Tiriti o Waitangi and co-governance in decision-making and extend the Climate Change Response Act to cover every sector and all greenhouse gases. 
Domestic air and shipping fuel emissions would also be included in calculating emissions for both the public and private sectors. Non-forestry carbon sequestration technologies would be on a pathway to net carbon-zero, and emission reduction targets would be transparent and align with global obligations. Purchases of offshore emissions credits would be allowed only as a last resort. There would be robust monitoring and enforcement of the legal obligations for emissions reduction contained in the Act.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54832"></a>
**Enforce timely and effective implementation of climate legislation**  
`POL54832` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, law-justice-and-government, climate-adaptation, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, local-government

- **Why:** This party says that the overarching legislation for addressing climate change is not strong enough to prevent the negative impacts we are seeing and will see more in the future. Economic activity, linked to greenhouse gas emissions, is prioritised over the voices of the community.
- **How:** This party would set legally binding emission reduction targets across regions and sectors that align with the national Emission Reduction Plans. Local governments would be held accountable for meeting their responsibilities under the Climate Change Response Act and Māori communities would be empowered to implement their own climate adaption plans, as well as co-design carbon-negative economic strategies. The party would also promote research and development into fossil fuel alternatives, improve coordination of climate mitigation and adaptation activities and ensure the NZ public have access to the latest climate change-related evidence.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54833"></a>
**Focus on reducing gross emissions, rather than net emissions**  
`POL54833` · topics: environment, economy, climate-adaptation, emissions-reduction, economic-development-and-industry, forestry

- **Why:** This party says that the overarching legislation for addressing climate change is not strong enough to prevent the negative impacts we are seeing and will see more in the future. Economic activity, linked to greenhouse gas emissions, is prioritised over the voices of the community.
- **How:** This party would ensure the Emissions Trading Scheme is sufficient to meet reduction obligations under the Climate Change Response Act and can compensate for climate change mitigation and adaptation costs. The scheme must be consistent with the Climate Change Commission's advice and incentivise gross rather than net emissions reductions. It would phase out the free allocation of units, removing forestry offsetting so the system functions as a genuine ‘cap-and-trade’ system, and redirect funding from the reformed ETS to an improved Government Investment in Decarbonising Industry Fund.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54834"></a>
**Ensure all sectors are covered by an effective emissions pricing system**  
`POL54834` · topics: environment, economy, climate-adaptation, emissions-reduction, economic-development-and-industry, forestry, agriculture

- **Why:** This party says that all sectors need to be incentivised to reduce their greenhouse gas emissions, in order to prioritise the health of the environment and humanity over profit.
- **How:** This party would phase out the free allocation of NZUs to emission-Intensive and trade-exposed industries, and implement a framework for pricing agricultural greenhouse gas emissions. It would also include emissions from international aviation in pricing schemes. Marine-based 'blue carbon' - carbon dioxide captured and stored by the world's ocean and coastal ecosystems - would be included in the ETS. Alternative emissions pricing mechanisms would be implemented when the ETS is not doing its job in reducing greenhouse gas emissions. Lastly, existing foresters would received equitable treatment from any changes in forestry-related carbon offsetting systems.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54841"></a>
**Advocate internationally for strong climate action from other countries and partners**  
`POL54841` · topics: environment, defence-and-foreign-affairs, climate-adaptation, international-relations

- **Why:** This party says that that New Zealand has a legal and moral responsibility, supported by the Te Tiriti o Waitangi, to lead climate action on a global scale. New Zealand is relatively wealthy but currently ranks poorly against other countries for its climate change policies. This party says we need to do better to help our vulnerable neighbours, like Pacific Island nations.
- **How:** This party would advocate internationally for the strengthening of international climate agreements and encourage all countries to increase their Nationally Determined Contributions so that global emissions halve by 2030 and reach net carbon-negative by 2050 to limit global average temperature rise to 1.5 degrees Celsius. The party would also advocate for urgent climate action to be embedded in all international agreements and institutions, with a legally binding mechanism created to ensure international compliance with climate commitments and ramped-up funding made available to finance climate adaptation around the world. It would promote that decisions are led by Indigenous leadership and countries and communities particularly vulnerable to climate change.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54843"></a>
**Adopt a collaborative approach to climate action**  
`POL54843` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, defence-and-foreign-affairs, community-and-inclusion, climate-adaptation, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, international-relations, pasifika

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand is relatively wealthy but currently ranks poorly against other countries for its climate change policies. This party says we need to do better to help our vulnerable neighbours, like Pacific Island nations and lead with an Indigenous approach.
- **How:** This party would integrate New Zealand's commitment to Te Tiriti o Waitangi and co-governance into international climate negotiations. It would also support global indigenous-led networks to combat climate change and help Pacific nations mitigate and adapt by providing ongoing, responsive financial support. The NZ public would lead the Government's stance on global climate action.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54845"></a>
**Support Pacific nation's leadership on global climate change action**  
`POL54845` · topics: environment, defence-and-foreign-affairs, climate-adaptation, international-relations

- **Why:** This party says that as a relatively wealthy nation, New Zealand has a duty to support our Pacific Island neighbours who are on the frontline of climate change.
- **How:** This party would actively support Pacific states' leadership on climate change issues of concern, including security and sovereignty related to the future survival of island nations. It would advocate for legal protection for people displaced by climate change and systems for loss and damage compensation from climate damage. Even in the case of climate-caused migration, it would advocate for sovereign cultural and political identities to be maintained.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54846"></a>
**Ensure all environmental protection and resource management legislation is in line with climate change policies**  
`POL54846` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, transport-and-infrastructure, climate-adaptation, emissions-reduction, land-and-resources, urban-planning-and-resource-management

- **Why:** This party says that climate-exacerbated pressures are threatening ecosystems and biodiversity. If ecosystems fail, the ability to hold greenhouse gases and adapt to climate change will also fail.
- **How:** This party would ensure that all environmental protection and resource management legislation integrates climate change goals. The legislation must also work to protect and restore the resilience of biodiversity and ecosystems and their ability to adapt to climate change.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54848"></a>
**Ensure marginalised groups are proactively protected from climate change**  
`POL54848` · topics: environment, community-and-inclusion, climate-adaptation, young-people, disability

- **Why:** This party says that both climate change and the necessary actions needed to mitigate and adapt to its negative consequences will worsen socioeconomic inequalities. Initiatives need to be put in place to protect already marginalised groups.
- **How:** This party would proactively identify those disproportionately impacted by climate change, such as people living in remote communities or people living with a disability, and support them to develop and implement inclusive plans to avoid harm. It would also strengthen human and labour rights to reduce inequitable vulnerabilities to the impacts of climate change and provide resources to grassroots climate change organisations focusing on community empowerment.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54849"></a>
**Support people whose work has been impacted by climate change responses**  
`POL54849` · topics: environment, incomes-and-employment, climate-adaptation, jobs-and-employment

- **Why:** This party says that both climate change and the necessary actions needed to mitigate and adapt to its negative consequences threaten to worsen socioeconomic inequalities. In moving towards a carbon-negative economy, people who work in high-emission industries may lose their jobs.
- **How:** This party would fund free vocational training and redeployment opportunities for those in high-emission industries to transition to climate-safe work. It would also provide access to redundancy compensation for people working in sectors that are adversely affected by climate change mitigation and adaptation actions.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54851"></a>
**Support effective climate adaption decision making and action**  
`POL54851` · topics: environment, law-justice-and-government, climate-adaptation, local-government

- **Why:** This party says that climate change adaptation measures must be adopted by all of New Zealand, meaning everyone needs to work together from the government down to the individual.
- **How:** This party would increase resourcing and accountability for climate adaptation within local government, including providing additional resources to those lease resourced to take climate action. It would also prioritise long-term, nature-based solutions aligned with tikanga Māori and culturally appropriate values and ensure every student knows how to respond and stay hopeful in the face of climate change. This party would also expand the role of the Natural Hazards Commission to include cover for climate-related weather events and ensure it plays a key role in managing community-led adaptation and retreat.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction)); ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation)); ['Climate Change Policy', undated (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/web-climate-change-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/climate_change_policy)); ['Climate Change Policy in Full ' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 31 August 2026)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54856"></a>
**Ensure climate adaptation planning affirms self-governance**  
`POL54856` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, climate-adaptation, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that all New Zealanders need to prepare for the impacts of climate change. In line with Te Tiriti o Waitangi, hapū, iwi and the Māori community must be empowered to develop and implement their own climate change response plans.
- **How:** This party would ensure climate adaptation planning affirms tino rangatiratanga and upholds the mana motuhake of whānau, hapū and iwi over whenua, wai and taonga by appropriately funding a Māori Climate Resilience Fund.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation))

<a id="pol54858"></a>
**Establish a fund for implementing climate adaptation and resilience measures**  
`POL54858` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, law-justice-and-government, climate-adaptation, wellbeing-and-services, local-government

- **Why:** This party says that as the impacts of climate change are felt more widely with more intense and frequent storms, community groups are often on the front lines of the emergency and clean-up response. Central and local governments must provide support for their communities to proactively plan and adapt.
- **How:** This party would establish a dedicated fund for central and local government to implement climate adaptation and resilience measures, including funding for partnerships with tangata whenua and community organisations.  It would also develop and implement strategies to support the wellbeing of people displaced by climate change, including supporting Māori communities to deal with detachment and loss of identity from places of historical and customary significance.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Resilience and Adaptation', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_climate_action_resilience_adaptation))

<a id="pol54859"></a>
**Increase the reporting and transparency aspects of the Zero Carbon Act**  
`POL54859` · topics: environment, climate-adaptation, emissions-reduction

- **Why:** This party says that by urgently phasing out fossil fuels, NZ can ease the cost of living pressures and reduce our country's vulnerability to global price shocks. To do this, NZ needs to be led by strong domestic targets that stop emissions at the source and make polluting corporations pay.
- **How:** This party would require the government Budget process to be consistent with limiting global warming to 1.5 degrees, gross emissions-reduction targets, and international commitments.  It would also improve public transparency on the costs of inaction by including the fiscal impact of the Nationally Determined Contribution obligation in the financial statements.
- **Full text:** ['Climate Action: Emissions Reduction', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2020_climate_action_emissions_reduction))

<a id="pol54909"></a>
**Reduce agricultural emissions in New Zealand**  
`POL54909` · topics: environment, economy, emissions-reduction, agriculture, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that the agriculture industry in New Zealand should be working to positively contribute to clean air and a safe climate. To do this, all agricultural businesses must work together to achieve a significant cut in gross emissions.
- **How:** This party would adjust stocking rates according to the holding capacity of the land, protect existing wetlands and encourage wetland restoration, phase out the use of fossil fuels for agricultural processes, and phase out synthetic nitrogen fertilisers. It would also require the agriculture sector to provide data that enables greenhouse gas emissions to be measured and incentivise low greenhouse gas agricultural initiatives.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54931"></a>
**Make nature-based solutions the primary mechanism for hazard mitigation**  
`POL54931` · topics: environment, conservation, climate-adaptation

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand faces certain and increasing threats of many kinds, both natural and man-made.  Looking after the natural world is critical for minimising the risks and impacts of emergencies.
- **How:** This party would set nature-based hazard mitigation as the default approach to hazard mitigation, but only if that approach does not negatively impact people and communities. The emergency response approach should be flexible and outcome- and needs-oriented, preserving local ecosystems, tikanga, and communities. To ensure this, the party would standardise Fire and Emergency NZ's chemical, biological, radioactive and nuclear response procedures and prepare a rapid, coordinated response plan covering marine emergencies and the impacts of land-based emergencies on the marine environment.
- **Full text:** ['Emergency Management Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/emergency-management-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64640/attachments/original/1778624600/Policy-Greens_Emergency_Management_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54934"></a>
**Prioritise making critical utilities resilient in preparation for future emergencies**  
`POL54934` · topics: environment, law-justice-and-government, climate-adaptation, local-government

- **Why:** This party says that emergency management should prioritise basic needs and restoring essential services. Emergency financial support systems should maintain basic living standards and avoid exacerbating existing inequities.
- **How:** This party would set minimum standards for regional resilience in critical utilities, including the electrical grid and satellite communications and provide resources to remote communities so they can establish local alternatives to these centralised critical systems. It would also provide easily accessible basic resources as needed, including food, shelter, repairs, and clothing,  regardless of people’s immediate ability to pay.
- **Full text:** ['Emergency Management Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/emergency-management-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64640/attachments/original/1778624600/Policy-Greens_Emergency_Management_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54952"></a>
**Increase overall public conservation funding**  
`POL54952` · topics: environment, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand's flora and fauna are central to our national identity and way of life. Yet they are often threatened by economic goals, as well as introduced predators. This party says that conservation decisions should be made for the public good, not in the interests of private profiteers.
- **How:** This party would increase overall public conservation funding, including increasing funding and support for the Predator Free 2050 goal.
- **Full text:** ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol55012"></a>
**Form a strategy for a zero-waste economy**  
`POL55012` · topics: environment, economy, te-ao-maori, waste-management, economic-development-and-industry, conservation, land-and-resources

- **Why:** This party says that all our activities should aim to eliminate waste, circulate resources and regenerate nature. It believes in transitioning to a zero-waste economy, where single-use plastics are largely eliminated and a circular product stewardship system, from design to disposal, is in place across the economy.
- **How:** This party would work towards phasing out landfills and resource zero-waste strategies and legislation. It would develop and resource a national resource recovery network. It would promote moderate consumption and ban most single-use plastics. It would lead international efforts to clean up waste from the Pacific Ocean.
- **Full text:** ['Waste and Hazardous substances policy', undated (accessed 17 September 2026)](documents/web-waste-and-hazardous-substances-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/waste_and_hazardous_substances_policy)); ['Waste and Hazardous Substances Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 17 December 2025)](documents/waste-hazardous-substances-policy-2022-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65046/attachments/original/1778624620/Policy-Greens_Waste_Hazardous_Substances_Policy_2022-2025.pdf)); ['Zero Waste', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-zero-waste.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_zero_waste))

<a id="pol55013"></a>
**Ban single-use plastics and require plastics or rare earth materials to be recycled**  
`POL55013` · topics: environment, te-ao-maori, waste-management, land-and-resources, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that NZ needs to transition to a zero-waste economy, where single use plastics are largely eliminated, wasteful consumption is ended, and a circular product stewardship is in place across the economy.
- **How:** This party would require all products using plastics and rare earth metals to be readily recycled. Most single-use plastic products and non-recyclable and non-home-compostable packaging would be banned, with necessary exceptions.
- **Full text:** ['Waste and Hazardous substances policy', undated (accessed 17 September 2026)](documents/web-waste-and-hazardous-substances-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/waste_and_hazardous_substances_policy)); ['Waste and Hazardous Substances Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 17 December 2025)](documents/waste-hazardous-substances-policy-2022-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65046/attachments/original/1778624620/Policy-Greens_Waste_Hazardous_Substances_Policy_2022-2025.pdf)); ['Zero Waste', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-zero-waste.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_zero_waste))

<a id="pol55095"></a>
**Repeal the Fast-track Approvals Act** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55095` · topics: te-ao-maori, economy, environment, land-and-resources, oil-gas-and-mining, fresh-water-and-pollution, key-policies-environment

- **Why:** This party says that a healthy environment is important for healthy communities. It says mining projects approved by the fast-track act are destructive to wild environments.
- **How:** This party would repeal the Fast-track approvals act and revoke consents for new coal, gold or seabed mining projects.
- **Full text:** ['Environmental Protection', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-environmental-protection.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_environmental_protection))

<a id="pol55199"></a>
**Protect 30% of the Hauraki Gulf by 2030**  
`POL55199` · topics: environment, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that everyone loves the Hauraki Gulf and we are supposed to protect and look after the things we love. This party wants to restore the marine ecosystem of the Gulf and ensure it is flourishing for generations to come.
- **How:** This party would commit to the Hauraki Gulf Forum's goal of protecting 30% of the Gulf by 2030, as part of its wider plan to protect at least 30% of New Zealand's oceans by reforming marine protected area legislation. It would also remove the ring-net fishing exemption from the Gulf's high protection areas immediately, through a Bill released in early October. Iwi and hapū would be supported to lead ocean protection in the Gulf. As part of its wider conservation policy, the party would also enable the Gulf’s fisheries to be managed according to the health of the entire ecosystem by reviewing the Quota Management System.
- **Full text:** ['Greens announce plans to protect the Hauraki Gulf', 6 October 2026 (accessed 06 October 2026)](documents/scoop-greens-announce-plans-to-protect-the-hauraki-gulf.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_announce_plans_to_protect_the_hauraki_gulf))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Climate Action: Emissions Reduction**: *Bold action and solutions that hold polluters to account* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2020-climate-action-emissions-reduction.md))

1. Strengthen the Zero Carbon Act by requiring the government Budget process to be consistent with limiting global warming to 1.5 degrees, gross emissions reduction targets and international commitments.
2. Empower the Climate Change Commission to directly set unit supply in the Emissions Trading Scheme, in line with emissions budgets. Align the government Budget process with the National Adaptation Plan and Emissions Reduction Plans.
3. Introduce a price on agricultural emissions to ensure methane reduction targets are met, with financial and technical support for on-farm emissions reduction.
4. Reform the Emissions Trading Scheme (ETS) to restore credibility to the market by phasing out the free allocation of units, removing forestry offsetting so the system functions as a genuine ‘cap-and-trade’ system, and redirecting funding from the reformed ETS to an improved Government Investment in Decarbonising Industry Fund.
5. Ensure Aotearoa meets our Paris Agreement commitments through strong domestic action and, if necessary, environmentally and socially responsible initiatives overseas.
6. Improve public transparency on the costs of inaction by including the fiscal impact of the Nationally Determined Contribution obligation in the financial statements.

**Climate Action: Resilience and Adaption**: *Keeping Aotearoa safe and prepared* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-climate-action-resilience-adaptation.md))

1. Ensure climate adaptation planning affirms tino rangatiratanga and upholds the mana motuhake of whānau, hapū and iwi over whenua, wai and taonga by appropriately funding a Māori Climate Resilience Fund.
2. Expand the role of the Natural Hazards Commission to include cover for climate-related weather events and ensure it plays a key role in managing community-led adaptation and retreat.
3. Prioritise long-term, nature-based solutions over short-term engineering solutions, and increase funding for hapū, iwi and community organisations to deliver these.
4. Ensure planning frameworks give effect to the need for climate adaptation in the approach to land and water use, together with supporting ecosystem restoration and conservation.
5. Establish a dedicated fund for central and local government to implement climate adaptation and resilience measures, including funding for partnerships with tangata whenua and community organisations.
6. Scale up support for, and work with, neighbouring countries in the Pacific to adapt to climate change, and advocate for an international agreement on the rights of people displaced by climate change.

**Conservation and Biodiversity**: *Protecting and restoring plants, animals and ecosystems.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md))

1. Reform conservation and wildlife protection laws to give effect to Te Tiriti, elevate Indigenous-led land stewardship and ensure the conservation management system prioritises halting and reversing nature loss in Aotearoa.
2. Ban new mining on conservation land, including stewardship land.
3. Expand funding for Jobs for Nature – Mahi mō te Taiao to protect our biodiversity and create thousands of regional jobs.
4. Protect all remaining natural wetlands in legislation, including coastal wetlands and estuaries, and implement a plan to restore at least 30% of degraded wetland area, prioritising areas most important for biodiversity recovery and community resilience.
5. Fund Action for Nature, the implementation plan for the biodiversity strategy, and implement national guidance to protect indigenous biodiversity, including the protection of taonga species.
6. Increase overall public conservation funding, including increasing funding and support for the Predator Free 2050 goal.

**Energy**: *Powering Aotearoa with affordable renewable energy.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md))

1. Put public wellbeing outcomes first in legislation to guarantee the power system works for people, not profits.
2. Establish a renewable energy infrastructure agency to ensure security of supply and lower prices for households, businesses and industry.
3. Increase resilience by enabling greater distributed solar and batteries for households, marae and suitable public buildings, and supporting community-led energy planning.
4. Ban new fossil fuel extraction, end subsidies for fossil fuels and cancel the LNG terminal.
5. Establish a single energy regulator and a Ministry for Energy, focusing on affordable, secure and sustainable energy with equitable access and Te Tiriti-based frameworks that enable Māori and community involvement, ownership and leadership in energy projects.
6. Work with industry to decarbonise and encourage demand response and energy efficiency.

**Environmental Protection**: *Protecting and restoring nature.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-environmental-protection.md))

1. Implement strong evidence-based limits for ecosystem health, secure the role of Te Tiriti and affirm the role of tangata whenua as shared decision-makers and kaitiaki in the new resource management system.
2. Repeal the Fast-track Approvals Act and revoke consents for fast-track approvals for new coal, gold and seabed mining projects.
3. Restore a standalone Ministry for the Environment. Increase resources for the Environmental Protection Authority to identify risks to the environment and human health, and for compliance, monitoring and enforcement to ensure polluters are held accountable.
4. Update air quality standards to bring Aotearoa in line with international standards and to protect human health.
5. Work towards eradication of biosecurity incursions, specifically wilding pines, Caulerpa, and yellow-legged hornets.
6. Re-establish resources for the Community Environment Fund to support grassroots environmental education and action.

**Food Fibre and Forestry**: *A regenerative approach that puts people and nature first.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-food-fibre-and-forestry.md))

1. Increase support for farmers to transition to more sustainable forms of agricultural production through finance mechanisms such as low-interest loans and grants.
2. Back Aotearoa-grown natural fibres, including wool, by using them in the construction and refurbishment of schools and public buildings, and establishing a contestable fund for sustainable fibre manufacturing and innovation.
3. Ban palm kernel expeller and ensure all supplementary feed is sustainably sourced and does not strain the carrying capacity of the local environment.
4. Prevent land from being used beyond its capacities by implementing strong controls on clear-fell of plantation forestry, working to transition extremely erosion-prone land to indigenous forestry and establishing a National Soil Strategy that sets direction on soil use, policies and research.
5. Support the local wood processing sector to manufacture higher-value timber products from plantation forestry, such as cross-laminated timber for construction and turning slash into solid biofuels, retaining regional employment and development.
6. Work with Māori-led forestry organisations to support investment in forestry and restoration of forests on whenua Māori and recognise tangata whenua customary use rights in indigenous forestry according to their tīkanga.
7. Establish a biodiversity credits scheme that supports landowners to undertake restoration and protection of land as a voluntary revenue alternative to the Emissions Trading Scheme. As part of this, prioritise marginal whenua Māori where agreed by landowners.

**Freshwater**: *Healthy water for drinking, swimming and gathering kai.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-freshwater.md))

1. Implement strong, evidence-based national bottom lines for freshwater for both human health and ecosystem health to address pollution including lowering the nitrate limit for drinking water.
2. Reinstate Te Mana o Te Wai to prioritise the health of freshwater in decision-making and regulation and to give effect to Te Tiriti o Waitangi.
3. End all subsidies and public funding for big new irrigation and dam projects and review resource consents where water levels have dropped too far to support a healthy river.
4. Strengthen environmental performance rules for wastewater discharges to ensure lakes, rivers, beaches and coastal areas remain swimmable.
5. Ensure the drinking water regulator is funded and given the mandate for robust and transparent monitoring, reporting and enforcement in the protection of drinking water sources.
6. Support greater resilience from floods by increasing funding for adaptation and allowing for a ‘room for rivers’ approach and using natural systems like wetlands to protect communities.
7. Phase out synthetic nitrogen fertiliser and take action to reduce stocking rates on a catchment basis to improve the health of rivers and drinking water and reduce climate pollution.

**Oceans and Fisheries**: *Abundant oceans teeming with kaimoana.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-oceans-and-fisheries.md))

1. Protect at least 30% of New Zealand’s ocean territory by 2030 through reforming marine protected areas legislation to ensure it is fit for purpose and enables both tikanga-led tools consistent with Te Tiriti, and approaches to improve ocean health outcomes.
2. Phase out damaging fishing methods, starting by banning bottom trawling on seamounts, and banning set netting and dredging.
3. Ban seabed mining in the coastal marine area and the exclusive economic zone and maintain pressure for a moratorium on seabed mining in international waters.
4. Embed kaitiakitanga Māori in marine policy and operational management. Resource iwi and hapū to lead oceans protection and implement mātauranga- and tikanga-based projects for ocean conservation.
5. Reduce fisheries bycatch of protected species by ensuring cameras are rolled out on the entire fishing fleet and by setting fisheries-related mortality limits.
6. Ratify the Agreement on Marine Biological Diversity of Areas Beyond National Jurisdiction and support marine protection proposals in the high seas, Southern Ocean and throughout the Pacific.
7. Review the Quota Management System and move towards an ecosystem-based fisheries management model, in line with the Māori fisheries settlements.

**Welfare of Animals**: *Caring for all animals that call Aotearoa home.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-welfare-of-animals.md))

1. Create a new Commissioner for Animals to independently monitor and advocate for the rights of animals, and an independent ministry.
2. Resource the National Animal Welfare Advisory Committee to ensure all animal welfare codes genuinely comply with the Animal Welfare Act, and introduce stronger codes for farmed animals.
3. Phase out harmful activities including debeaking of hens, intensive winter grazing, factory farming, octopus farming, farrowing crates and rodeo.
4. Require that imported products meet the animal welfare standards of Aotearoa, including for cosmetics.
5. Introduce consistent, clear and enforceable standards for manufacturing claims about animal welfare and ingredients (such as ‘free range’, ‘cruelty free’ and ‘vegan’).
6. Retain the ban on live exports of farmed animals by sea and extend the ban to include live exports of farmed animals by air and wild-caught animals such as endangered tuna longfin eels.

**Zero Waste**: *Building a sustainable circular economy.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-zero-waste.md))

1. Establish strong product policy measures, including mandatory product stewardship, a beverage container return scheme, phase-outs of problematic products and comprehensive right-to-repair.
2. Direct revenue from the waste disposal levy into waste minimisation initiatives and stop diverting the local government portion to other purposes. Increase the levy to match international best practice and apply a consistent rate across all landfill classes.
3. Introduce a moratorium on waste-to-energy plants and explore energy generation from biogas associated with animal and plant wastes.
4. Build on proposed reforms of the Waste Minimisation Act to implement timely, binding, measurable and ambitious waste reduction targets. Ensure legislation gives effect to Te Tiriti o Waitangi. Introduce comprehensive duty-of-care systems for the waste and resource recovery sector covering all waste streams, including hazardous waste.
5. Make greater use of discarded soil. Divert soil from landfills, where safe and appropriate to do so.
6. Prioritise local, mana whenua and community-driven solutions to resource recovery, implementation of national zero-waste strategies, and organic waste reduction, diversion and processing.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **'Drink Swim Fish' freshwater and oceans policy**: A 4-page water policy (announced 6 July 2026) aimed at safe drinking water, swimmable rivers and healthier oceans: science-based national freshwater limits including lower nitrate limits, stronger wastewater-discharge rules, phasing out synthetic nitrogen fertiliser, phasing out bottom trawling and other destructive fishing, protecting at least 30% of oceans, and reviewing the fisheries quota system. Full text: [Drink Swim Fish — Fixing the rules for fresh water and oceans](documents/drink-swim-fish-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74309/attachments/original/1783476578/DSF_Policy_2026.pdf?1783476578))
- **'Power for all of us' energy policy: Kiwipower, Warmer Kiwi Homes expansion, community energy and zero-interest solar loans**: The 12-page energy policy (announced 20 July 2026). It proposes 'Kiwipower', a publicly owned company to contract and build electricity firming; an expanded Warmer Kiwi Homes insulation/heating programme; $200m for community-owned energy; a government-backed Clean Energy Loans Scheme (modelled as zero-interest, tied to the property) for rooftop solar and other home energy upgrades; and other electricity-market changes. It includes four-year cost tables and says spending would be funded by the proposed super-rich tax. Full text: [Power for all of us — Lower bills. Cleaner energy. Public ownership.](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens'_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079))
- **Establish Kiwipower, a publicly owned electricity firming company**: A 6-page explainer for the proposed publicly owned firming company 'Kiwipower': it would contract access to existing hydro/thermal firming, invest in new renewable firming and storage (geothermal, biomass, batteries, small pumped hydro, demand response) and sell firming at fair prices to independent retailers and large users. Costed at $412.6m opex and $568m capex over 2027/28-2030/31, to be established by the end of 2027. (Image-only PDF; text transcribed by hand.) Full text: [The Greens’ KiwiPower explainer](documents/kiwipower-explainer-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510143/The_Greens'_Kiwipower_explainer.pdf?1784510143))
- **Bill to remove ring-net fishing exemption from Hauraki Gulf high protection areas**: A 6-page draft member's bill (released 6 October 2026) amending the Hauraki Gulf / Tīkapa Moana Marine Protection Act 2025 to remove the exemption that allows ring-net fishing in high protection areas, and to allow further management actions, developed with iwi, where protection objectives are not being met. Full text: [Hauraki Gulf / Tīkapa Moana Marine Protection (Restoration of High Protection Areas) Amendment Bill (Green Party draft bill)](documents/hauraki-gulf-restoration-of-high-protection-areas-bill-draft.md) ([original](https://img.scoop.co.nz/media/pdfs/2610/Hauraki_Gulf___Tipaka_Moana_Marine_Protection.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Agriculture Policy**: Standing party policy on farming: support farmers to reduce environmental harm and emissions, uphold Te Tiriti in sector governance, fair prices for producers, better data, and treat farm animals as sentient. Full text: [Agriculture Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Animal Welfare Policy**: Standing party policy to align animal-welfare law with animals' needs (nutrition, environment, health, behaviour, mental state), strengthen oversight, reduce systemic exploitation of animals and recognise Māori kaitiaki relationships with animals. Full text: [Animal Welfare Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/animal-welfare-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64486/attachments/original/1778624593/Policy-Greens_Animal_Welfare_2025-2026.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy**: Standing party policy on conservation: protect threatened indigenous species from pests, disease and human activity, take a holistic approach to restoring ecosystems, and support tangata whenua as kaitiaki. Full text: [Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Climate Change Policy**: Standing party climate policy: Te Tiriti-based solutions, energy efficiency and democratisation, low-carbon transport, a rapid fossil-fuel phase-out, more plant-based and resilient farming, an economy that does not rely on growth, and adaptation planning that prioritises the most vulnerable. Full text: [Climate Change Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/climate-change-policy-2022-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/63498/attachments/original/1778713043/Policy-Greens_Climate_Change_Policy_2022-2026.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Emergency Management Policy**: Standing party policy on civil defence: resource agencies and communities to prepare for and respond to emergencies, strengthen infrastructure resilience, use local and Māori knowledge, prioritise vulnerable people and fund managed retreat from high-risk areas. Full text: [Emergency Management Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/emergency-management-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64640/attachments/original/1778624600/Policy-Greens_Emergency_Management_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Energy Policy**: Standing party energy policy: reform electricity markets, support community energy ownership, legislate fossil-fuel reductions, electrify transport and heat, and expand efficiency and renewable supply. Full text: [Energy Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/energy-policy-2022-2025.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64654/attachments/original/1778624600/Policy-Greens_Energy_Policy_2022-2025.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Food Policy**: Standing party policy on the right to safe, affordable food, including Māori food practices, and a national food-system strategy for local resilience, less waste and lower emissions. - **Standing policy: Forestry Policy**: Standing party forestry policy: long-term carbon storage and biodiversity, community-protecting forestry rules, value-added wood processing and kaupapa Māori forestry governance.
