## Transport and infrastructure

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54758"></a>
**Legalise plug-in solar and establish a renters’ right to solar**  
`POL54758` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, housing, energy, renting

- **Why:** This party says that says that a warm, dry home should be possible for everyone. This policy would help lower power bills for renters by improving access to affordable, renewable solar power. Plug-in solar systems can save up to $350 a year on people's power bills. It is also smaller and cheaper to install than rooftop solar.
- **How:** This party would urgently progress regulation to legalise plug-in solar and establish a renters' right to solar in law. It would amend tenancy and body corporate law to allow this to happen.
- **Full text:** ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy)); ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies))

<a id="pol54760"></a>
**Expand Warmer Kiwi Homes to provide more funding for house insulation**  
`POL54760` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, housing, incomes-and-employment, energy, housing-quality, prices

- **Why:** This party says that the Warmer Kiwi Homes programme has already proven its worth, helping hundreds of thousands of households insulate and heat their homes. Expanding the programme would support more low-income people to make their homes warm, dry and energy efficient.
- **How:** This party would expand the capacity of the government's Warmer Kiwi Homes programme to include the replacement of gas heating and gas stoves, installing ventilation and hot water heat pumps at an 80 subsidy. The capacity would expand to provide for 50,000 upgrades over four years.  This party estimates that this policy would cost $969.8 million over four years.
- **Full text:** ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy)); ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies))

<a id="pol54761"></a>
**Reform pricing so people are paid fairly for excess solar power exported to the grid**  
`POL54761` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, environment, incomes-and-employment, energy, emissions-reduction, prices

- **Why:** This party says that households and businesses with rooftop solar should receive a fair price for the electricity they export back to the grid.
- **How:** This party would regulate energy pricing to ensure that households and businesses with rooftop solar receive a fair price for the electricity they export back to the grid.
- **Full text:** ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies))

<a id="pol54763"></a>
**Invest $200m in community energy projects across the country**  
`POL54763` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, environment, economy, energy, emissions-reduction, regional-development, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says that restoring public ownership of energy projects would make power more affordable and grow community resilience. It would ensure clean-energy benefits go back into the community rather than to shareholders. Building renewable energy close to where the demand is can cut costs.
- **How:** This party would fund this policy by reallocating subsidies for fossil fuel companies to community energy projects. Locally-led projects such as schools, marae, libraries, recreation centres, community energy groups, iwi/Māori, local government and other locally governed organisations would be eligible for funding. Small-scale wind, solar and geothermal projects that deliver benefits to the community would be included.  This party estimates that this policy would cost $200m over 4 years.
- **Full text:** ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies))

<a id="pol54765"></a>
**Invest $80 million into renewable energy for Māori housing**  
`POL54765` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, housing, te-ao-maori, economy, energy, social-and-public-housing, te-reo-maori-and-culture, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says that Māori are two to three times more likely to experience energy hardship compared to the general population. This policy would ensure Māori can access clean energy, resulting in cheaper power bills that also uphold kaitiakitanga, rangatiratanga and tikanga.
- **How:** This party would invest in projects ranging from solar on kaumātua homes and papakāinga (communal housing built by families on ancestral Māori land) to geothermal energy to local distribution solutions that help manage energy on Māori land. This party estimates that this policy would cost $80 million over four years.
- **Full text:** ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies))

<a id="pol54766"></a>
**Establish a publicly-owned Crown entity to invest in renewable generation.**  
`POL54766` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, economy, energy, economic-development-and-industry

- **Why:** This party says that NZ needs a resilient energy system to provide backup renewable power that keeps electricity affordable when hydro lakes are low and wind power decreases. Gas historically fills this gap, but gas is being exhausted more quickly than expected.
- **How:** This party would create Kiwipower, a new publicly owned Crown entity that invests in renewable energy generation. Its main focus would be on renewable firming capacity such as geothermal, batteries, demand response, biomass and pumped hydro, to ensure energy demands can be met at all times. Kiwipower would contract firming capacity and make that capacity available to independent generators, retailers and energy users at affordable and transparent prices. Large power companies would be required to provide a portion of their firming capacity to Kiwipower. The entity would be funded by a tax on the wealthy, at an estimated cost $980m over four years.
- **Full text:** ['Power for all of us, Greens Full Power Policy' (PDF), undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/power-for-all-of-us-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/74612/attachments/original/1784510079/The_Greens%27_Full_Power_Policy.pdf?1784510079)); ['Policy Announcement - Power for all of us', undated (accessed 24 August 2026)](documents/web-power-for-all-of-us.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/power_for_all_of_us)); ['Energy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-energy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_energy)); ['Green Party launches plan to cut power bills and take power back from the big energy companies', 20 July 2026 (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-plan-to-cut-power-bills-and-take-power-back-from-the-big-energy-compa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_plan_to_cut_power_bills_and_take_power_back_from_the_big_energy_companies))

<a id="pol55029"></a>
**Introduce free public transport fares for young people, the disabled and CSC card holders** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55029` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, incomes-and-employment, community-and-inclusion, public-transport-cycling-and-walking, rail-transport, prices, key-policies-transport-and-infrastructure, young-people, disability

- **Why:** This party says our rail system has fallen into disarray, and our spread-out communities are poorly served by bus networks. Young people, non-car owners, families and minority groups all benefit from better transport options, as does the environment.
- **How:** This party would extend the Community Connect programme to provide free fares for young people, disabled and community service card holders. It would also work towards more bike lanes, and e-bike sharing.
- **Full text:** ['Transport', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-transport.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_transport))

<a id="pol55030"></a>
**Build mass rapid transit in three major cities and invest in better rail and bus links** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55030` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, economy, public-transport-cycling-and-walking, rail-transport, infrastructure-funding-and-planning, urban-planning-and-resource-management, key-policies-transport-and-infrastructure, regional-development

- **Why:** This party says that our rail system has fallen into disarray, and our communities are poorly served by our bus networks, particularly on the outskirts of towns. Young people, non-car owners, families and minority groups all benefit from better transport options, as does the environment.
- **How:** This party would build mass rapid transit in Auckland, Wellington and Christchurch and invest in a national passenger and freight rapid rail system to connect major cities and regions better. It would also fund affordable inter-regional and rural bus services and beef up bus lanes in cities.
- **Full text:** ['Transport', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-transport.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_transport))

<a id="pol55031"></a>
**Phase out fossil-fuel vehicle imports and incentivise electric vehicles**  
`POL55031` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, cars-and-roads, public-transport-cycling-and-walking

- **Why:** This party says that NZ's public transport system has left many people with no option but to rely on cars to get around, adding to congestion and making people vulnerable to oil price shocks. Young people, non-car owners, families and minority groups particularly benefit from better transport options, as does the environment.
- **How:** This party would phase out the importation of fossil-fuel vehicles, and build more electric vehicle charging stations, promote EV charging stations, car-sharing and introduce incentives for EV uptake.
- **Full text:** ['Transport', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-transport.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_transport))

<a id="pol55195"></a>
**Make public transport free for children and other specified people and cap fares at $2 for adults**  
`POL55195` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, public-transport-cycling-and-walking, rail-transport

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealanders deserve a fast, convenient and affordable transport system. It believes that years of underinvestment in public transport have left our cities harder to get around without a car. However, it says our cities have the potential to be accessible, vibrant and connected for locals and visitors. This will make it easier and more affordable for people to get where they need to go using cleaner modes of transport, also helping to cut greenhouse gas emissions.
- **How:** This party would make bus and train fares free for children, teenagers, students, apprentices, Community Service Card holders, and Total Mobility Users, and cap bus and train fares at $2 for adults.
- **Full text:** ['Green Party launches the transport plan to combat fossil fuel crisis', 4 October 2026 (accessed 05 October 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-the-transport-plan-to-combat-fossil-fuel-crisis.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_the_transport_plan_to_combat_fossil_fuel_crisis))

<a id="pol55196"></a>
**Create improved public transport services between regions**  
`POL55196` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, public-transport-cycling-and-walking

- **Why:** This party says that the fuel crisis is costing ordinary people every time they travel. We need transport alternatives that are more affordable, fast, frequent, and reliable. It says communities are currently designed to prioritise working best for cars, when they should be designed to work best for people.
- **How:** This party would fund up to 150 new regional bus services to supplement existing services, extend the Te Huia train to Tauranga with track and tunnel improvements and pilot an overnight passenger train service between Wellington and Auckland. It would also bring back the Southerner train connection between Christchurch and Dunedin.
- **Full text:** ['Green Party launches the transport plan to combat fossil fuel crisis', 4 October 2026 (accessed 05 October 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-the-transport-plan-to-combat-fossil-fuel-crisis.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_the_transport_plan_to_combat_fossil_fuel_crisis)); ['Fast, frequent, affordable public transport for all of us', undated (accessed 05 October 2026)](documents/web-connect-aotearoa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/connect_aotearoa))

<a id="pol55197"></a>
**Improve public transport within cities**  
`POL55197` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, public-transport-cycling-and-walking

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealanders spend too much time and money sitting in traffic, filling up the car, and waiting for trains and buses. This party says there needs to be more affordable and accessible alternatives to driving a car.
- **How:** This party would deliver immediate improvements to bus routes along the planned Mass Rapid Transit routes in Auckland, Wellington and Christchurch and plan and deliver light rail or bus rapid transit in line with the Infrastructure Commission’s preferred option. The party would also fund Wellington’s critical rail renewal work needed to fix broken-down track assets and infrastructure. It would lastly work with Auckland Transport to fund and deliver level crossing removals to allow for more frequent trains, fewer delays, and better safety outcomes.
- **Full text:** ['Green Party launches the transport plan to combat fossil fuel crisis', 4 October 2026 (accessed 05 October 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-the-transport-plan-to-combat-fossil-fuel-crisis.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_the_transport_plan_to_combat_fossil_fuel_crisis)); ['Fast, frequent, affordable public transport for all of us', undated (accessed 05 October 2026)](documents/web-connect-aotearoa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/connect_aotearoa))

<a id="pol55198"></a>
**Fund public transport for the regions**  
`POL55198` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, economy, public-transport-cycling-and-walking, regional-development

- **Why:** This party says that regions are often left behind when it comes to public transport infrastructure upgrades, with the focus on major cities. It says that communities living in smaller towns also deserve the opportunity to access affordable and accessible public transport.
- **How:** This party would fund the planned bus improvements in smaller cities and towns in the Waikato, Bay of Plenty, Taranaki, Horowhenua, and Otago. It would also work to revitalise town and city centres and increase safe walking and biking infrastructure.
- **Full text:** ['Green Party launches the transport plan to combat fossil fuel crisis', 4 October 2026 (accessed 05 October 2026)](documents/web-green-party-launches-the-transport-plan-to-combat-fossil-fuel-crisis.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_the_transport_plan_to_combat_fossil_fuel_crisis)); ['Fast, frequent, affordable public transport for all of us', undated (accessed 05 October 2026)](documents/web-connect-aotearoa.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/connect_aotearoa))

<a id="pol55200"></a>
**Fund and deliver level crossing removals in Auckland**  
`POL55200` · topics: transport-and-infrastructure, infrastructure-funding-and-planning, public-transport-cycling-and-walking

- **Why:** This party says that over the last 10 years, there have been almost 70 crashes and hundreds of near-misses at level crossings across Auckland. Removing them will allow for better safety outcomes, more frequent trains and fewer delays.
- **How:** This party would work with the Auckland Council to identify the best options to fund and deliver level crossing removals.
- **Full text:** ['Greens Commit To Funding And Delivering Level Crossing Removals', 5 October 2026 (accessed 06 October 2026)](documents/scoop-greens-commit-to-funding-and-delivering-level-crossing-removals.md) ([original](https://www.scoop.co.nz/stories/PA2610/S00037/greens-commit-to-funding-and-delivering-level-crossing-removals.htm))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Transport**: *Easy, sustainable ways to get around.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-transport.md))

1. Transform public transport networks, including building mass rapid transit in Auckland, Wellington and Christchurch, and providing comprehensive bus lanes in all cities.
2. Invest in nationwide rapid rail for passengers and freight to connect regions and major cities and contribute to economic development and decarbonisation.
3. Introduce targeted subsidies for e-cargo bikes. Introduce e-bike sharing schemes in community hubs, to allow more families to save on transport costs.
4. Fund affordable, reliable, interregional rural bus services, and on-demand public transport, including by-Māori for-Māori public transport to serve kōhanga reo and rural schools.
5. Extend Community Connect to provide free fares for students and apprentices, community service card holders, everyone under 18, and all Total Mobility card holders.
6. Enable the provision of transport services publicly instead of for profit by replacing the Public Transport Operating Model.
7. Create safe walking and biking routes to all community hubs including schools through building more bike lanes and safe crossings, and lowering speed limits near schools.
8. Implement a phase-out date for the import of fossil-fuel powered vehicles, build more EV (electric vehicle) charging stations, remove regulatory barriers to EV car-sharing and re-introduce incentives for EV uptake.

Full text: [Green Party launches the transport plan to combat fossil fuel crisis](documents/web-green-party-launches-the-transport-plan-to-combat-fossil-fuel-crisis.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_launches_the_transport_plan_to_combat_fossil_fuel_crisis))

## Te ao Māori

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54767"></a>
**Create a self-governance trust to restore and sustain marae**  
`POL54767` · topics: te-ao-maori, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, wellbeing-and-services

- **Why:** This party says that marae are a key part of New Zealand's national identity for Māori and non-Māori. They are a central hub during states of emergency, serving and connecting communities. Despite this significance, marae across NZ are ageing with failing infrastructure and need long-term financial support to be sustained.
- **How:** This party would create Te Rangatiratanga Trust, which provides immediate support and long-term funding certainty for marae, owned and operated by hapū and iwi. The Crown would provide $2.44bn to establish the trust and two funds: Te Rākau Whakarauora Restoration Fund, which would provide $400m over four years for urgent needs, and Te Tōtara Haemata Intergenerational Fund, to protect and grow capital for future generations. The latter would be funded by returns from a $2bn diversified portfolio. Trust funding decisions would be made independently of ministers and successive governments.
- **Full text:** ['Policy Annoucement - Te Waonui a Tāne', undated (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/web-te-waonui-a-taane.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/te_waonui_a_taane)); ['Te Waonui a Tāne Thriving Marae for Thriving Communities' (PDF), undated (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/te-waonui-a-tane-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/81707/attachments/original/1786238613/TWT_policy_2026_ONLINE.pdf?1786238613)); ['Green Party announce marae funding policy Te Waonui a Tāne', 9 August 2026 (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-announce-marae-funding-policy-te-waonui-a-taane.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_announce_marae_funding_policy_te_waonui_a_taane))

<a id="pol54930"></a>
**Uphold the leadership of Māori during emergency management**  
`POL54930` · topics: te-ao-maori, environment, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, climate-adaptation

- **Why:** This party says that the government has a responsibility to protect Māori interests, work in partnership with Māori, and respect Māori autonomy in disasters.  Decisions made during emergencies should also respect local knowledge.
- **How:** This party would guarantee Māori representation at all levels of emergency management planning. It would formalise the role of iwi and hapū Māori in risk reduction, adaptation and recovery. It would also invest in the capacity and capability of iwi and hapū to respond in emergencies, and resource rural marae that want to be autonomous hubs for emergency preparedness and response.
- **Full text:** ['Emergency Management Policy', undated (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/web-emergency-management-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/emergency_management_policy)); ['Emergency Management Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/emergency-management-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64640/attachments/original/1778624600/Policy-Greens_Emergency_Management_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Te Waonui a Tāne Thriving Marae for Thriving Communities' (PDF), undated (accessed 27 August 2026)](documents/te-waonui-a-tane-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/81707/attachments/original/1786238613/TWT_policy_2026_ONLINE.pdf?1786238613)); ['Green Party announce marae funding policy Te Waonui a Tāne', 9 August 2026 (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/web-green-party-announce-marae-funding-policy-te-waonui-a-taane.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/green_party_announce_marae_funding_policy_te_waonui_a_taane)); ['2026 Māori Manifesto' (PDF), undated (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/maori-manifesto-2026-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1785798775/Ma%CC%84ori_Manifesto_Web_Version.pdf?1785798775))

<a id="pol54936"></a>
**Support access to food security and traditional food practices**  
`POL54936` · topics: te-ao-maori, economy, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, wellbeing-and-services, agriculture

- **Why:** This party says food security and sovereignty are essential for the health, wellbeing, and resilience of communities. It believes all consumers should have agency over their food and be able to make informed choices. There should also be a wide range of options close to where people live so they can choose nourishing food that meets their cultural needs.
- **How:** This party would support tangata whenua-led food production, distribution, and decision-making processes It would protect and promote foods and practices that reflect mana whenua (the people of the land). This party would work to ensure consumers have access to clear, accurate information about the nutritional, environmental, and ethical impacts of food choices. It would revise relevant legislation to protect the human right to be free from hunger and malnutrition.
- **Full text:** ['Food, Fibre and Forestry', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-food-fibre-and-forestry.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_food_fibre_and_forestry)); ['Food Policy', undated (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/web-food-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/food_policy)); ['Food Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/food-policy-2024-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64668/attachments/original/1779663153/Policy-Greens_Food_Policy_2024-2026.pdf?1779663153))

<a id="pol54979"></a>
**Provide $200m towards te reo Māori initiatives** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54979` · topics: te-ao-maori, education, te-reo-maori-and-culture, schools, key-policies-te-ao-maori

- **Why:** This party says that te reo Māori is a national treasure and initiatives that help it thrive should be supported so future generations can learn and enjoy the language. Supporting te reo Māori also supports whānau, hapū and iwi to be connected to their culture.
- **How:** This party would fund an additional $50m towards te reo Māori initiatives each year. This would be made up of: $30m a year to programmes that provide continuous education and professional development for teachers in te reo Māori, and $20m a year towards increased funding for Te Taura Whiri and Te Mātāwai.
- **Full text:** ['Ake, Ake, Ake: Green Party commits $200m to the future of te reo Māori', 14 September 2026 (accessed 14 September 2026)](documents/web-ake-ake-ake-green-party-commits-200m-to-the-future-of-te-reo-maori.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/ake_ake_ake_green_party_commits_200m_to_the_future_of_te_reo_maori)); ['Māori Centred', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-maori-centred.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_maori_centred)); ['Education', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-education.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_education)); ['Te Tiriti o Waitangi', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-te-tiriti-o-waitangi.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_te_tiriti_o_waitangi)); ['2026 Māori Manifesto' (PDF), undated (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/maori-manifesto-2026-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1785798775/Ma%CC%84ori_Manifesto_Web_Version.pdf?1785798775))

<a id="pol54995"></a>
**Create a disability rights law based on Te Tiriti o Waitangi**  
`POL54995` · topics: te-ao-maori, community-and-inclusion, health, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, disability, public-health

- **Why:** This party says that disabled people are often denied the same rights as others. They can also be excluded from places and activities that non-disabled people take for granted. These barriers are often worse for tāngata whaikaha Māori.
- **How:** This party would introduce a disability rights law based on Te Tiriti with enforceable accessibility standards. Policy-makers would need to collaboratively design laws, policies, and systems in ways that work for disabled people.
- **Full text:** ['Disability', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_disability)); ['Disability Policy', 2 March 2026 (accessed 16 September 2026)](documents/unretrievable-disability-policy-full.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/disability_policy_full))

<a id="pol55018"></a>
**Improve training in te reo Māori and Te Tiriti o Waitangi for all public servants**  
`POL55018` · topics: te-ao-maori, law-justice-and-government, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, public-sector, wellbeing-and-services

- **Why:** This party says that public services are investments in our shared future. Capable public servants need a strong grasp of Māori language, culture, and Te Tiriti o Waitangi as NZ's founding document in order to deliver services effectively.
- **How:** This party would improve training in Te Tiriti and te reo Māori for all public servants, so that results across agencies are more consistent.
- **Full text:** ['Public Services', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-public-services.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_public_services))

<a id="pol55022"></a>
**Fund community organisations and marae to fulfil the needs of their communities and regions**  
`POL55022` · topics: te-ao-maori, law-justice-and-government, wellbeing-and-services, public-sector

- **Why:** This party says that NZ has enough to invest in services for everyone, and community organisations, marae, iwi and hapū fulfil important roles in responding to the needs of their communities. These organisations should receive more money to meet the needs of their communities.
- **How:** This party would fund Māori organisations, marae, iwi and hapū for community and environmental work. It would also provide secure and flexible funding arrangements to the community sector to recognise the importance of its work and ensuring community work and remain independent.
- **Full text:** ['Public Services', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-public-services.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_public_services))

<a id="pol55024"></a>
**Allow people with Māori whakapapa to become New Zealand citizens no matter where they are born** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55024` · topics: migration, te-ao-maori, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, key-policies-te-ao-maori

- **Why:** This party says that increasing numbers of New Zealanders are moving or living overseas, and the government represents these people too. New Zealanders living overseas deserve to connect to Aotearoa, and tangata whenua should always be able to return to their turangawaewae by being New Zealand citizens, regardless of where they were born.
- **How:** This party would allow all people with Māori whakapapa to become New Zealand citizens, even if they have been born in another country.
- **Full text:** ['Overseas New Zealanders', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-overseas-new-zealanders.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_overseas_new_zealanders))

<a id="pol55039"></a>
**Uphold te Tiriti in the Treaty of Waitangi Act and across government decision-making**  
`POL55039` · topics: te-ao-maori, law-justice-and-government, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, land-and-resources, parliament-and-democracy

- **Why:** This party says that te Tiriti must be upheld as the fundamental document that the Treaty of Waitangi Act is based on. In its view, successive governments have consistently breached Te Tiriti, eroding the true partnership that was intended. Te Tiriti o Waitangi references have recently been weakened or stripped out of 19 acts of Parliament. And in government decision-making, iwi and hapū are often treated as advisors, rather than the partners which they are meant to be.
- **How:** This party would amend the Treaty of Waitangi Act so Te Tiriti is the authoritative text. It would implement genuine Te Tiriti decision-making models across government, and ensure iwi and hapu are resourced to participate as partners.
It would increase resourcing for the Waitangi Tribunal and give it the power to make binding recommendations for the return of Crown and private land. 
It would facilitate the return of whenua outside of Treaty Settlements that was wrongfully alienated from tangata whenua, including a possible right-of-first-refusal process at point of sale. It would also reform the Treaty Settlements process to enable fair negotiations and just reparations without cessation of sovereignty.
- **Full text:** ['Te Tiriti o Waitangi', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-te-tiriti-o-waitangi.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_te_tiriti_o_waitangi)); ['2026 Māori Manifesto' (PDF), undated (accessed 18 August 2026)](documents/maori-manifesto-2026-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1785798775/Ma%CC%84ori_Manifesto_Web_Version.pdf?1785798775)); ['Māori Centred', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-maori-centred.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_maori_centred))

<a id="pol55102"></a>
**Uphold Te Tiriti o Waitangi in the new resource management system**  
`POL55102` · topics: te-ao-maori, transport-and-infrastructure, land-and-resources, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, urban-planning-and-resource-management

- **Why:** This party says that shared-decision making between the Government and tangata whenua is important to uphold Te Tiriti o Waitangi, and that this has been taken out of environmental laws.
- **How:** This party would secure the role of Te Tiriti in the resource management system and affirm the role of tangata whenua as shared decision-makers and kaitiaki (guardians). The party will also implement strong evidence-based limits for ecosystem health.
- **Full text:** ['Environmental Protection', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-environmental-protection.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_environmental_protection)); ['Greens commit to undoing attacks on conservation and environment laws', 19 September 2026 (accessed 22 September 2026)](documents/web-greens-commit-to-undoing-attacks-on-conservation-and-environment-laws.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_commit_to_undoing_attacks_on_conservation_and_environment_laws))

<a id="pol55106"></a>
**Create wrap-around services for new parents**  
`POL55106` · topics: te-ao-maori, health, community-and-inclusion, wellbeing-and-services, health-services, women

- **Why:** This party says that that single mothers are vulnerable to hardship and need more support.
- **How:** This party would fund a one-stop-shop service for new parents, providing pā harakeke wrap-around suppport for whānau during pregnancy, after birth, in early childhood, and throughout parenthood.  This is in addition to its policy to extent paid parental leave.
- **Full text:** ['Gender Equity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_gender_equity)); ['Health', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-health.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_health))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Māori Centred**: *By Māori, for Māori.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-maori-centred.md))

1. Devolve power and redistribute resources back to tangata whenua in recognition that Māori are the decision-makers and facilitators of the destiny of their own whānau, hapū and iwi.
2. Invest in outcomes that minimise the disparities and inequities tangata whenua face across the majority of social and economic outcomes.
3. Resource marae through a Marae Resilience Fund for Māori to lead initiatives for their people, wāhi tapu (sacred sites) and whenua in accordance with their own tikanga. Create ongoing funding for provisions for civil emergencies in recognition of being consistent community lifelines.
4. End perpetual leases on whenua Māori and amend the Public Works Act to prevent the acquisition of Māori freehold land, Māori customary land and lands within a reasonable proximity to marae, urupā and wāhi tapu.
5. Expand the learning, speaking and promotion of te reo Māori in both formal and informal settings to encourage the widespread use of the language across the nation.

**Te Tiriti o Waitangi**: *Advancing an Aotearoa that embraces Te Tiriti.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-te-tiriti-o-waitangi.md))

1. Amend the Treaty of Waitangi Act to recognise that Te Tiriti is the authoritative text and that Te Tiriti works in conjunction with He Whakaputanga o te Rangatiratanga o Nu Tireni.
2. Implement genuine Te Tiriti-based decision-making models across government and resource iwi and hapū to participate as partners rather than as advisors.
3. Reform the Treaty Settlements process by amending the settlement policy guide for negotiators to enable fair negotiations and just reparations without ceding sovereignty, removing any clauses that refer to ‘Full and Final settlement’, ending the ‘large natural grouping’ approach to Treaty settlements, and validating hapū rangatiratanga.
4. Increase resourcing for the Waitangi Tribunal, providing them with the power to make binding recommendations for the return of Crown and Private land to iwi, whānau and hapū.
5. Facilitate the return of whenua outside of Treaty Settlements that was wrongfully alienated from tangata whenua, including through exploring a right-of-first-refusal process that enables the return of private land to iwi, hapū and whānau at point of sale.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **Māori Manifesto 2026: 'Kei ō tātou ringa'**: A separate Māori-focused version of the 2026 manifesto (34 pages) organised around taiao, tāngata, tāhua and rangatiratanga. It opens with a 'Kaupapa Māori — by Māori, for Māori' chapter and then restates each manifesto chapter with emphasis on Te Tiriti o Waitangi, tino rangatiratanga and outcomes for whānau, hapū and iwi. Full text: [Green Party Māori Manifesto 2026 — Taiao, Tāngata, Tāhua, Rangatiratanga: Kei ō tātou ringa](documents/maori-manifesto-2026-pdf.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/78863/attachments/original/1785798775/Ma%CC%84ori_Manifesto_Web_Version.pdf?1785798775))
- **Te Waonui a Tāne: an ongoing, protected funding source for marae**: An 11-page policy (announced 9 August 2026) to recognise and resource marae as social and cultural infrastructure. It proposes a Rangatiratanga Trust with a $2.44 billion Crown funding input, including a $2 billion intergenerational fund (Te Tōtara Haemata) and annual restoration funding for marae, governed with regional marae representation. Full text: [Te Waonui a Tāne — Thriving Marae for Thriving Communities](documents/te-waonui-a-tane-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/81707/attachments/original/1786238613/TWT_policy_2026_ONLINE.pdf?1786238613))
- **Standing policy: Te Tiriti o Waitangi Policy**: Standing party policy on honouring Te Tiriti o Waitangi, upholding Māori rights and supporting Māori development and constitutional transformation.
