## Community and inclusion

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54913"></a>
**Ensure the agricultural industry uses an ethical and human approach to animal management**  
`POL54913` · topics: community-and-inclusion, economy, animal-welfare, agriculture

- **Why:** This party says that animals in New Zealand's agricultural industry should enjoy the highest animal welfare standards. Welfare standards need to be properly enforced so that any animal death is managed as humanely as possible.
- **How:** This party would ban the importation of animals and animal products when living conditions in their country of origin have not met Aotearoa New Zealand’s animal welfare standards and would empower resource animal welfare inspectors and prosecutors to fully uphold the Animal Welfare Act. It would also address the requirements of the Global Animal Protection Index to improve animal welfare in New Zealand, and prohibit new, and phase out existing factory farming of animals, including highly intensive outdoor facilities like feedlots. Transport standards that are fit-for-purpose and animal-focused would be adopted.
- **Full text:** ['Agriculture Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-agriculture-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/agriculture_policy)); ['Agriculture Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/agriculture-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/65102/attachments/original/1778624623/Policy-Greens_Agriculture_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54914"></a>
**Ensure New Zealand's legal system upholds an animal's right to a life worth living**  
`POL54914` · topics: community-and-inclusion, animal-welfare

- **Why:** This party says that animals have the right to good nutrition and a safe and happy environment. They are due respect and love, and humans have a duty of care towards animals they interact with. They should not be violated or exploited, and their distress and pain should be minimised.
- **How:** This party would ensure that the Animal Welfare Act 1999 and other related legislation are fit for purpose, evidence-based, and enforced, aligning Codes of Welfare with the Act. It would also establish a Department of Animal Welfare, led by a Minister for Animal Welfare and a Parliamentary Commissioner for Animal Justice, and resource it to investigate and make recommendations on animal welfare issues. Local governments would be adequately resourced to uphold and monitor Animal Welfare bylaws. It would also develop codes of welfare for home kills and support animals.
- **Full text:** ['Animal Welfare Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-animal-welfare-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/animal_welfare_policy)); ['Animal Welfare Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/animal-welfare-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64486/attachments/original/1778624593/Policy-Greens_Animal_Welfare_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Welfare of Animals', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-welfare-of-animals.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_welfare_of_animals))

<a id="pol54915"></a>
**Create national standards for the registered breeding of companion animals**  
`POL54915` · topics: community-and-inclusion, animal-welfare

- **Why:** This party says that humans have a duty of care towards animals they interact with. They should not be violated or exploited, and their distress and pain should be minimised.
- **How:** This party would create national standards for the registered breeding of companion animals and limit the number of litters an individual animal can have over a lifetime. People who breed companion animals for sale and rescue centres must register and hold a current licence. It would also review the appropriateness of breed-specific legislation, and ban breeding of lineages with high susceptibility to genetic welfare issues.
- **Full text:** ['Animal Welfare Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-animal-welfare-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/animal_welfare_policy)); ['Animal Welfare Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/animal-welfare-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64486/attachments/original/1778624593/Policy-Greens_Animal_Welfare_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Welfare of Animals', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-welfare-of-animals.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_welfare_of_animals))

<a id="pol54927"></a>
**Implement improved welfare regulations for companion animals**  
`POL54927` · topics: community-and-inclusion, housing, animal-welfare, renting

- **Why:** This party says that people have a duty of respect for animals' rights. They should not be violated or exploited, and their distress and pain should be minimised.
- **How:** This party would limit the number of companion animals an individual can have at once, with pathways for exemptions, such as for owners who can demonstrate being able to uphold welfare. It would implement codes of welfare for highly social animals, such as guinea pigs, rabbits, birds, rats, goats, horses and pigs, that require them to always be kept with others of their species. The party would also ban prolonged close confinement of animals and amend the Residential Tenancies Act 1986 to ensure that renters are able to own a registered companion or support animal when the animal meets local bylaws.
- **Full text:** ['Animal Welfare Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/animal-welfare-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64486/attachments/original/1778624593/Policy-Greens_Animal_Welfare_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54928"></a>
**Ensure animal welfare regulations upholds Te Tiriti o Waitangi and incorporates Māori cultural values**  
`POL54928` · topics: community-and-inclusion, te-ao-maori, animal-welfare, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says humans have an ethical obligation to make informed decisions on behalf of animals, drawing on the best available knowledge of their needs and behaviours. This includes tikanga and mātauranga Māori.
- **How:** This party would reform animal welfare legislation to recognise Māori concepts and values, in partnership with tāngata whenua, and ensure Māori representation on decision-making bodies for animal welfare. It would establish a Department of Animal Welfare, led by a Minister for Animal Welfare.
- **Full text:** ['Animal Welfare Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/animal-welfare-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64486/attachments/original/1778624593/Policy-Greens_Animal_Welfare_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Animal Welfare Policy', undated (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/web-animal-welfare-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/animal_welfare_policy))

<a id="pol54929"></a>
**Reform animal welfare legislation so that it protects indigenous biodiversity**  
`POL54929` · topics: community-and-inclusion, environment, media-culture-and-recreation, animal-welfare, conservation, sports-and-recreation

- **Why:** This party says that we need to carefully balance the needs of indigenous species and introduced animals. Indigenous species and ecosystems are essential for maintaining ecological balance, so their interests should be prioritised over invasive species.
- **How:** This party would develop local regulations around pet ownership to cater for the indigenous species residing in that locale, such as requiring catios to keep cats safe and protect local biodiversity. This includes funding free spaying and neutering for pets, introducing national standards for desexing dogs and registering, desexing and microchipping cats, and regulating recreational activities like hunting and fishing when they negatively affect indigenous species.
- **Full text:** ['Animal Welfare Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 09 September 2026)](documents/animal-welfare-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64486/attachments/original/1778624593/Policy-Greens_Animal_Welfare_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54947"></a>
**Make a child's voice central in all decisions that matter to them**  
`POL54947` · topics: community-and-inclusion, law-justice-and-government, young-people, family-justice-and-safety

- **Why:** This party says that our children and young people deserve to be loved, nurtured and safe. We have a shared responsibility to make sure tamariki's voices are heard, centred and reflected in all decisions that impact them.
- **How:** This party would create and amend legislation so that the child’s voice is central in all decisions that matter to them. It would do this by using the Child Impact Assessment Tool consistently in all government policy and planning, and fully incorporating the Rights of the Child into legislation.
- **Full text:** ['Children', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-childen.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_childen))

<a id="pol54948"></a>
**Make the Best Start payment for children universal**  
`POL54948` · topics: community-and-inclusion, young-people

- **Why:** This party says that our children and young people deserve to be loved, nurtured and safe. We have a shared responsibility to make sure children are free from poverty as soon as they are born.
- **How:** This party would increase the Best Start payment and extend it to every child under three as a universal child benefit for the early years.
- **Full text:** ['Children', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-childen.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_childen))

<a id="pol54949"></a>
**Improve workforce safety in the child protection system**  
`POL54949` · topics: community-and-inclusion, young-people

- **Why:** This party says that children and young people deserve a skilled, well-supported workforce that is trauma-informed, culturally responsive and properly trained when dealing with their safety, health and education.
- **How:** This party would would fund training and create a workforce development strategy. This would prioritise Māori, Pacific, rainbow, disabled and community provider pathways.
- **Full text:** ['Children', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-childen.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_childen))

<a id="pol54950"></a>
**Improve access for diagnosis services for children with disabilities or learning support needs**  
`POL54950` · topics: community-and-inclusion, young-people, disability

- **Why:** This party says that children deserve to be supported in each of their unique journeys through life. A functioning society should ensure children with disabilities or learning support needs get the help they need to have a great quality of life.
- **How:** This party would improve access to both diagnosis and support services for children with disabilities or learning support needs.
- **Full text:** ['Children', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-childen.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_childen))

<a id="pol54951"></a>
**Amend the Oranga Tamariki Act to explicitly protect Māori children**  
`POL54951` · topics: community-and-inclusion, te-ao-maori, young-people, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, wellbeing-and-services

- **Why:** This party says that young Māori children are disproportionately impacted by New Zealand's current child protection system. This is because it excludes meaningful partnership and underinvests in culturally grounded, whānau-led solutions. The party says that to uphold our commitment to Te Tiriti o Waitangi, we need to address the inequities tamariki Māori face in everyday life and in the state care system.
- **How:** This party would amend the Oranga Tamariki Act to protect tamariki Māori and explicitly embed whānau participation in decision-making to exercise iwi or hapū authority. It would implement the Waitangi Tribunal recommendations regarding Oranga Tamariki.
- **Full text:** ['Children', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-childen.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_childen))

<a id="pol54959"></a>
**Increase funding for community media**  
`POL54959` · topics: community-and-inclusion, media-culture-and-recreation, young-people, broadcasting-and-media, arts-culture-and-heritage

- **Why:** This party says that young people and local communities deserve opportunities to foster their skills and talents in media and radio. This promotes culture and creativity that is vital for creating vibrant cities and communities, alongside encouraging young New Zealanders to grow their careers in the country.
- **How:** This party would fund and resource student, community, regional, Māori and Pacific media and radio.
- **Full text:** ['Culture and Creativity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-culture-and-creativity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_culture_and_creativity))

<a id="pol54996"></a>
**Provide accessible and timely income support for disabled people**  
`POL54996` · topics: community-and-inclusion, incomes-and-employment, disability, tax-and-income-support

- **Why:** This party says that in New Zealand, disabled people are often denied the same rights as others. They can also be excluded from places and activities that non-disabled people take for granted. All disabled people deserve enough support so that they can participate equally in life and in their communities.
- **How:** This party would provide easily accessible and timely income support to meet all disability-related living and medical costs. This income support would be regardless of relationship status and cause of disability.
- **Full text:** ['Disability', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_disability))

<a id="pol55002"></a>
**Ensure all disabled people can access and live in safe housing**  
`POL55002` · topics: community-and-inclusion, housing, disability, social-and-public-housing, housing-quality

- **Why:** This party says that our current laws, policies and social norms mean disabled people are often denied the same rights as others. They are often excluded from places and activities non-disabled people take for granted, such as the basic human right to shelter.
- **How:** This party would address homelessness and work towards ensuring all disabled people can live in safe, accessible, affordable housing in the community of their choice, including by setting robust universal design standards for public and private housing. Institutional group home settings would be phased out.
- **Full text:** ['Disability', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_disability)); ['Disability Policy', 2 March 2026 (accessed 16 September 2026)](documents/unretrievable-disability-policy-full.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/disability_policy_full))

<a id="pol55003"></a>
**Make travel accessible for disabled people**  
`POL55003` · topics: community-and-inclusion, transport-and-infrastructure, incomes-and-employment, disability, public-transport-cycling-and-walking, cars-and-roads, benefits

- **Why:** This party says that our current laws, policies and social norms mean disabled people are often denied the same rights as others. They are often excluded from places and activities non-disabled people take for granted, such as accessing public transport and travelling around their communities.
- **How:** This party would make getting around accessible through continuing to fund the 75% subsidy for the Total Mobility taxi scheme, making it easier to use and increasing the number of mobility vehicles to meet demand.
- **Full text:** ['Disability', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_disability)); ['Disability Policy', 2 March 2026 (accessed 16 September 2026)](documents/unretrievable-disability-policy-full.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/disability_policy_full))

<a id="pol55005"></a>
**Ensure national and local emergency response plans address the needs of disabled people**  
`POL55005` · topics: community-and-inclusion, environment, law-justice-and-government, disability, climate-adaptation, local-government

- **Why:** This party says that disabled people are often more vulnerable than non-disabled people during emergencies. It does not want disabled people to be left behind in times of emergency. Their needs must be considered and supported.
- **How:** This party would resource a coordinated approach to disability in emergency management, including a national disability emergency management strategy. It would prioritise disabled input and leadership locally and nationally within emergency response planning and communication.
- **Full text:** ['Disability', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_disability)); ['Disability Policy', 2 March 2026 (accessed 16 September 2026)](documents/unretrievable-disability-policy-full.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/disability_policy_full))

<a id="pol55006"></a>
**Uphold legal capacity for everyone in New Zealand**  
`POL55006` · topics: community-and-inclusion, health, disability, mental-health, health-services

- **Why:** This party says that everyone, with the support of people of their own choosing when appropriate, should have autonomy and equal recognition before the law. Disabled people, like others should be in charge of decisions about our own lives.
- **How:** This party would uphold legal capacity for everyone, moving from legal capacity assessments to rights-based systems of supported decision-making. This includes disabled people and their families being able to rely on accessible, timely, and affordable healthcare, mental health support and palliative care, without fear of judgement.
- **Full text:** ['Disability', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_disability)); ['Disability Policy', 2 March 2026 (accessed 16 September 2026)](documents/unretrievable-disability-policy-full.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/disability_policy_full))

<a id="pol55009"></a>
**Increase resourcing for rainbow community organisations**  
`POL55009` · topics: community-and-inclusion, health, rainbow-community, health-services

- **Why:** This party says that all New Zealanders need to live with dignity and safety. Rainbow New Zealanders, in particular, need access to public services and healthcare that meet their needs.
- **How:** This party would work with rainbow communities and organisations to develop strategies for wellbeing, especially for access to health care. Māori and Pasifika services specifically would also be included. Rainbow community organisations would receive dedicated resources for their work and be involved in developing and delivering services.
- **Full text:** ['Rainbow Communities', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-rainbow-communities.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_rainbow_communities))

<a id="pol55011"></a>
**Promote the human rights of takatāpui, intersex and rainbow people against discrimination**  
`POL55011` · topics: community-and-inclusion, migration, rainbow-community

- **Why:** This party says that all New Zealanders need to be able to live with dignity and safety. Rainbow New Zealanders in particular need their identities to be recognised and protected, as recommended by the Law Commission’s Ia Tangata report. Protecting human rights means being protected from non-consensual surgeries, and that rainbow migrants have their identities recognised and protected.
- **How:** This party would amend the Human Rights Act to clarify that all forms of sexual orientation, gender identity and variations in sex characteristics, are prohibited grounds of discrimination and adopt the recommendations of the Ia Tangata report. Non-consensual and medically unnecessary surgeries for intersex people would be prohibited. Migrants, asylum seekers and refugees with rainbow identities would be able to access legal services, have their gender and partners legally recognised, and reunite with their families.
- **Full text:** ['Rainbow Communities', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-rainbow-communities.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_rainbow_communities))

<a id="pol55019"></a>
**Restore the monitoring role of the Children's Commissioner** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55019` · topics: community-and-inclusion, law-justice-and-government, young-people, public-sector, key-policies-community-and-inclusion

- **Why:** This party says that that independent commissions with expertise, like the Climate Change Commission, Human Rights Commission and Children's Commission play an important role. Public services ensure that all New Zealanders can thrive, and these commissions are part of how issues are monitored.
- **How:** This party would restore the "monitoring" function of the Children's Commissioner, in addition to the advocacy function. It would also support the importance of independent commissions in the government more broadly.
- **Full text:** ['Public Services', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-public-services.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_public_services))

<a id="pol55023"></a>
**Standardise pay and other support across the public sector**  
`POL55023` · topics: community-and-inclusion, law-justice-and-government, disability, public-sector

- **Why:** This party says that underinvestment has hurt public services and damaged their ability to fulfil their responsibilities to the public, leaving services stretched. Staff members in any part of the public sector should have access to the same pay and career progression to make things equal, improve retention and develop capacity and skills. The public service should also lead the way in providing flexible employment options for disabled people.
- **How:** This party would ensure all public servants can access career progression, pay, community secondment opportunities and training in all sectors. It would provide resources and training to meet disabled employees access needs and counter ableism.
- **Full text:** ['Public Services', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-public-services.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_public_services))

<a id="pol55042"></a>
**Create a state-owned retirement village system and more support for seniors** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55042` · topics: community-and-inclusion, health, te-ao-maori, seniors, public-health, wellbeing-and-services, key-policies-community-and-inclusion

- **Why:** This party says that seniors have cared for us and many continue to do so during retirement. They should feel valued, connected and able to live with dignity. Seniors should have warm, accessible homes, and the community spaces and marae-based centres that support them should they need assistance. The says that, additionally, there should also be more support for the conditions and pay of aged care workers.
- **How:** This party would launch a long‑term seniors strategy that addresses kaumātua wellbeing, elder abuse, equitable incomes, and the creation of a state‑owned, not‑for‑profit retirement village system. It would boost funding for senior community spaces and activities, expand housing options such as papakāinga and address social housing waitlists for kaumātua. It would support warm, accessible, smaller home designs and back fair pay and conditions for aged care workers.
- **Full text:** ['Seniors', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-seniors.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_seniors))

<a id="pol55104"></a>
**Reverse changes to pay equity and fair pay agreements**  
`POL55104` · topics: community-and-inclusion, incomes-and-employment, women, wages-and-working-conditions, jobs-and-employment

- **Why:** This party says that that women deserve to be paid fairly and the same as men for equal work. This party says changes made by the Government to pay equity shifted the goal posts for pay equity claims.
- **How:** This party would undo the current Government's changes to pay equity and resume pay equity claims, pay transparency measures and pay gap reporting.
- **Full text:** ['Gender Equity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_gender_equity))

<a id="pol55105"></a>
**Improve endometriosis treatment**  
`POL55105` · topics: community-and-inclusion, health, women, health-services

- **Why:** This party says that that women face disparities in healthcare, especially for pelvic pain conditions and endometriosis.
- **How:** This party would work towards diagnosing and treating endometriosis faster. It would develop formal clinical guidelines for diagnosing and treating endometriosis, and fund specialist clinics for endometriosis across NZ.
- **Full text:** ['Gender Equity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_gender_equity)); ['Health', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-health.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_health))

<a id="pol55107"></a>
**Support kaupapa Māori and community based solutions to family and sexual violence**  
`POL55107` · topics: community-and-inclusion, te-ao-maori, law-justice-and-government, women, wellbeing-and-services, family-justice-and-safety, pasifika, rainbow-community, disability

- **Why:** This party says that that gender-based violence is increasing, and front-line organisations should be resourced to end family and and sexual violence in NZ.
- **How:** This party would will resource kaupapa Māori and community-led solutions to family and sexual violence. These solutions would be developed by groups that are disproportionately impacted by family and sexual violence, including women, Pasifika, rainbow and disabled communities.
- **Full text:** ['Gender Equity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_gender_equity))

<a id="pol55108"></a>
**Amend the definition of consent to be free and voluntary** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55108` · topics: law-justice-and-government, community-and-inclusion, criminal-justice, women, key-policies-community-and-inclusion

- **Why:** This party says that that gender-based violence is increasing and women and gender minorities should be safe, respected and valued.
- **How:** This party would amend the Crimes Act to amend the definition of consent and affirm that consent must be free and voluntary, and can be withdrawn at any time.
- **Full text:** ['Gender Equity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_gender_equity))

<a id="pol55110"></a>
**Extend paid parental leave to 15 months**  
`POL55110` · topics: community-and-inclusion, incomes-and-employment, women, wages-and-working-conditions

- **Why:** This party says that women and families deserve to be safe and supported. Women deserve to get paid for their work, and compensated for time at home carrying out the essential work of caregiving.
- **How:** This party would increase paid parental leave to 15 months for the primary caregiver, and have additional ring-fenced leave for partners. This party would also increase payments.
- **Full text:** ['Gender Equity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_gender_equity))

<a id="pol55143"></a>
**Extend hate speech laws**  
`POL55143` · topics: community-and-inclusion, free-expression-and-hate-speech, rainbow-community, disability

- **Why:** This party says that that everyone deserves to live free from violence, harm and discrimination.
- **How:** This party would extend hate speech laws to cover religion, gender, disability and rainbow communities. This party would implement this alongside other recommendations of the Royal Commission of Inquiry into the Terrorist Attack on Christchurch Mosques on 15 March 2019.
- **Full text:** ['Justice and Community Safety', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-justice-and-community-safety.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_justice_and_community_safety))

<a id="pol55181"></a>
**Restore pay equity and negotiate a settlement for care workers**  
`POL55181` · topics: community-and-inclusion, incomes-and-employment, health, seniors, tax-and-income-support, health-funding-and-workforce

- **Why:** This party says that the previous pay equity law better protected care and support workers who look after disabled people, older people and some of the most vulnerable people in New Zealand every day. However, this law was changed in 2025, and since then, these workers have been undervalued. This party believes a carer's pay should reflect the skill and responsibility their jobs demand.
- **How:** This party would restore the pay equity law to its pre-previous-government form. The party would also fund the settlement for the approximately 65,000 care and support workers whose claim was stopped after those changes were made to the law by passing legislation to settle the claim. The final settlement would be agreed through bargaining with the workers and their unions. This party estimates that this policy would cost $1.1 billion a year.
- **Full text:** ['Greens commit to pay equity for care workers', 1 October 2026 (accessed 02 October 2026)](documents/web-greens-commit-to-pay-equity-for-care-workers.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_commit_to_pay_equity_for_care_workers))

<a id="pol55254"></a>
**Guarantee every 16-to 24-year-old who has been out of work for six months a pathway into education, training, or a new job**  
`POL55254` · topics: community-and-inclusion, incomes-and-employment, young-people, jobs-and-employment

- **Why:** This party says that youth unemployment is at nearly 15 per cent, roughly three times that of the wider working-age population, which is at an 11-year high. Yet there is so much work to be done in New Zealand. This party says it does not make sense, with nearly 100,000 young people not in employment, training or education. A long period without a job at the start of a working life also lowers a person’s wages for years afterwards.
- **How:** This party would establish the Jobs for Young People pilot programme, based on the successful Jobs for Nature model, to support new jobs in businesses, councils, iwi, non-profits or the public sector. It would also scale up funding of the Mayor's Taskforce for Jobs for all 67 councils, connecting young people with local businesses, community organisations, iwi and hapū. Lastly, it would make sure there is a national workforce strategy plan for young people.
- **Full text:** ['Greens announce jobs guarantee for young people', 11 October 2026 (accessed 12 October 2026)](documents/unretrievable-greens-announce-jobs-guarantee-for-young-people.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_announce_jobs_guarantee_for_young_people)); ['Youth Jobs Guarantee' (PDF), 11 October 2026 (accessed 12 October 2026)](documents/youth-jobs-guarantee-policy-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/96649/attachments/original/1791665775/a_job_for_every_young_person-policy.pdf?1791665775))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Children**: *Thriving tamariki and rangatahi.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-childen.md))

1. Legislate so that the child’s voice is central in all decisions that matter to them by using the Child Impact Assessment Tool consistently and fully incorporating the Rights of the Child into legislation.
2. Increase the Best Start payment and extend it to every child under three as a universal child benefit for the early years.
3. Elevate workforce safety by funding training and a workforce development strategy prioritising Māori, Pacific, rainbow, disabled and community provider pathways.
4. Improve access to both diagnosis and support services for children with disabilities or learning support needs.
5. Amend the Oranga Tamariki Act so that it protects tamariki Māori and explicitly embeds whānau participation in decision-making to exercise iwi or hapū authority.

**Disability**: *An accessible Aotearoa for all.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-disability.md))

1. Introduce a disability rights law based on Te Tiriti with enforceable accessibility standards.
2. Provide easily accessible and timely income support to meet all disability-related living and medical costs, regardless of relationship status and cause of disability.
3. Address homelessness and work towards all disabled people being able to live in safe, accessible, affordable housing in the community of their choice, including by setting robust universal design standards for public and private housing.
4. Make getting around accessible, including by continuing to fund the 75% subsidy for the Total Mobility taxi scheme, making it easier to use and increasing the number of mobility vehicles to meet demand.
5. Draw on the Enabling Good Lives approach to guide how government agencies work with disabled people. Remove barriers to accessing the essentials like health care, transport, inclusive education and disability support for all who need it.
6. Ensure national and local emergency response plans address the needs of disabled people.
7. Uphold legal capacity for everyone, moving from legal capacity assessments to rights-based systems of supported decision-making.

**Gender Equity**: *For a more equal Aotearoa.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md))

1. Close the gender and ethnic pay gaps by undoing the Government’s changes to the pay equity regime and resuming claims, pay transparency measures, and comprehensive pay gap reporting.
2. Increase the Best Start payment and extend it to every child under three as a universal child benefit for the early years.
3. Fund one-stop-shops that provides pā harakeke wrap-around services for whānau throughout hapūtanga, after birth, first stages of life, and navigating parenthood.
4. Work towards diagnosing and treating endometriosis faster, including by developing formal clinical guidelines for diagnosing and treating endometriosis and other pelvic pain, and by funding specialised clinics across Aotearoa.
5. Resource kaupapa Māori solutions and community-led solutions to family and sexual violence, that have have been developed by disproportionately impacted groups, including women, Pasifika, rainbow, and disabled communities.
6. Amend the Crimes Act to criminalise coercive control, amend the definition of consent, and preclude a defence of reluctant consent to affirm that consent must be free and voluntary, and can be withdrawn at any time while ensuring these changes cause no further harm.
7. Increase paid parental leave to 15 months for the primary caregiver, with additional ring-fenced leave for partners, while also increasing payments.

**Pacific Peoples**: *Deepening connections and uplifting communities.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-pacific-peoples.md))

1. Extend visa-free travel to Aotearoa for visitors from the Pacific Islands.
2. Restore and increase the international climate aid funding Aotearoa contributes. Affirm and support calls by Pacific Island nations for stronger climate action in international negotiations.
3. Resource tangata moana leadership on climate action within Aotearoa.
4. Replace the Recognised Seasonal Employer Scheme with an alternative that gives Pacific workers access to fair wages, housing and essential services, and provides for open work rights and residency pathways rather than being tied to a single employer.
5. Support the independence of West Papua and French-occupied Polynesia. Call for an Indigenous-led human rights investigation and a visit from the Special Rapporteur on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples.
6. Support Pacific Island states with their marine protection initiatives, including by increasing funding support for the South Pacific Regional Environment Programme and supporting marine protected areas in the Pacific Ocean under the High Seas Treaty.

**Rainbow Communities**: *Rainbow communities thriving with freedom, equality and respect.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-rainbow-communities.md))

1. Work with the different rainbow communities to develop appropriate health care services and strategies built cultural values and holistic models of care and wellbeing.
2. Increase resourcing for rainbow organisations, including dedicated support for Māori and Pasifika-led organisations, to support the development and delivery of services that reflect the diverse needs, values, and strengths of rainbow communities.
3. Resource clear pathways for timely, free, non-discriminatory access to all forms of gender-affirming health care based on informed consent and self-determination, including reversal of the ban on puberty blockers.
4. Promote the human rights of takatāpui, intersex and rainbow people, amending the Human Rights Act to clarify that all forms of sexual orientation, gender identity and variations in sex characteristics, are prohibited grounds of discrimination, and implement the Law Commission’s Ia Tangata report recommendations.
5. Prohibit all non-consensual and medically unnecessary surgeries or medical interventions on intersex people.
6. Reform laws and policies so rainbow asylum seekers, refugees and migrants can access legal gender recognition, partnership recognition, family reunification, and resettlement support.

**Seniors**: *Happy, healthy and supported.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-seniors.md))

1. Launch a dedicated long-term strategy for the needs of an ageing population which encompasses the holistic wellbeing of kaumātua, elder abuse prevention, ensuring income is equitably accessed and sufficient to live comfortably, and the establishment of a state-owned, not-for-profit retirement village scheme.
2. Increase funding for community spaces and the establishment of facilities, programmes, education and volunteering opportunities that are accessible to seniors.
3. Expanding housing options by building more papakāinga, prioritising kaumātua on social housing waitlists, and ensuring housing designs are suitable for their needs — single-level and warm, with mobility features and room for intergenerational living.
4. Ensure support services are grounded in cultural values, and that home and community care workers are well trained, supported and paid equitably and fairly by including travel time.
5. Resource Māori, Pacific and migrant organisations to provide kaumātua care that is aligned with their values, cultural practices and tikanga.

## Law, justice and government

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54775"></a>
**Support criminalisation of "ecocide"**  
`POL54775` · topics: law-justice-and-government, te-ao-maori, environment, criminal-justice, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, te-reo-maori-and-culture, conservation

- **Why:** This party says that human activities such as resource extraction are leading to pollution, habitat loss and an increasing rate of biodiversity loss. Criminalising ecocide would disincentivise environmentally destructive activities.
- **How:** This party would explore and support criminalising "ecocide" in domestic and international law. This would incorporate a Te Tiriti o Waitangi perspective.
- **Full text:** ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy', undated (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/web-biodiversity-environmental-regeneration-policy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/biodiversity_environmental_regeneration_policy)); ['Biodiversity and Environmental Regeneration Policy FULL' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 28 August 2026)](documents/biodiversity-environmental-regenration-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64514/attachments/original/1778624595/Policy-Greens_Biodiversity_Environmental_Regenration_Policy_2025-2026.pdf)); ['Conservation and Biodiversity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-conservation-and-biodiversity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_conservation_and_biodiversity))

<a id="pol54932"></a>
**Adequately resource national, regional and local emergency systems so communities are prepared for future emergencies.**  
`POL54932` · topics: law-justice-and-government, environment, economy, local-government, climate-adaptation, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says that New Zealand faces certain and increasing threats of many kinds, both natural and man-made. The government should provide the financial, material and training resources that agencies and community organisations require to effectively plan for and respond to emergencies of all kinds.
- **How:** This party would fund critical roles and equipment for all emergency response services, including energy and communications providers, so they can prepare to maintain service resilience in emergencies. Stockpiles and systems providing lifesaving supplies would be organised in local and regional hubs, with regional preparedness plans created. Community organisations would receive training in emergency response, with multilingual, community-led education about emergency preparation and response for the public. It would also fund scientific research on resilience, risk mitigation, and adaptation and funding for locally led long-term recovery work with public engagement on who pays for this.
- **Full text:** ['Emergency Management Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/emergency-management-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64640/attachments/original/1778624600/Policy-Greens_Emergency_Management_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54933"></a>
**Take a preventative approach regarding emergency management responses**  
`POL54933` · topics: law-justice-and-government, environment, local-government, climate-adaptation

- **Why:** This party says that that all people should be empowered to pre-emptively shape the emergency management systems that affect them through community engagement enabled by good evidence, co-design, and adequate resourcing.
- **How:** This party would prioritise collaborative plans and strategies for targeted support towards communities at risk, including isolated people or communities, people at risk, and marginalised groups. It would ensure these communities were supported through developing and resourcing individualised preparedness plans. This would include providing tailored emergency preparedness and response information in various formats and languages to communities and households.
- **Full text:** ['Emergency Management Policy' (PDF), 9 March 2026 (accessed 10 September 2026)](documents/emergency-management-policy-2025-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64640/attachments/original/1778624600/Policy-Greens_Emergency_Management_Policy_2025-2026.pdf))

<a id="pol54980"></a>
**Resource a national conversation for constitutional change**  
`POL54980` · topics: law-justice-and-government, te-ao-maori, parliament-and-democracy, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that that democracy in New Zealand should uphold Te Tiriti o Waitangi and the vision of Matike Mai Aotearoa, creating a decision-making process that reflects genuine partnership.
- **How:** This party would resource a national conversation for constitutional change that upholds He Whakaputanga (the Declaration of Independence of the United Tribes of New Zealand) and Te Tiriti o Waitangi as our founding constitutional documents, utilising Matike Mai Aotearoa to facilitate community discussions.
- **Full text:** ['Democracy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-democracy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_democracy)); 'Mana Motuhake', undated (accessed 18 August 2026) (<https://www.maoriparty.org.nz/mana_motuhake>), cited by policy.nz but this is another party's web page, not a Green Party document; not archived here (maoriparty.org.nz is Cloudflare-blocked; the labour.org.nz page's article body is loaded by script and was not checked)
- **Note:** policy.nz also cites a Te Pāti Māori web page (maoriparty.org.nz/mana_motuhake) for this Green policy; that page is not a Green source.

<a id="pol54981"></a>
**Expand voting rights in New Zealand** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54981` · topics: law-justice-and-government, parliament-and-democracy, key-policies-law-justice-and-government

- **Why:** This party says that expanding voting rights creates a more representative democracy where everyone's voices are respected and heard.
- **How:** This party would uphold the right to vote by extending the voting age to 16, extending the right to vote to all people in custody and restoring enrolment up to election day.
- **Full text:** ['Democracy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-democracy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_democracy))

<a id="pol54982"></a>
**Entrench the Māori electoral seats** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54982` · topics: law-justice-and-government, te-ao-maori, parliament-and-democracy, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, key-policies-law-justice-and-government, key-policies-te-ao-maori

- **Why:** This party says that that democracy in New Zealand needs to represent our entire population. It should appreciate Te Tiriti o Waitangi as a decision-making process that reflects a genuine partnership.
- **How:** This party would entrench the Māori electoral seats to guarantee Māori representation in Parliament and extend the time in which Māori can switch rolls.
- **Full text:** ['Democracy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-democracy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_democracy))

<a id="pol54983"></a>
**Reform electoral funding policies**  
`POL54983` · topics: law-justice-and-government, parliament-and-democracy

- **Why:** This party says that democracy should work for everyone, not just those who are wealthy. The richest few can currently spend millions of dollars backing different political parties and ministers.
- **How:** This party would limit donations to registered voters, increase public financing of campaigns and cap a donor’s donations at a level that does not risk donor influence over policy.
- **Full text:** ['Democracy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-democracy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_democracy))

<a id="pol54984"></a>
**Strengthen political lobbying rules** ⭐ key policy  
`POL54984` · topics: law-justice-and-government, parliament-and-democracy, key-policies-law-justice-and-government

- **Why:** This party says that the wealthiest few in New Zealand have the resources to influence government policies and decisions through political lobbying. Yet this excludes the rest of the country from advocating for the issues that matter most to them.
- **How:** This party would strengthen the rules and transparency over political lobbying, through creating a lobbyist register and a code of conduct.
- **Full text:** ['Democracy', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-democracy.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_democracy))

<a id="pol55020"></a>
**Reform the public finance system to focus on resilience, productivity and wellbeing**  
`POL55020` · topics: law-justice-and-government, economy, public-sector, government-revenue-and-finance

- **Why:** This party says that public services need to be owned by the public, and the government's fiscal management needs to be future focused, not just based on short cycles. Public services should be funded to do their job properly and remain in public ownership, and the government's fiscal management should promote wellbeing, health, resilience and productivity.
- **How:** This party would reform the public finance system to create an approach that supports resilience, wellbeing, and productivity. It would fund public services adequately and ensure that public services and state owned enterprises continue to be owned by the government.
- **Full text:** ['Public Services', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-public-services.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_public_services))

<a id="pol55025"></a>
**Extend the time New Zealanders can live overseas and retain voting eligibility**  
`POL55025` · topics: law-justice-and-government, migration, parliament-and-democracy

- **Why:** This party says that increasing numbers of New Zealanders are moving or living overseas, and the government represents these people too. New Zealanders overseas are still invested in the future of the country and should be able to vote. If they return to New Zealand and contribute here again, they should be able to receive superannuation.
- **How:** This party would extend the time New Zealanders can live overseas and retain voting eligibility. Currently, overseas-based New Zealanders must have lived in NZ continuously for 12 months at some point in their life and been in NZ at some point in the last three years.
- **Full text:** ['Overseas New Zealanders', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-overseas-new-zealanders.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_overseas_new_zealanders))

<a id="pol55053"></a>
**Transfer responsibility for local elections to the Electoral Commission**  
`POL55053` · topics: law-justice-and-government, local-government, parliament-and-democracy

- **Why:** This party says that democratic engagement in local government is low, especially at turnout for local elections. Local government should have a mandate from communities and local elections should be consistent across the country, rather than being run by different private companies.
- **How:** This party would transfer responsibility of local elections to the Electoral Commission. Sixteen year olds would be allowed to vote in local elections and all councils would use a Single Transferable Vote (STV) system. To enhance democracy and community input, councils could use tools like citizens assemblies to make decisions, especially regarding reorganisation of council bodies.
- **Full text:** ['Local Government', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-local-government.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_local_government))

<a id="pol55055"></a>
**Ensure that fulfilling Te Tiriti and wellbeing goals is a core responsibility of local government**  
`POL55055` · topics: law-justice-and-government, te-ao-maori, local-government, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says that tino rangatiratanga is often sidelined in council decision making. Local government shapes a lot of people's everyday life, and community wellbeing should be a priority.
- **How:** This party would reinforce the role of local government to give effect to Te Tiriti o Waitangi, and formally establish local councils as Te Tiriti partners. Councils would be allowed to reinstate Māori wards. Councils should also consider multiple sphere through the "four wellbeings" (social, economic, environmental and cultural) and take responsibility for more areas from central government, with appropriate resourcing.
- **Full text:** ['Local Government', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-local-government.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_local_government))

<a id="pol55056"></a>
**Reject the proposed rates cap and allow councils to use more funding tools**  
`POL55056` · topics: law-justice-and-government, economy, transport-and-infrastructure, local-government, government-revenue-and-finance, tourism, infrastructure-funding-and-planning, regional-development

- **Why:** This party says that councils have been loaded with new responsibilities like emergency management and tourist influxes, but not given enough money to carry out these responsibilities. To carry out their role in communities, councils should not have limits on rates set by central government. Instead they need appropriate resourcing from a range of tools.
- **How:** This party would reject the proposed rates cap where councils could only increase rates between 2 and 4 percent annually. Councils could set rates as they chose. Councils would also be allowed to use bed levies, congestion charging and charge GST on rates and other growth.
- **Full text:** ['Local Government', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-local-government.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_local_government))

<a id="pol55096"></a>
**Restore the Ministry for the Environment**  
`POL55096` · topics: law-justice-and-government, te-ao-maori, public-sector, land-and-resources

- **Why:** This party says that that restoration and protection of the environment is important for the future and that a strong ministry is important for this.
- **How:** This party would restore the Ministry for the Environment, and increase resources for the Environmental Protection Authority to identify risks to the environment and to support compliance, monitoring and enforcement. It would also re-establish resources for the Community Environment Fund.
- **Full text:** ['Environmental Protection', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-environmental-protection.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_environmental_protection))

<a id="pol55099"></a>
**Regulate lobbying and party donations**  
`POL55099` · topics: law-justice-and-government, parliament-and-democracy

- **Why:** This party says that democracy should work for everyone, not just the wealthy. Capping donations and increasing the transparency around lobbying mean big money and professional lobbying can have less influence over politics.
- **How:** This party would bring in a package of reforms, including a public register for lobbying so meetings with politicians are on the record. Former ministers would have to wait two years before they can be employed by a lobbying group. A new bill would cap party donations at $30,000 per individuals, and companies will not be able to donate. Donors above $1000 would have to be named.
- **Full text:** ['Greens announce plans to get big money and influence out of politics', 22 September 2026 (accessed 22 September 2026)](documents/web-greens-announce-plan-to-get-big-money-and-influence-out-of-politics.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/greens_announce_plan_to_get_big_money_and_influence_out_of_politics))

<a id="pol55109"></a>
**Criminalise coercive control**  
`POL55109` · topics: law-justice-and-government, community-and-inclusion, family-justice-and-safety, criminal-justice, women

- **Why:** This party says that that gender-based violence is increasing and women and gender minorities should be safe, respected and valued.
- **How:** This party would amend the Crimes Act to criminalise coercive control.
- **Full text:** ['Gender Equity', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-gender-equity.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_gender_equity))

<a id="pol55138"></a>
**Prioritise rehabilitation for convicted offenders**  
`POL55138` · topics: law-justice-and-government, criminal-justice, family-justice-and-safety

- **Why:** This party says that rehabilitation is important to keep communities safe. It says current approaches to justice neglect rehabilitation, which causes more harm and hardship for communities.
- **How:** This party would resource evidence-based rehabilitation for anyone convicted of an offence. Tikanga based and restorative justice solutions, like Te Pae Oranga, would be available to anyone to access. This party would also improve community re-integration support for offenders leaving the criminal justice system, and would increase the Steps to Freedom grant to cover at least 30 years of inflation.
- **Full text:** ['Justice and Community Safety', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-justice-and-community-safety.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_justice_and_community_safety))

<a id="pol55139"></a>
**Increase accountability in policing**  
`POL55139` · topics: law-justice-and-government, policing

- **Why:** This party says that the justice system should be safe, equitable and fair. This party aims to meet the basic needs of communities in order to prevent crime happening in the first place.
- **How:** This party would require body cameras for frontline police and strengthen the powers of the Independent Police Conduct Authority. The party would also deliver de-escalation and trauma training for police, and increase resourcing for Māori Wardens and Pacific Wardens.
- **Full text:** ['Justice and Community Safety', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-justice-and-community-safety.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_justice_and_community_safety))

<a id="pol55140"></a>
**End military-style academies for young offenders**  
`POL55140` · topics: law-justice-and-government, community-and-inclusion, criminal-justice, young-people

- **Why:** This party says that says the justice system should be fair and equitable. This party says people should not be punished for being poor, and communities are safer when young people have access to food, healthcare, education and have a safe home environment.
- **How:** This party would end military-style academies for young people and raise the age of criminal responsibility. This party would also ban young people being detained alongside adults. This party would expand access to specialist youth courts, including the Rangatahi and Pasifika Courts, Alcohol and Other Drug Treatment Court, and New Beginnings Court, so young people across NZ could access them.

<a id="pol55141"></a>
**Improve accessibility to justice and legal assistance**  
`POL55141` · topics: law-justice-and-government, criminal-justice

- **Why:** This party says that the justice system should be fair and equitable, and communities should be supported to prevent crime happening in the first place.
- **How:** This party would expand the district court initiative, Te Ao Marama, to all courts in New Zealand. This party would also extend legal aid and duty lawyers, better resource community law centres and make applications for protection and parenting orders free.
- **Full text:** ['Justice and Community Safety', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-justice-and-community-safety.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_justice_and_community_safety))

<a id="pol55142"></a>
**Reduce restrictions on the decisions judges can make**  
`POL55142` · topics: law-justice-and-government, criminal-justice

- **Why:** This party says that that laws restricting the decisions a judge can make about sentences have caused more harm and hardship. This party says bias and discrimination in the justice system should be addressed.
- **How:** This party would help enable judicial discretion in sentencing. This includes ending mandatory minimum sentences, life sentences, preventive detention, limits on sentence discounts, three strikes and limits on when home detention can be imposed instead of imprisonment.
- **Full text:** ['Justice and Community Safety', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-justice-and-community-safety.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_justice_and_community_safety))

- **Why:** This party says that that everyone deserves to live free from violence, harm and discrimination.
- **How:** This party would implement all recommendations from the royal commission of inquiry, including passing hate speech laws, and continuing to oppose possession of military-style semi-automatic firearms.

<a id="pol55145"></a>
**Implement recommendations of Royal Commission of Inquiry into Abuse in Care**  
`POL55145` · topics: law-justice-and-government, family-justice-and-safety

- **Why:** This party says that that the Royal Commission of Inquiry into abuse in care was a harrowing account of how society and the Government failed, and must change. The party says survivors have called for all 138 recommendations to be implemented.
- **How:** This party would implement all recommendations of the Royal Commission of Inquiry into Abuse in Care, in consultation with people with lived experience of abuse in care.

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Democracy**: *Politics for people, not big money.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-democracy.md))

1. Resource a national conversation for constitutional transformation that upholds He Whakaputanga and Te Tiriti o Waitangi as our founding constitutional documents, utilising Matike Mai Aotearoa to facilitate community discussions.
2. Uphold the right to vote by extending the voting age to 16, extending the right to vote to all people in custody and restoring enrolment up to election day.
3. Entrench the Māori electoral seats to guarantee Māori representation in Parliament and extend the time in which Māori can switch rolls.
4. Reform electoral funding by limiting donations to registered voters, increasing public financing of campaigns and capping a donor’s donations at a level that does not risk donor influence over policy.
5. Strengthen the rules and transparency over political lobbying, including through a lobbyist register and a code of conduct.

**Justice and Community Safety**: *Keeping communities safe through prevention and rehabilitation.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-justice-and-community-safety.md))

1. Resource evidence-based rehabilitation for anyone convicted of an offence, and ensure tikanga-based and restorative justice solutions (such as Te Pae Oranga) are available to anyone who wishes to access these.
2. Expand Te Ao Mārama to all courts in Aotearoa, and ensure specialist youth courts including the Rangatahi and Pasifika Courts, Alcohol and Other Drug Treatment Court, and New Beginnings Court are available to all who wish to access them.
3. End military-style academies, raise the age of criminal responsibility and ban the detention of young people in the company of adults in all circumstances.
4. Require body cameras for frontline police, strengthen powers for the Independent Police Conduct Authority, deliver de-escalation and trauma training for police, and increase resourcing for Māori Wardens and Pacific Wardens.
5. Improve community reintegration support for people leaving the criminal justice system, including housing, and increase the Steps to Freedom Grant to cover at least 30 years of inflation.
6. Extend legal aid and duty lawyers, better resource community law centres and make applications for protection and parenting orders free.
7. Enable judicial discretion in sentencing, including by abolishing mandatory minimum sentences, life sentences, preventive detention, limits on sentence discounts, three strikes and limits on when home detention can be imposed instead of imprisonment.
8. Implement the recommendations of the Royal Commission of Inquiry into the Terrorist Attack on Christchurch Mosques on 15 March 2019. Extend hate speech laws to cover religion, gender, disability and rainbow communities. Continue to oppose possession of military-style semi-automatic firearms.
9. Implement all recommendations of the Royal Commission of Inquiry into Abuse in Care, in consultation with people with lived experience.

**Local Government**: *Empowering local democracy.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-local-government.md))

1. Strengthen local electoral democracy by transferring administration of local government elections to the Electoral Commission, enabling 16-year-olds to vote and requiring the use of a single transferable voting system.
2. Reinforce councils’ responsibility to give effect to Te Tiriti o Waitangi by making the role of local government as a Te Tiriti partner explicit, and reinstate their ability to keep Māori wards for their districts.
3. Reject the proposed rates cap and enable a range of new funding and financing tools, such as a bed levy, congestion charging and GST from rates and growth.
4. Ensure that the ‘four wellbeings’ (social, economic, environmental and cultural) are core to the purpose of local government and empower councils by devolving further responsibilities from central government, with appropriate resourcing.
5. Enable greater use of community-based decision-making processes — such as citizens’ assemblies — including greater community input in the process for local government reorganisation.

**Public Services**: *Strong essential services accessible to everyone.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-public-services.md))

1. Uphold Te Tiriti throughout the work of the public service, and fund Māori organisations, iwi, hapū and marae to run community and environmental initiatives that meet the needs and aspirations unique to their rohe (region).
2. Fund public services adequately to ensure that they are able to work in service of the public good, and that public services (and state-owned enterprises) remain in public hands.
3. Reform the public finance system to ensure that the fiscal management approach supports New Zealand’s long-term resilience, the productive capacity of our economy, and an ensures the health and well-being of everyone.
4. Affirm the importance of advocacy and monitoring done by independent commissions with expertise in an area, starting with restoring the monitoring role of the Children’s Commissioner alongside its advocacy function.
5. Introduce standardised career progression, pay, community secondment opportunities and training across the public sector to improve staff retention and skills development, and ensure equal pay for equivalent roles.
6. Provide secure, flexible and full funding arrangements for the community sector, recognising the value of the work, and ensuring independence within a supportive legislative framework.
7. Improve training in Te Tiriti and te reo Māori for all public servants, resulting in consistency across all agencies.
8. Ensure that the public service is a leader in disability-responsive employment, including by providing resources and training to meet access needs and counter ableism.

**Violence Prevention**: *Keeping whānau and communities safe.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-violence-prevention.md))

1. Fund the implementation of the action plan for Te Aorerekura: National Strategy to Eliminate Family Violence and Sexual Violence, centred on Te Tiriti.
2. Resource kaupapa Māori solutions and community-led solutions to family and sexual violence, that have have been developed by disproportionately impacted groups, including women, Pasifika, rainbow, and disabled communities.
3. Prioritise violence responses and prevention by improving workforce and broader public understanding of the drivers and signs of violence, and how to respond in ways that enhance safety and healing.
4. Amend the Crimes Act to criminalise coercive control, amend the definition of consent, and preclude a defence of reluctant consent to affirm that consent must be free and voluntary, and can be withdrawn at any time while ensuring these changes cause no further harm.
5. Restore relationship and sexuality teaching guidelines — including education on healthy relationships, diverse identities and sexualities, and affirmative consent — in all schools.
6. Increase funding for counselling and mental health support for victims, whānau, witnesses, defendants and legal professionals engaging in the justice system.

## Migration

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol55028"></a>
**Pursue reciprocal healthcare, welfare and visa-waiver arrangements with other countries** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55028` · topics: migration, health, incomes-and-employment, defence-and-foreign-affairs, health-services, benefits, international-relations, key-policies-migration

- **Why:** This party says that overseas New Zealanders deserve to remain connected to NZ, and have a interest in the future of the country. Nearly 15% of New Zealanders live in other countries. The government should take a compassionate approach to New Zealanders who have lived or are living overseas. These people, and visitors from other countries to New Zealand, should be able to access public services like healthcare wherever they are.
- **How:** This party would seek further reciprocal arrangements with other countries where New Zealanders might live. These would include access to public healthcare, welfare support or benefits and visa waivers. In return, the citizens of those countries would be offered the same services in New Zealand. Existing arrangements, as already exist in some form with the UK and Australia, could be widened to include temporary visitors as well as people working.
- **Full text:** ['Overseas New Zealanders', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-overseas-new-zealanders.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_overseas_new_zealanders))

<a id="pol55123"></a>
**Support migrant workers to residency**  
`POL55123` · topics: migration, incomes-and-employment, wages-and-working-conditions

- **Why:** This party says that that people coming to NZ for work should have fair and achievable pathways to residence. The party says that migrant workers are not disposable, yet they face exploitation, are denied basic rights and are unfairly targeted by immigration compliance officers.
- **How:** This party would create new residency pathways for migrants who have settled in NZ long-term, including those whose visas have expired. This party would also allow migrant work visa holders to switch employers and would end the 12-month stand-down period requiring workers to leave the country between visas.
- **Full text:** ['Immigration and Refugees', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-immigration-and-refugees.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_immigration_and_refugees))

<a id="pol55125"></a>
**Abolish acceptable standards of health requirements for migrants and refugees**  
`POL55125` · topics: migration, community-and-inclusion, disability

- **Why:** This party says that says that migrants and refugees with health conditions or disabilities are discriminated against by the acceptable standards of health requirements.
- **How:** This party would abolish the Acceptable Standards of Health requirements for visa applicants.
- **Full text:** ['Immigration and Refugees', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-immigration-and-refugees.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_immigration_and_refugees))

<a id="pol55126"></a>
**Support families of migrants and refugees to get visas**  
`POL55126` · topics: migration

- **Why:** This party says that says that nobody should be unfairly separated from their families or communities. This party says people who flee war or persecution to seek asylum in New Zealand should be supported and treated with dignity.
- **How:** This party would clear the backlog for refugee family reunification, including resourcing Immigration New Zealand to clear the backlog of tier 1 and 2 family reunification categories and process asylum claims quickly. This party would restore birthright citizenship, expand work rights for partners, remove income barriers to supporting a parent visa, and stop detention and electronic monitoring of asylum seekers.
- **Full text:** ['Immigration and Refugees', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-immigration-and-refugees.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_immigration_and_refugees))

<a id="pol55127"></a>
**Increase the refugee quota** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55127` · topics: migration, key-policies-migration

- **Why:** This party says that that people who fear for their life in their country, should be supported to seek a better life in NZ. They say people escaping war or persecution should be supported to rebuild their lives as part of our communities.
- **How:** This party would progressively increase the refugee quota to 5,000 by 2029. This party would also increase funding for refugee resettlement programmes and create a new humanitarian visa for family of New Zealanders in conflict zones.
- **Full text:** ['Immigration and Refugees', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-immigration-and-refugees.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_immigration_and_refugees))

<a id="pol55128"></a>
**Create an immigration system based on Te Tiriti o Waitangi**  
`POL55128` · topics: migration, te-ao-maori, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance

- **Why:** This party says migrants and refugees should be supported to rebuild their lives in NZ.
- **How:** This party would implement Māori aspirations for an immigration system based on Te Tiriti o Waitangi. It would develop resources and decision-making with iwi and hapū to strengthen traditional whanaungatanga connections. This party would also provide Te Tiriti education to new migrants and refugees.
- **Full text:** ['Immigration and Refugees', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-immigration-and-refugees.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_immigration_and_refugees))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Immigration and Community Safety**: *Welcoming, safe and fair pathways to make Aotearoa home.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-immigration-and-refugees.md))

1. Create residence pathways for migrants who have settled long-term, including for people living in Aotearoa whose visas have expired.
2. Abolish the Acceptable Standards of Health requirements, so that migrants and refugees are not discriminated against based on their health conditions or disabilities.
3. Allow migrant work visa holders to switch employers and remove the 12-month stand-down period that requires workers to leave Aotearoa between visas.
4. Unite families by clearing the backlog for refugee family reunification, expanding work rights for partners, restoring birthright citizenship and removing income barriers to sponsoring a parent visa.
5. Implement Māori aspirations for an immigration system based on Te Tiriti, including devolving resources and decision-making to hapū and iwi to strengthen traditional whanaungatanga connections. Provide Te Tiriti education to new migrants and refugees.
6. Progressively increase the refugee quota to 5,000 by 2029, increase funding for refugee resettlement programmes and create a humanitarian visa for family of New Zealanders in conflict zones.
7. Resource Immigration New Zealand to clear tier 1 and tier 2 family reunification categories and process asylum claims quickly, and stop the unjust detention and electronic monitoring of asylum seekers.

**Overseas New Zealanders**: *Cultivating Kiwi connections around the world.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-overseas-new-zealanders.md))

1. Enable a tikanga-based pathway for people who whakapapa Māori to establish Aotearoa citizenship, regardless of country of residence or birth.
2. Reduce and re-balance student loan repayments and interest rates for overseas student loan borrowers, extend the repayment holiday for overseas borrowers, and abolish border arrests for student loan repayment violations.
3. Extend the time that overseas New Zealanders can spend outside Aotearoa without losing voting eligibility.
4. Restore the 10-year period for time spent in Aotearoa New Zealand to be eligible for superannuation.
5. Pursue reciprocal health care, welfare support and visa waiver arrangements with other countries, and widen existing agreements to include temporary visitors.

## Media, culture and recreation

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol54953"></a>
**Require all publicly funded projects to pay at least the living wage to artists**  
`POL54953` · topics: media-culture-and-recreation, arts-culture-and-heritage

- **Why:** This party says that artists and creatives deserve to be valued, respected and recognised for their work. Yet currently, too many are without the certainty of a stable income, and many lack access to the training and education needed to foster their skills and talents. Creatives should be supported in their work.
- **How:** This party would require that all publicly funded projects pay at least the living wage to artists and expand paid artist residencies so creatives can upskill and grow their practice while building a meaningful livelihood.

Creatives should be supported in their practice and provided opportunities to participate in events where they can grow and showcase their work.

Creatives should be supported in their practice and provided opportunities to participate in events where they can grow and showcase their work.

<a id="pol54956"></a>

`POL54956` · topics: media-culture-and-recreation, te-ao-maori, arts-culture-and-heritage, te-reo-maori-and-culture, te-tiriti-o-waitangi-and-governance, key-policies-media-culture-and-recreation
