### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Culture and Creativity**: *An Aotearoa that values artists.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-culture-and-creativity.md))

1. Require that all publicly funded projects pay at least the living wage to artists, and expand paid artist residencies.
2. Provide stable funding for creative organisations and events such as Te Urungi, including long-term funding for significant creative initiatives such as Te Matatini and Polyfest.
3. Provide sustainable long-term arts funding that is not reliant on gambling revenue, alongside alcohol-free venues for performers.
4. Support and invest in repatriating taonga currently overseas back to iwi, hapū and whānau Māori, as well as keeping taonga in Aotearoa.
5. Establish infrastructure across ministries to strengthen our creative and cultural economy and maximise opportunities for local artists.
6. Increase funding for quality, non-commercial public media that nurtures and promotes our talent and creativity as a nation, including sustainable funding for Radio New Zealand and Whakaata Māori.
7. Increase resourcing for student, community, regional, Māori and Pacific media and radio.

### Policy documents and 2026 announcements

- **Standing policy: Arts, Culture and Heritage Policy**: Standing party policy to resource the arts and heritage sector, protect traditional and contemporary Māori art, and expand arts education and platforms in schools and communities. Full text: [Arts, Culture and Heritage Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/arts-culture-heritage-policy-2014-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64500/attachments/original/1781650645/Policy-Greens_Arts_Culture_Heritage_Policy_2014-2026.pdf?1781650645))
- **Standing policy: Media Policy**: Standing party media policy: support independent and community media, publicly owned broadcasting and funding for local content, prioritising under-served audiences including Māori and children. Full text: [Media Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/media-policy-2014-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64864/attachments/original/1778624610/Policy-Greens_Media_Policy_2014-2026.pdf))
- **Standing policy: Recreation and Sport Policy**: Standing party policy on equitable, publicly funded access to sport and recreation, and regulation of harmful activities including gambling. Full text: [Recreation and Sport Policy (Green Party policy document)](documents/recreation-sport-policy-2016-2026.md) ([original](https://assets.nationbuilder.com/beachheroes/pages/64906/attachments/original/1778624612/Policy-Greens_Recreation_Sport_Policy_2016-2026.pdf))

## Defence and foreign affairs

### Policies (policy.nz)

<a id="pol55034"></a>
**Increase climate aid funding to the Pacific** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55034` · topics: environment, defence-and-foreign-affairs, economy, community-and-inclusion, emissions-reduction, climate-adaptation, international-relations, aid-development-and-institutions, fisheries, key-policies-defence-and-foreign-affairs, pasifika

- **Why:** This party says that the current government has cut climate funding to the Pacific, threatening prosperity and damaging relationships with these neighbouring countries. With the risk of climate change, Pacific countries should be supported to adapt and reduce emissions. Projects should be lead by the Pacific, and the marine environment in this crucial part of the world should be taken care of too.
- **How:** This party would restore climate funding to the Pacific to previous levels, and increase funding where needed. Pasifika people would also be resourced to lead climate action in NZ. This party would also support Pacific countries with ocean protection, enforcing protected areas under the High Seas Treaty and increasing funding for the South Pacific Regional Environment Programme.
- **Full text:** ['Pacific Peoples', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-pacific-peoples.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_pacific_peoples))

<a id="pol55036"></a>
**Increase visa-free travel and work visa set-ups for Pasifika visitors and workers in NZ** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55036` · topics: migration, community-and-inclusion, economy, defence-and-foreign-affairs, pasifika, agriculture, international-relations, key-policies-defence-and-foreign-affairs

- **Why:** This party says that Pacific peoples are an important part of building NZ's sustainable and flourishing future. Pacific communities are some of the youngest and fastest-growing in NZ. However, it can be hard for their family from Pacific Island countries to visit NZ, and workers under the RSE (Recognised Seasonal Employer) scheme can be mistreated.
- **How:** This party would extend visa-free travel to NZ for citizens of Pacific countries. It would also replace the current RSE scheme with an alternative that guarantees housing and fair wages, and create an open work system where it is easier for these workers to change employers if they are not being treated well. Workers under the new scheme would have a clearer path to NZ residency.
- **Full text:** ['Pacific Peoples', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-pacific-peoples.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_pacific_peoples))

<a id="pol55113"></a>
**Recognise Palestine as a state and sanction Israel**  
`POL55113` · topics: defence-and-foreign-affairs, international-relations

- **Why:** This party says that that NZ should be an independent voice against global atrocities, authoritarians, and imperialist ambitions. This party says most United Nations member states have already recognised Palestinian statehood.
- **How:** This party would recognise Palestine as a state and pass a bill drafted by this party to sanction Israel.
- **Full text:** ['Global Affairs and Trade', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-global-affairs-and-trade.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_global_affairs_and_trade)); [' Govt’s empty words do nothing to stop genocide in Gaza ', 11 August 2025 (accessed 23 September 2026)](documents/web-govt-s-empty-words-do-nothing-to-stop-genocide-in-gaza.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/govt_s_empty_words_do_nothing_to_stop_genocide_in_gaza))

<a id="pol55114"></a>
**Rewrite foreign policy to oppose imperialism**  
`POL55114` · topics: defence-and-foreign-affairs, international-relations, defence-and-veterans

- **Why:** This party says that war and authoritarianism is rising globally, and NZ should be an independent voice for peace, freedom, democracy, and should speak out against nuclear weapons and American militarism. This party says foreign policy should challenge imperialism that creates chaos and hardship, and should always support countries facing conflicts and human rights abuses.
- **How:** This party would withdraw NZ from the Five Eyes security alliance and decline to participate in AUKUS, as part of efforts to step away from military and security frameworks with the United States. This party would rewrite NZ's foreign policy to stand against human rights abuses and focus on upholding peace and justice, and would have the Defence Force focus on climate change, humanitarian responses and environmental monitoring.
- **Full text:** ['Global Affairs and Trade', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-global-affairs-and-trade.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_global_affairs_and_trade))

<a id="pol55115"></a>
**Support sovereignty and independence in the Asia-Pacific** ⭐ key policy  
`POL55115` · topics: defence-and-foreign-affairs, international-relations, key-policies-defence-and-foreign-affairs

- **Why:** This party says that that NZ should work with Pacific neighbours to create a greener, safer, and more just world. This party says NZ should be a world leader on issues of climate action, protection of nature and indigenous and human rights, and should work to uphold the international rule of law and collective obligations towards issues like climate change.
- **How:** This party would support Pacific sovereignty, independence, and oppose militarisation in the Pacific. This party would also support decolonisation and democracy movements globally, including those of indigenous peoples in Asia-Pacific region and work to urgently progress climate change initiatives through international institutions. It would support calls for West Papua to be independent of Indonesia, and for French-ruled Polynesian islands to be independent and call for an indigenous-led human rights investigation and a visit from the UN's Special Rapporteur on the Rights of Indigenous People.
- **Full text:** ['Global Affairs and Trade', undated (accessed 13 August 2026)](documents/web-manifesto-2026-global-affairs-and-trade.md) ([original](https://www.greens.org.nz/manifesto_2026_global_affairs_and_trade))

### Manifesto 2026 commitments (verbatim)

**Global Affairs and Trade**: *A safer, greener, fairer future for people and planet.* ([full chapter](documents/web-manifesto-2026-global-affairs-and-trade.md))

1. Restore an independent foreign policy that stands against human rights abuses wherever they occur and upholds peace and justice.
2. Focus defence policy on climate change responses, humanitarian responses and environmental monitoring.
3. Decouple military and security frameworks from the United States, including declining to participate in AUKUS and withdrawing from Five Eyes.
4. Support Pacific sovereignty and independence and oppose the militarisation of the Pacific.
5. Recognise Palestine as a state and sanction Israel.
6. Stand with decolonisation and democracy movements globally, especially those of Indigenous peoples in our Asia–Pacific region.
7. Implement the Trade For All agenda, including democratic scrutiny of trade agreements, and enforceable recognition of our Te Tiriti o Waitangi obligations, workers’ rights, human rights and climate commitments.
8. Urgently progress climate change initiatives through international institutions, which include reducing patent protections on green technologies and negotiating an international approach to climate border adjustments.
